# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1995

## Introduction by Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

Vyāsa is the great sage who incarnated in this world to do the Lord’s own work by editing and expanding the revealed Vedic scriptures so that people of this fallen age could understand and follow them. *Pūjā* is the process of honoring an honorable person who represents Lord Kṛṣṇa, the supreme object of reverence. Thus *vyāsa-pūjā* means, literally, to honor the great sage Vyāsa, who is everyone’s *guru*, since all spiritually advanced persons take information and guidance from Vedic knowledge as he presented it.

Lord Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that the spiritual science descends through human history by a process called *paramparā*, or “succession,” in which one fully Kṛṣṇa conscious teacher passes on his own perfect spiritual understanding to a fully qualified student, who thereby becomes the next perfect teacher. It is the central and incontrovertible realization of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness that Śrīla Prabhupāda is the most perfect representative of his own perfect spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and that Śrīla Prabhupāda is thus, within his spiritual generation, the supreme link in the chain of *paramparā*.

Beyond this, however, Śrīla Prabhupāda is the current *ācārya* who guides the destiny of the Vaiṣṇava community in a broad historical sense. The word *ācārya* is derived from the word *ācāra*, meaning “behavior” or, more specifically, “proper behavior.” Thus an *ācārya* is one who teaches not only by precept but by his own behavior in this world. The word *ācārya* at times indicates a bona fide spiritual master, a learned teacher of the spiritual science. But in its highest sense, the *ācārya* is the great soul sent by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself to teach the entire world.

We acknowledge the special position Śrīla Prabhupāda occupies as the *ācārya* of the entire world by calling him the founder-ācārya of the society he created, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda himself insisted on this specific title, and not merely the title *ācārya*, so that the truth would be clear. Śrīla Prabhupāda is not merely a bona fide spiritual master in disciplic succession, nor just a learned professor of the spiritual science who teaches by example as well as precept. More than this, Śrīla Prabhupāda is the ultimate spiritual authority for the entire world, and all those who wish to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the best way must validate their realizations and conclusions in reference to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings.

Thus the faithful followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda regard the occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā not merely as a chance to fulfill the traditional duty to honor one’s *guru*, nor just as a pleasant annual opportunity to relish anecdotes about the life of a great soul. Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā is certainly these things, but it is much more than that. Precisely because Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself has chosen Śrīla Prabhupāda to lead the Vaiṣṇava community, and indeed all humanity and all living beings, on he progressive path back home, back to Godhead, the Vyāsa-pūjā day of Śrīla Prabhupāda is an occasion on which the most fortunate Vaiṣṇavas in the world reaffirm a central and essential truth of the Lord’s mission on the earth: that Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu has personally empowered Śrīla Prabhupāda to reveal in the clearest and best way the Lord’s desire for our world. Śrīla Prabhupāda embodies most perfectly the mission of the Supreme Lord, and any deviation from his example or teachings is a lessening of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, a weakening of the very principle of *yoga*: to link up with the Supreme in intimate loving service.

Let us rededicate ourselves to the most vital and precious understanding of our lives: that Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, has chosen Śrīla Prabhupāda to lead us and that we shall best serve the Lord by humble and exclusive obedience to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his bona fide representatives who in no way try to diminish his exalted position as the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead.

Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami  
GBC Minister of Vaiṣṇava History

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Bhānu Swami

True knowledge comes through submission to a teacher. This has been recognized in India since time immemorial. What to speak of spiritual matters, it is a tradition in India to accept a *guru* even to learn material arts and sciences, for it is understood that this is the best way to receive knowledge. The verbal knowledge is not a secret, since it is recorded in books, but real knowledge flows to the student not through words but through communion of consciousness. As water flows down a hill, knowledge naturally flows to the student who has become submissive, receptive, and eager.

This is all quite reasonable to a spiritualist, since consciousness, a manifestation of spiritual existence, is as real as earth and water, and it similarly has its own real laws and its own creations.

Consciousness is dynamic.  
Consciousness is focused by the intelligence.  
Consciousness interacts with matter (there is a mutual influence).  
Consciousness interacts with other conscious entities.  
Consciousness acts beyond material time and space.  
Consciousness reacts personally and responds personally.  
Consciousness thrives on humility and selfless devotion.  
Consciousness manifests perfect qualities when serving the Supreme Consciousness, God, who is full of all perfect qualities.

What is knowledge? In the *Upaniṣads* and the *Vedānta-sūtra, vidyā* (knowledge) is equated with *bhakti*—devotion and worship—rather than with intellectual discipline. As the *Bhagavad-gītā* says, *rāja-vidyā rāja-guhyam:* devotional service is the highest knowledge. The process of devotional service (*bhakti* or *vidyā*), is the science for directing consciousness to its natural perfection, *prema*.

The process of *bhakti* is learned through conscious reception, a flow of mercy from the *guru*, after one accepts him as the authority. The scriptures have told us there is no other way. *Guru* means “heavy” or “important.” That means the disciple must regard the teacher as essential for his progress. The perfect example is Śrīla Prabhupāda, who took the words of his *guru* as his life and soul. To comprehend the meaning of *guru,* and thereby to strengthen the bond and the communication, the disciple must daily worship the *guru* before worshiping the Lord. And to further strengthen the bond, the disciple must worship the *guru* more elaborately on his appearance day. This particular worship, called Vyāsa-pūjā, commemorates his presence on earth to deliver the fallen souls.

Our line of written *Bhāgavata* knowledge starts with Vyāsa. Vyāsa first recorded and divided the *Vedas,* and then he wrote the *Purāṇas* and the *Mahābhārata*. He finally revealed the highest form of God, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. With this act, Vyāsa made *Bhāgavata* knowledge available to the general public. That was not sufficient, however, since, as we have mentioned, knowledge is carried not by words but by consciousness. Thus Vyāsa accepted the role of teacher and imparted the highest knowledge, devotion to Kṛṣṇa—the import of his written works—to his disciples. His disciples in their turn imparted this knowledge to their disciples. This line of transmission by way of devotional consciousness has given us Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Vyāsa-pūjā thus refers to the worship of the *guru* who is representing Vyāsa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by the grace of his *guru,* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, and by the grace of the line of all previous *ācāryas* reaching back to Vyāsadeva, fulfilled the expectations of his *paramparā* by completing the task of bringing the *Bhāgavata* knowledge to the whole world. This is the greatest accomplishment in recent human history.

On Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā day, we, his direct disciples, affirm our connection with him by means of submission, praise, and worship. For each of us he is as alive as we wish him to be, since his consciousness, being eternal and ever-present, is perceptible when we approach him with a submissive attitude. Thus he can still guide our advancement, impart to us the qualities that moved the world during his time on earth, and keep alive the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement as he envisaged and established it.

For those who are disciples of his disciples, what is the difference? Can they not reap the same benefits by approaching him (an eternal soul) directly now? Yes, they can reap the same benefits, but first they must learn with submission from their *guru* through direct personal interaction. Having learned submission, and having learned the glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and having worshiped and praised him, the same knowledge will certainly shine in their hearts.

May the devotion that shone in our *paramparā* and blazed in His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda reflect upon us as we attempt to offer homage as his humble servants.

Bhānu Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyan dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Of the innumerable gifts you have given to me,  
one of the greatest is how you have changed the way that I see.

From the bewildering maze of attachment and aversion,  
I can’t help but marvel at my gradual conversion.

Every calculation was based on how I could enjoy;  
every product of nature was seen as my toy.

Even my occasional impulses of charity and kindness  
were ultimately useless due to my inherent blindness.

How could I have helped to save a drowning man  
when I wasn’t a strong swimmer and had no idea where was land?

Kṛṣṇa’s eight separated energies create the “river of names.”  
The conditioned souls are swept away by Māyā’s endless games.

When I heard you quote the *Gītā* I awoke from the dead:  
“All things rest on Me, like pearls on a thread.”

As I listened, the puzzle pieces fell into place.  
Clear vision finally granted, by Your Divine Grace.

You’ve built a house in which the whole world can live.  
There is no greater gift that any person can give.

The duty of the disciple is to always feel indebted;  
I don’t want to die with this one thing regretted.

Please don’t let me miss this chance to serve your great mission.  
Please let me always remember my constitutional position.

Aspiring to be a genuine and qualified disciple,  
Badrinārāyan dāsa

## Bhakti Charu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my humble obeisances at your lotus feet for rescuing me from the dangerous ocean of nescience and allowing me to become connected to Your Divine Grace.

Very mercifully you brought me to the spiritual platform. On that platform I saw my real identity—my spiritual identity, as your dog. I was lost in this vast and cruel world, but you took pity on me, picked me up, cleaned me, fed me, and showered your affection upon me.

My life got a new meaning. I never knew what happiness was, but you made me swim in an ocean of joy. I felt so grateful to you, but I did not know how to express it. I just jumped and barked with joy. While many thought it to be a disturbance, you smiled and patted me on the head. Sometimes you even picked me up on your lap. At that time my heart would melt with a feeling that I never experienced before. I knew that my life had achieved its perfection.

Everyone called you Prabhupāda, but I did not know what Prabhupāda actually meant. I presumed it meant the monarch of the entire world because I used to see how you were always so concerned about the welfare of everyone. Sometimes you would cry to think how the living entities were suffering in this world. I used to wonder how much love you had in your heart, to feel this way for everyone.

Those who knew you told me that you had come to establish the kingdom of God. And you did. You conquered the whole world all by yourself. Although it is the most unique achievement in the history of this world, you never established yourself as the king. You make the King of kings, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, sit on the throne, and you ruled as His faithful representative.

I was told that Bharata, the brother of Rāmacandra, also did that a long time ago. In Lord Rāmacandra’s absence, he put Lord Rāmacandra’s shoes on the throne and ruled the kingdom as His representative. During the fourteen years of Lord Rāmacandra’s absence, Bharata wore the bark of a tree, did not apply oil on his hair, and ate only boiled barley.

To me your example is even greater: You conquered the world yourself, established the Lord on the throne, and took care of all the affairs as His loyal servant. You wore the robes of a *sannyāsī,* shaved your head, and ate only *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. You are the perfect example of renunciation.

You taught the world that everything belongs to Kṛṣṇa and everyone is being maintained by Him, and that therefore Kṛṣṇa must be accepted as the Supreme Lord of the universe. Whoever does that becomes happy. Those who do not will suffer endlessly.

I do not know Kṛṣṇa, but I know you. I know that you are maintaining me. Since you gave me the shelter of your lotus feet, my life has become so sublime that I do not want anything else besides remaining attached to those lotus feet.

\* \* \*

Then one day I could not find you. I started to cry like a child. You always took me with you wherever you went, but this time you left me behind. Those who knew you told me you did not leave, you are still with me. However, as I do not have proper vision, I cannot see you. You want me to open my spiritual eyes and develop my real vision.

Therefore, you first appeared in front of my material eyes and allowed me to see your physical form to develop my love for you. Now you have disappeared from that vision to force me to open my spiritual eyes for the sake of seeing you again. To see you, I must anoint my eyes with the mascara of love. When my heart becomes filled with my love for you, then I will be able to see you again and be with you as before.

I do not know how to do anything. Whatever I learnt, I learnt from you. I am just a dog. I do not even know what love means. Please teach me how to love you because I depend upon you for everything. I just want to remain as your dog eternally.

In your absence my heart aches. Sometimes that pain finds a vent to come out in the form of tears. I feel such a relief at that time. I wish I could drain my heart completely of that pain and have tears flow incessantly from my eyes. Sometimes I dream of you; my heart becomes filled with joy again and I want to leap out of the confines of my body. And when I can’t, then my body leaps up into the sky along with my heart and my thoughts want to cry out loud, “You are my everything, and all I want is to be with you.”

Aspiring to be your dog eternally,  
Bhakti Charu Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You have touched the lives of so many suffering souls. Even though you gave your association but briefly to so many devotees, these precious moments have been locked in the interior of their hearts for eternity. Such is the power of the pure devotee. He is like a touchstone: whoever comes in contact with him becomes imbued with pure love of Kṛṣṇa, and all perfection develops:

*‘sādhu-saṅga’, ‘sādhu-saṅga’—sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya*

In my recent visit to Jakarta, where you visited in 1973, I had the opportunity to meet such a fortunate soul who but longs for your association. She still has pictures of you sitting on the *vyāsāsana* receiving garlands during *guru-pūjā*. You did not perform any fire sacrifice when giving her initiation, as she was quite young and from an Indian family. You explained that the fire *yajña* is mainly for purification but that in her case she was already pure, so you simply chanted on her beads and awarded her the name Aravindākṣā-devī dāsī, the first and only devotee you initiated in Indonesia, on your first and only visit to Jakarta. She related what she called a miracle you performed while in Jakarta. For your morning walk the devotees had taken you to a nearby park. In Indonesia the parks are closed to the public in the early morning. Still, the devotees thought they would try, as you had expressed a desire to walk in the park. Upon arriving at the park, the devotees tried but were unsuccessful in getting the attendant to give permission to enter the park. During all this time you were simply sitting in the car. After a few moments, noticing that there was some delay, you rolled down the window and asked a simple question: “Is there any difficulty?” When the park attendant saw you and heard you say this, with deep and profound respect he immediately consented to open the park for you, and he allowed you and your devotees to stroll in the park for one hour. All the devotees were extremely happy, especially Aravindākṣā-devī dāsī. You also came to know that they have corn-on-the-cob in Indonesia. You asked if Aravindākṣā Mātājī knew how to cook it as they do in India, on an open flame with the corn making some funny sound. She was able to prepare some nice corn for you, which you enjoyed very much. All these memories were just coming back to her, and I could see in her eyes how fond she was of recollecting these wonderful memories of your wonderful pastimes in Jakarta.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your devotees live on such memories. Indeed, by just a few moments’ association with a pure devotee like yourself, any conditioned soul can relish again and again within his heart such precious moments of the association with a pure devotee.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

How fortunate is that soul who, after wondering in this universe from planet to planet, gets the opportunity to come in contact with a pure devotee of the Lord! Because he carries Kṛṣṇa, that pure devotee can give the seed of pure *bhakti*, the highest and rarest gift anyone can receive. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this wonderful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You are still giving it today to anyone who comes in contact with you. Please help me to become your surrendered devotee and to fondly remember your wonderful pastimes. Please teach me how to engage in your service nicely. Thank you for your kind attention.

Your servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In explaining life’s mission, Your Divine Grace writes:

“A human being is defeated in all his activities as long as he does not know the goal of life, which can be understood when one is inquisitive about Brahman. It is such inquiry that begins *Vedānta-sūtra: athāto brahma-jijñāsā*. A human being should be inquisitive to know who he is, what the universe is, what God is and what the relationship is between himself, God, and the material world. Such questions cannot be asked by cats and dogs, but they must arise in the heart of a real human being. Knowledge of these four items—namely oneself, the universe, God, and their internal relationship—is called *sambandha-jñāna,* or the knowledge of one’s relationship. When one’s relationship with the Supreme Lord is established, the next program is to act in that relationship. This is called *abhidheya,* or activity in relationship with the Lord. After executing such prescribed duties, when one attains the highest goal of life, love of Godhead, he achieves *prayojana-siddhi,* or the fulfillment of his human mission. In the *Brahma-sūtra,* or *Vedānta-sūtra,* these subjects are very carefully explained. Therefore one who does not understand the *Vedānta-sūtra* in terms of these principles is simply wasting his time. This is the version of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.8)” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā* 7.146, purport).

Your Divine Grace also explains how this mission is accomplished by the mercy of a saintly person:

“Once we had the opportunity to meet Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, and on first sight he requested this humble self to preach his message in the Western countries. There was no preparation for this, but somehow or other he desired it, and by his grace we are now engaged in executing his order, which has given us a transcendental occupation and has saved and liberated us from the occupation of material activities. Thus it is actually a fact that if one meets a saintly person completely engaged in transcendental duties and achieves his favor, then one’s life mission becomes complete. What is not possible to achieve in thousands of lives can be achieved in one moment if there is an opportunity to meet a saintly person. It is therefore enjoined in the Vedic literature that one should always try to associate with saintly persons and try to disassociate oneself from the common man because by one word of a saintly person one can be liberated from material entanglement. A saintly person has the power, because of his spiritual advancement, to give immediate liberation to the conditioned soul” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.22.5, purport)

You further elaborate on the relationship of the *guru* and the disciple in your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta:*

“Śrī Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja comments in this connection, ‘One can become perfectly successful in the mission of his life if he acts exactly according to the words he hears from the mouth of his spiritual master.’ This acceptance of the words of the spiritual master is called *śrauta-viya,* which indicates that the disciple must carry out the spiritual master’s instructions without deviation. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura remarks in this connection that a disciple must accept the words of his spiritual master as his life and soul. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu here confirms this by saying that since His spiritual master ordered Him only to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, He always chanted the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* according to this direction (*kṛṣṇa-mantra japa sadā, ei mantra-sāra*)” (*Ādi-līlā* 7.72 purport)

You tell us of the mercy of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa in the Eight Chapter of *The Science of Self-Realization:*

“When I left India alone in 1965, I feared that I would have a great deal of trouble. The Indian government would not allow me to take any money out of the country, so I came with only a few books and forty rupees. I arrived in New York City in such a condition, but it was all by the grace of Guru Mahārāja and Kṛṣṇa. Everything happens by the combined mercy of Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master.”

You give us more instruction on how the disciple should take the *guru’s* instruction as his life and soul in the Fourth Canto of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

“After being initiated and receiving the orders of the spiritual master, the disciple should unhesitatingly think about the instructions or orders of the spiritual master and should not allow himself to be disturbed by anything else. This is also the verdict of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, who, while explaining a verse of *Bhagavad-gītā* (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* Bg. 2.41), points out that the order of the spiritual master is the life substance of the disciple. The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master. Thus a disciple should always meditate on the order of the spiritual master, and that is perfectional meditation. Not only should he meditate upon that order, but he should find out the means by which he can perfectly worship and execute it” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.15, purport).

Again in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, Your Divine Grace explains the mission of the spiritual master as follows:

“According to revealed scriptures, if a spiritual master can convert even one soul into a perfectly pure devotee, his mission in life is fulfilled. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura always used to say, ‘Even at the expense of all the properties, temples, and *maṭhas* that I have, if I could convert even one person into a pure devotee, my mission would be fulfilled.’ It is very difficult, however, to understand the science of Kṛṣṇa, what to speak of developing love of Godhead. Therefore if by the grace of Lord Caitanya and the spiritual master a disciple attains the standard of pure devotional service, the spiritual master is very happy. The spiritual master is not actually happy if the disciple brings him money, but when he sees that a disciple is following the regulative principles and advancing in spiritual life, he is very glad and feels obliged to such an advanced disciple” (*Ādi-līlā* 7.91 purport).

You glorify the *ācāryas* as follows:

“The greatest philanthropists are those transcendentalists who represent the mission of Vyāsa, Nārada, Madhva, Caitanya, Rūpa, Sarasvatī, etc. They are all one and the same. The personalities may be different, but the aim of the mission is one and the same, namely to deliver the fallen souls back home, back to Godhead” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.4.17–18, purport).

Your Divine Grace explains the mission of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in the following way:

“The mission of Lord Kṛṣṇa was performed on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra, for by the Lord’s mercy Arjuna was victorious due to being a great devotee whereas the others were killed simply by the Lord’s glance, which cleansed them of all sinful activities and enabled them to attain *sārūpya*. Finally, Lord Kṛṣṇa instructed Uddhava about the transcendental life of devotional service, and then, in due course of time, He returned to His abode. The Lord’s instructions in the form of *Bhagavad-gītā* are full of *jñāna* and *vairāgya,* knowledge and renunciation. In the human form of life, one must learn these two things—how to become detached from the material world and how to acquire full knowledge in spiritual life. This is the Lord’s mission (*paritrānāya sādhūnāṁ vināśāya ca duṣkṛtām*). After executing His complete mission, the Lord returned to His home, Goloka Vṛndāvana” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.24.67, purport)

We find more about the Lord’s mission in the Third Canto:

“The mission of the appearance of the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the form of Kapila was to distribute the transcendental knowledge of Sāṅkhya philosophy, which is full of devotional service. Having imparted that knowledge to His mother—and, through His mother, to the world—Kapiladeva had no more need to stay at home, so He took permission from His mother and left. Apparently He left home for spiritual realization, although He had nothing to realize spiritually because He Himself is the person to be spiritually realized. Therefore this is an example set by the Supreme Personality of Godhead while acting like an ordinary human being so that others might learn from Him. He could, of course, have stayed with His mother, but He indicated that there was no need to stay with the family. It is best to remain alone as a *brahmacārī, sannyāsī,* or *vānaprastha* and cultivate Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout one’s whole life. Those who are unable to remain alone are given license to live in household life with wife and children, not for sense gratification but for cultivation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.33.12, purport)

In a lecture in Bombay in 1974 you told us about the mission of Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the duty of those born in India:

“*Pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma, sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma.* \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\] And that is the duty of the Indians, Bhārata-vāsīs. That is also Caitanya Mahāprabhu's desire. *Bhārata-bhūmite manuṣya-janma haila yāra, janma sārthaka kari’ kara para-upakāra.* This Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy, *kṛṣṇa-prema* distribution, is the best, topmost *para-upakāra,* welfare activity. Therefore every Indian should understand the philosophy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu or the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the same philosophy. Kṛṣṇa says his philosophy is *sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇam vraja:* ‘Just surrender to Me. I will give you protection.’ And Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s philosophy is the same. He is Kṛṣṇa. But as He has appeared as a devotee, He says, *yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa:* “Whomever you meet, you Indian, Bhārata-vāsī, you just try to understand this Kṛṣṇa philosophy, *Bhagavad-gītā,* and make your life successful and go door to door and say this *‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*.” (lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* November 30, 1974, Bombay)

You have more to say on this topic on the Fifth Canto:

“Unfortunately, because of the influence of Kali-yuga, everything is now being lost. The inhabitants of Bhārata-varṣa are gradually becoming degraded *mlecchas* and *yavanas*. How then will they teach others? Therefore, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has been started not only for the inhabitants of Bhārata-varṣa but for all the people of the world, as announced by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. There is still time, and if the inhabitants of Bhārata-varṣa take this movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously, the entire world will be saved from gliding down to a hellish condition” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.19.10, purport).

From the Preface to your *Teachings of Lord Caitanya* we learn more about Lord Caitanya and his *saṅkīrtana* movement:

“Lord Caitanya is the ideal teacher of life’s prime necessities. He is the most munificent bestower of love of Kṛṣṇa. He is the complete reservoir of all mercies and good fortune. As confirmed in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the *Bhagavad-gītā,* the *Mahābhārata* and the *Upaniṣads,* He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa Himself, and He is worshipable by everyone in this age of disagreement. Everyone can join in His *saṅkīrtana* movement. No previous qualification is necessary. Just by following His teachings, anyone can become a perfect human being. If a person is fortunate enough to be attracted by Lord Caitanya, he is sure to be successful in his life’s mission. In other words, those who are interested in attaining spiritual existence can easily be released from the clutches of *māyā* by the grace of Lord Caitanya.”

In the *Madhya-līlā* you explain how easy it is to follow Lord Caitanya’s process:

“This is the sublime mission of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Many people come and inquire whether they have to give up family life to join the Society, but that is not our mission. One can remain comfortably in his residence. We simply request everyone to chant the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 7.128, purport).

To inspire sincere souls in general to attain life’s mission, Your Divine Grace has brought out the wonderful example of Dhruva Mahārāja as follows:

“What was the task that Dhruva Mahārāja undertook? The most difficult task in life is to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and Dhruva Mahārāja would be able to do that. We must remember that Dhruva Mahārāja was not fickle: he was determined to execute his service and then come back. Every devotee, therefore, should be determined that in this life he will be able to satisfy the Supreme Personality of Godhead and by that process go back home, back to Godhead. That is the perfection of the highest mission of life” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.69 purport).

And a little further on in the same chapter:

“It is our duty to remember always that in comparison to Dhruva Mahārāja we are insignificant. We cannot do anything like what Dhruva Mahārāja did for self-realization because we are absolutely incompetent to execute such service. But by Lord Caitanya’s mercy we have been given all concessions possible for this age, so at least we should always remember that neglect of our prescribed duties in devotional service will not make us successful in the mission we have undertaken. It is our duty to follow in the footsteps of Dhruva Mahārāja, for he was very determined. We should also be determined to finish our duties in executing devotional service in this life; we should not wait for another life to finish our job” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.72 purport).

In making a humble attempt to execute your divine orders, this insignificant servant is making a humble attempt to host the Second World Congress for the Synthesis of Science and Religion, to be held in Calcutta, in December of 1996, in honor of the celebration of the 100th anniversary of your divine appearance on this planet. Many leading scientists, philosophers, and students in Bengal are taking a keen interest in participating in and helping with this historic congress. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always instructed me that you would send all the men and money necessary to fulfill these Bhaktivedanta Institute activities. By your divine mercy this has always happened, and many sincere souls and well-wishers are coming forward to glorify Your Divine Grace.

Also, for the first time in the history of Vedic culture, by your inspiration and unlimited mercy a Master of Science and Ph.D. program for the study of consciousness well be inaugurated in Bombay on a grand scale during your Centennial celebrations. Many of your very dedicated and competent granddisciples are taking primary roles in accomplishing these projects for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace. These programs are stepping stones toward changing the modern material paradigm to the consciousness-based paradigm of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

On this most auspicious day of Your Divine Appearance, kindly shower your blessings upon us so that we can always beg for your mercy and in this way try to understand life’s mission.

Your insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please permit me to offer humble obeisances on this most grand occasion of your divine appearance day. On your appearance days, disappearance days, holidays, festivals, etc., when I should be spiritually focused, I normally find myself selfishly absorbed with my mental and bodily demands. So today, unfortunately, is similar. Perhaps one can say I am very regulated and consistent with my nonsense. Such regulation in the mode of ignorance and passion is a very poor display for a disciple of yours.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have come to a point in time when for over half of my life in this body I have been engaged in devotional service. There are many of your sons and daughters around the world in similar positions. I pray that they have offered you much more than I have and have been more grateful about what you have done and are still doing for us. As I look back, I see how you have protected me time and time again, constantly being very compassionate, forgiving, and tolerant. However, when I look at my record, I have been a neophyte, and have been very expert, when it comes to your devotees and your service, in deluding, polluting, refusing, confusing, abusing, and misusing all that I survey.

As I think about the next half or quarter of my life, I first realize that, even if I live a normal life span, I am already over two-thirds dead. Now I must seriously ask myself, How shall I engage in my devotional service? i.e., What shall I offer you these last few years of my life? I pray that I do not continue business as usual by continuing to offer you mediocrity at best. But I pray that this last sector of my life will be a chance for me to make up for my poor achievements and commitments thus far. So many of us are now grandparents, and even some are great-grandparents, i.e., those who are householders, as well as those, like myself, who have disciples who have children and grandchildren. We are all of your seed—your family. Now we must go about our lives so everyone will clearly realize, by examining your spiritual offspring, how powerful and merciful a mentor you were and are.

In my own case, I feel I have seriously disappointed you, for I have not come up to the mark, nor have I offered you grandchildren who are much better. We are, sad to say, poor representatives of your tolerance and mercy. Today I beg forgiveness for them as well as myself, and I beg forgiveness for other devotees who have been as ungrateful and dull as I am. Please do not let us receive what we deserve, for we would be rather helpless and pitiful. Rather, please continue to tolerate us, but also push us on more and more; otherwise we will be a serious embarrassment to you and our spiritual family heritage.

My own lack of potency and gratitude, my sabotaging your movement (spiritual family), and my desires are so great that I feel that my only hope for a chance to be forgiven rests in my ability to help create more of a strong spiritual family. Therefore, I have set out to be a catalyst to help devotees in trying to establish holistic self-sufficient communities that are less and less dependent on the normal structures of a materialistic, demonic society.

I have no knowledge or potency to do this, but if I love your devotees, how can I stand by and watch so much suffering go on due so often to a lack of material and spiritual integratedness and security? If I love you, how can I not try to offer myself to one of your greatest desires—the establishment of self-sufficient *varṇāśrama* communities. If I have any love and compassion for the materialists, how can I do anything less than try to help create some model spiritual communities? For there is no better way to attract people to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and there is no better way to keep people in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, PLEASE HELP! *Māyā* is attacking more and more, and the tasks at hand to build for the future are most overwhelming—but fully possible to perform by your love and mercy.

As always, I remain selfishly begging for forgiveness and mercy.

Your son,  
Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, but my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Now we are approaching the hundredth anniversary of your appearance, and it is amazing to see how many devotees and followers of Your Divine Grace are becoming enthusiastic and inspired to actively participate in the many wonderful preparations to celebrate your all-auspicious appearance in this world one hundred years ago. What is more wonderful and auspicious in this world than spreading your glories to others? Just by hearing and conversing about your unfathomable qualities, activities, and achievements, the whole universe becomes purified. Let the whole world glorify you! Then Lord Kṛṣṇa will certainly be very pleased. In the *Ādi Purāṇa* Lord Kṛṣṇa addresses Arjuna: “My dear Pārtha, one who claims to be My devote is not so. Only a person who claims to be the devotee of My devote is actually My devotee.”

In conditioned existence, no one can approach the Supreme Personality of Godhead directly. One must approach Him through His pure devotee, who is more dear to Kṛṣṇa than His own self. Indeed, Kṛṣṇa Himself spoke the following verse: “I am walking behind My devotees so that some of the dust from their feet will fall on My head. Then by their blessing I will be able to do all these wonderful things which I do. Actually, I have no power. It is only because of the blessings of the devotees that I am able to perform all this.” What is our position, then? How much more we have to be eager to gain your blessings in order to do something useful for your satisfaction? O Śrīla Prabhupāda, O ocean of mercy, be merciful unto us. Give us the shade of your lotus feet and purify us. Let us hold on to your lotus feet. Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to us.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for providing us with ever-new and -increasing challenges to keep us absorbed in serving your mission, and please bless us to always remain in the wonderful association of your disciples and granddisciples, which simply deepens our attachment to Your Divine Grace at every moment.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The spiritual world—  
Goloka Vṛndāvana—  
is real.  
I wish to go there  
as your servant.  
This world  
I no longer see  
as a place of enjoyment,  
for I am older  
and my life  
for twenty-seven years  
has been yours.

I want to go there, Prabhupāda,  
to Goloka;  
as you told me in Japan, 1972,  
when I glibly volunteered  
to serve  
birth after birth.  
“Don’t make me stay here  
to accept your service.”  
I want to go back home,  
back to Godhead,  
and serve through you,  
forever, Prabhupāda.

Your instructions,  
both personal and general,  
guide my journey;  
Kṛṣṇa’s names I chant on your order;  
Your books I read  
now hours and hours  
each day  
in forsaken Bangladesh,  
where unlimited suffering  
is heaped on Muslim Bengali masses  
(who not only forgot Mahāprabhu  
but who despise all non-Muslim followers  
of Allah).

Yes, Prabhupāda,  
you are clearly here  
in your temple and your books.  
Personal association  
is for fools, you said.  
And I find myself  
a fool  
who needs you—  
personally before me—  
so I can touch  
your lotus feet  
and clear my heart  
with your words.

I sit here  
on a hard chair  
with a hard heart,  
needing help,  
needing you,  
in separation.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bir Krishna Dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your divine lotus feet! All glories to your ISKCON movement! All glories to the devotees who have devoted their lives to your service.

I want to thank you for the wonderful realizations that you blessed me with this year at your *samādhi* in Śrī Māyāpur-dhāma. I was overwhelmed with your presence and kindness, even though my service to you has been minuscule at best.

When touring the museum on the second level of your *samādhi,* I perceived your selfless dedication to carrying out the orders of your spiritual master. The dioramas displayed your one-pointed determination and enthusiasm in reaching into the hearts of the fallen conditioned souls to give them *kṛṣṇa-prema*. This is your life and soul. I saw you descending from the *Jaladuta* in New York with fierce determination. Preaching to the tea-drinkers in Delhi. Dancing in ecstasy to see Lord Jagannātha carried on His Ratha-yātrā cart in San Francisco. Intensely trying to change the hearts of the hippies in Tompkins Square Park and in your small room in New York. And finally, continuing to dictate your Bhaktivedanta Purports to the very end of your manifest pastimes.

I felt I was with you at every stage. I wished that I had been with you to personally assist you. Then I realized that your pastimes were continuing and did not end with your departure. The Māyāpur *samādhi* is evidence of this!!!

It (the *samādhi*) is evidence that your movement is stronger than ever. Not only has your movement continued as you directed, but the number of devotees, number of temples, and number of distributed books have increased exponentially. This is an attestation to the fact that you are still with us.

Your servants have sacrificed their personal happiness for your pleasure. If this were not so, such a magnificent memorial to Your Divine Grace could not have been finished. I bow millions of times to the feet of those servants of yours: the wonderful book distributors, those who donated money, those who gave their intelligence, and all those who worked in different capacities to please you by honoring you. The most wonderful realization I had when I took *darśana* of your *samādhi* was the understanding that your movement indeed will take over the world. It is not simply some wishful thinking or hope. You have built a house in which the whole world can live, and in fact the whole world will be worshiping you in the future. The big temple in Māyāpur that Lord Nityānanda and Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura predicted will be constructed by your followers. People will come from all the three worlds to see it and glorify you. It will happen!! Without a doubt!

Your purpose in coming here was not simply to convince a few people, nor maintain a few temples. Your purpose was to engulf the whole universe in the ocean of pure devotional service. And we are being offered the mercy of assisting you in doing this. We only have to give up our selfishness—our desire to enjoy—our small-mindedness.

I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to be able to constantly remember this purpose, to constantly act in consonance with this purpose, and to assist those who have dedicated their lives to this purpose. I want to be able to please you. You have shown me the way.

Your insignificant servant,  
Bir Krishna Dāsa Goswami

## Gour Govinda Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O my dear Gurudeva, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The moments of time that I have so fortunately spent with you, serving at your holy lotus feet as an unworthy servant, those auspicious moments have been stored in my heart of hearts and imprinted in my memory as eternal. And now I am living as your most unworthy servant in the service of the Supreme Lord, with you as the charioteer and guide, leading me through the maze of confusions, showing the truest enlightened path of pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I am eternally obliged.

On this auspicious occasion of your holy appearance day, I, on behalf of all the devotees who could not get a place in this book, am offering my prostrated *daṇḍavat praṇāmas,* my humblest obeisances, at your divine lotus feet. You have shown me the way to serve the supreme cause of ŚRĪ ŚRĪ GURU AND GAURĀṄGA. The sense of service you have instilled in me is unfathomable, pure, and beyond anybody’s debate or appraisal. You are the essence of my existence.

Arjuna questioned the Lord in *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā:*

*atha kena prayukto ’yaṁ pāpaṁ carati pūruṣaḥ*  
*anicchann api vārṣṇeya balād iva niyojitaḥ*

**“O descendant of Vṛṣṇi, by what is one impelled to sinful acts, even unwillingly, as if engaged by force?”**

Gurudeva, I am extremely pained at the sight of this so-called modern material world. The mundane *karmīs* are suffering out of sheer ignorance. Their inefficiency in seeing the glory of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord and the Absolute Truth, and in not surrendering unto His Divine Lotus Feet, paves the way for Māyā to spread her ever-growing tentacles and bind them to the sorrows and distress of the mundane material world forever. Therefore, O dear Śrīla Gurudeva, I herewith pray at your lotus feet to please have mercy upon them. Kindly enable these prisoners of a fools’ paradise to see the enlightening nectarean path that leads to the Supreme Divine Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa.

O my dear Gurudeva Śrīla Prabhupāda, being inspired by Your Divine Grace, this unworthy yet eternal servant of yours is somehow conceiving to take part next year in the worldwide Centennial, the celebration of the hundredth anniversary of your auspicious appearance. I am doing this in the way that Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace are prompting me in my heart of hearts. The following are a few of the ambitious Centennial programs we intend to pursue here in your last-founded temple project at Bhubaneswar, the capital city of Orissa, the land of Lord Jagannātha.

1\) To help establish an ideal world order, we are planning to found a self-supporting residential Vedic Gurukula and University. Subjects to be taught there will include Bhakti-śāstra, Sanskrit, Astrology, Āyurveda, Gāndharva-śāstra, Indian Art and Architecture, Comparative Study of Religions, Computer Science, Behavioral Science, Environmental Studies, Agriculture-Horticulture, and Mass Communications—all in the midst of a pure Kṛṣṇa conscious environment.  
We intend that this Vedic preaching project, with its basis in the practice of purity, will become one of the most advanced and ideal multidimensional, sacred Kṛṣṇa conscious educational institutes. Thus it will help form the foundation of a Kṛṣṇa conscious world order. It will educate students from kindergarten to the postgraduate level, Ph.D., and onwards, and will include a program to regularly preach the nectarean *kṛṣṇa-kathā* from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Śrīmad *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, etc. In this way this institute will inculcate pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the heart of hearts of its thousands of students, aiming for the highest attainment of supreme spiritual perfection in this rarely obtainable human form of life.  
2\) We plan a vigorous renovation, restoration, and beautification program of (a) the temple of His Lordship Śrī Śrī Ālanātha-deva at Brahmagiri, near Purī, and (b) the ancient Śrī Haridāsa Ṭhākura Samādhi at Swargadwar, Purī.  
3\) We will engage in a large-scale vegetarian and “kindness-toward-animals” awareness program to inculcate in the hearts of human beings love and respect for their fellow living entities, and  
4\) We will promote a massive forestation project for the service of Lord Jagannātha, the Supreme Lord, especially with the objective of making an adequate wood supply available both for the great Kitchen Hall of the Jagannātha Temple and for the construction of the chariots for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Mai at Śrī Jagannātha Purī.

However, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, without your blessings and guidance I feel myself quite feeble and not at all able to move even a step forward in this direction. Therefore, dear eternal Spiritual Master, kindly bestow upon this unworthy yet eternal servant of Your Divine Grace the ability to move ahead and successfully render unalloyed service to accomplish these Centennial objectives for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace and Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord. Please shower your nectarean mercy upon all of us associated with these projects so that we can successfully discharge these enormous responsibilities by deriving strength from the Supreme Lord and His dear and pure devotee, Your Divine Grace.

Whatever I do,  
Wherever I go,  
Whatever I preach,  
and wherever I reach,  
Everything is because of you and for you. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your unworthy yet eternal servant,  
Gour Govinda Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

**Keeping Śrīla Prabhupāda in the Center**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This past year you gave me a powerful experience and taught me how to keep Your Divine Grace in the center of my heart. The experience was a dream, the most vivid and inspiring dream of Your Divine Grace that I have ever had.

But I am a beginner in spiritual life, and you have taught us that immature devotees should submit their realizations to *guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra* for verification. We should approach an authority to confirm whatever we feel, think, or do. Beyond the safe enclosure of Vedic authority lies the jungle of speculation, where death waits at every turn.

So I researched what you have said about dreaming of the spiritual master, and I came across this passage in a morning-walk conversation in Los Angeles in 1976:

**Bharadvāja:** What happens when he dreams of the spiritual master or Kṛṣṇa?

**Prabhupāda:** That’s all right. Dreaming means he’s seeing. The seer is the same.

And in a letter to Mālatī dāsī you said, “So far dreaming is concerned, we regard dreaming condition as another form of illusion or *māyā,* only more subtle, that’s all. But just like we may be serving Krishna in our waking state and thinking about Him then, so also it is possible to dream about Krishna and the Spiritual Master and so many things. But generally we should not take such dreaming very seriously. These are not very important matters. But if we occasionally dream of Krishna, that is nice, that means you are making advancement in Krishna Consciousness. But do not take such things very seriously, \[because our\] important business is to become conscious of Krishna all the time by absorbing the mind, body, and words in pleasing Krishna, and that means following strictly the regulative principles and practices of devotional service, such as chanting, reading, attending arati, going for street sankirtana, like that, and engaging oneself full time in some work for Krishna. In this way, you will become too much busy serving Krishna and there will be no time for feeling emotions or dreaming, \[and\] that is advanced stage of spiritual perfection.”

I take these two passages as both an encouragement and a warning. So with caution I will tell you about the dream and the effect it had on me.

I dreamed I was with Your Divine Grace in a car. We were riding around Manila, in the Philippines, and you were commenting about degraded social conditions there. Ninety percent of the Philippine people pride themselves on being Christian, but they indulge in sinful activities, especially illicit sex. This is the height of hypocrisy, you said, and it makes a mockery of Christianity.

We drove to the Manila temple, a small run-down house in a shoddy neighborhood. Last year, the temple had been forced by a lack of financial support to give up a large two-story Spanish house on a quarter-acre estate and move to the present house, less than one-fourth as big.

Fifty or so bright-faced Filipino devotees greeted you with a lively *kīrtana*. You smiled broadly and told me that these devotees naturally took to chanting and dancing and were among the best “*kīrtanīers*” you had seen.

Inside the tiny temple room, you offered *daṇḍavats* to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi-sundara Gaura-naṭarāja. Standing again, you looked at Their Lordships for a long time. You looked around the temple and the adjoining rooms. Then you took *caraṇāmṛta,* and we waited outside. You turned and looked me in the eye. “This house is not fit for Gaura-Nitāi or the devotees to live in,” you said gravely. “Make an adequate arrangement as soon as possible.”

Suddenly we were in the Hong Kong temple room. Your Divine Grace was walking back and forth the full length of the room, softly chanting *japa*. You looked at Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and then you looked around the sparkling temple room of glass, mirrors, brass, and marble. “Everything is very nicely done,” you said, smiling, “and I am very pleased. The devotees should simply continue to preach enthusiastically.”

Then I awoke. I was feeling shaken, and I was crying. On one hand, you were pleased with what we had done in Hong Kong. But on the other hand, you were displeased with our work in Manila and gave a clear order to improve things as soon as possible.

In the days and weeks that followed, I meditated on the dream. I could feel you inspiring me, instructing and pushing in ways I had never known before. I felt a sense of urgency in your instruction. Other devotees and I set to work to fulfill your desire. Knowing that you were pleased with the Hong Kong temple and its humble preaching efforts made me feel indebted to Your Divine Grace for letting us serve you.

After a long search, we found a new building in the best part of Manila. We raised funds for the renovation, and on April 9, 1995, the new center officially opened. It has administrative offices, a library, an *āśrama,* and a large temple room that also serves as a restaurant.

Of course, a new rented center in the Philippines is not impressive on a worldwide scale, but by your mercy we were able to spread the sense of urgency to leading members of the Indian community in Manila. Now they want to help us buy land and build a large temple and cultural center. The search is on for the right property, and when we find it, the fund-raising will follow.

This must be what you had in mind when you told me, “Make an adequate arrangement as soon as possible.” You were not thinking of renting an apartment or house. You wanted a grand project for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. You wanted us to push forward the *saṅkīrtana* movement in a grand way.

Since the night Your Divine Grace came to me in a dream, I have had some important realizations. First, whether as individuals or as a Society, our success in spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement depends only on your mercy. Second, advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness also comes only by your mercy. Third, to the extent that we keep you in the center of our thoughts, words, and activities, to that extent only will we be free from envy, pride, and false prestige. And fourth, only if we love and trust one another and work together to develop ISKCON will you be pleased.

I am the least of Your Divine Grace’s disciples, and my realizations are meager at best. Still, I have firm faith that if I simply become a true follower of Your Divine Grace, some drops of your unlimited mercy will fall on me, and my efforts to please you will bear fruit.

Your humble servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

When you became ill in 1967 and decided to return to India, one devotee suggested that one of your Godbrothers might come and help your young disciples. You sat with your eyes closed. Suddenly tears began to flow from your eyes and you said in a choked voice, “My spiritual master was no ordinary spiritual master.” Then you paused, wiped the tears from your eyes, and said in an even more choked voice, “He saved me.”

Thus you showed us how we should see our spiritual master. Although there have been other spiritual masters, you were no “ordinary spiritual master.” You were extraordinarily empowered by your spiritual master and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to reclaim fallen souls like us. Factually only you were merciful and powerful enough to have delivered one as deluded and degraded as me.

“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.28)

To serve you means to serve the Supreme Lord and the disciplic succession, to follow your instructions and preach. “It is impossible for the disciple to repay the debt to the spiritual master. Therefore the disciple continually works in such a way that the spiritual master may become pleased upon him. … If you feel at all indebted to me, then you should preach vigorously like me. … Preach all over the world and you will become happy and make others happy also.”Your instruction is the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s. The Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya instructed the *brāhmaṇa* Kūrma:

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

*kabhu nā bādhibe tomāra viṣaya-taraṅga*  
*punarapi ei ṭhāñi pābe mora saṅga”*

**“Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land. If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life at home will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these regulative principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose My company.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **7.128–29)**

As Lord Caitanya’s most faithful servant, you have presented the same message to us. In the purport you elaborate, “This is an opportunity for everyone. If one simply follows the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, under the guidance of His representative, and chants the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* teaching everyone as far as possible the same principle, the contamination of the materialistic way of life will not even touch him. It does not matter whether one lives in a holy place like Vṛndāvana, Navadvīpa or Jagannātha Purī or in the midst of European cities, where the materialistic way of life is very prominent. If a devotee follows the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he lives in the company of the Lord. … This is the secret of success for one advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

On this auspicious occasion, I have only one prayer: “Let me desire only to follow you, repeat your message, serve your mission. and have your association.”

I have no other desire.

Your fallen servant,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāla Krishna Goswami

I was born in the darkness of ignorance, but you, my dear Spiritual Master, have so mercifully opened my eyes with the torchlight of transcendental knowledge. I offer my most fallen obeisances at your divine lotus feet on the ninety-ninth anniversary of your auspicious appearance on this planet.

Your lotus feet are the greatest treasure within the three worlds. From your childhood you worshipped Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, organized festivals and performed *kīrtana*. Your supreme devotion to Kṛṣṇa was exhibited throughout your life. Your every action was done as an offering to Kṛṣṇa, and every instruction was to help others come to Kṛṣṇa. In a world filled with materialism and impersonalism, your devotion shone like the sunshine, and you established *bhakti-yoga* as supreme.In the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 2.3-4) it is stated:

*sarva-loka uddhārite gaura-avatāra*  
*nistārera hetu tāra trividha prakāra*

*sākṣāt-darśana, āra yogya-bhakta-jīve*  
*‘āveśa’ karaye kāhāṅ, kāhāṅ ‘āvirbhāve’*

**“In His incarnation as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa descended to deliver all the living beings in the three worlds, from Brahmaloka down to Pātālaloka. He caused their deliverance in three ways.”**

“The Lord delivered the fallen souls in some places by meeting them directly, in other places by empowering a pure devotee, and in still other places by appearing before someone Himself.”Later in the same chapter (*Antya* 2.13-14), Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja states,

*tā-sabā tārite prabhu sei saba deśe*  
*yogya-bhakta jīva-dehe karena ‘āveśe’*

*sei jīve nija-bhakti karena prakāśe*  
*tāhāra darśane ‘vaiṣṇava’ haya sarva-deśe*

**“To deliver people in regions throughout the universe who could not meet Him, Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu personally entered the bodies of pure devotees. Thus He empowered living beings \[His pure devotees\] by manifesting in them so much of His own devotion that people in all other countries became devotees by seeing them.”**

From the above, we can conclude that you are an *āveśa-avatāra*, or a personality who was endowed with the power of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are a very dear servant of Krishna who came to establish the true teachings of Vyāsadeva for the benefit of the suffering humanity. Of all the spiritual masters, Kṛṣṇa empowered you to take His message all over this planet. Your greatness is constantly increasing as your Kṛṣṇa conscious movement continues to expand every day. Through your transcendental literatures, your glories are being known throughout the entire universe as we distribute them more and more. Your purports were dictated within your heart directly by the Supreme Lord, and therefore each word is surcharged with *bhakti* to convince us that our real business is to engage in service to Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. The might and power of your words dispell the darkness of our ignorance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the guide for all our activities. Without your mercy there is no possibility of solving any problem. Our happiness and advancement is dependent on our undeviatingly following your instructions.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, I want to fall at your lotus feet and beg for forgiveness of my countless offenses. I beg to be forever engaged in expanding your movement birth after birth. I beg for the ability to always meditate on you. I beg to become a humble servant of your divine lotus feet.

Gopāla Krishna Goswami

## Guruprasāda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, which is the transcendental foodstuff for those desiring pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

This year, on the eve of your Centennial celebration, I was considering how you have manifest Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement in its most perfect form. Your spiritual strategy is still unfolding before us in the most amazing ways. As I sit amongst the devotees, I can perceive how you have given the most precious gift: the association with devotees. It dispels *māyā* and brings us constant remembrance of yourself and Kṛṣṇa. Through your books you teach us how to associate with the devotees by developing the proper attitude in our own spiritual life.

As you often stated, you have full faith in the holy name of the Lord. I have no such faith. Whatever I may be able to do or whatever I possess is only due to your mercy. By one drop of your faith in Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, the whole world has been flooded and the hearts of all living beings are being touched, what to speak of the heart of a foolish person such as me, who am the most unworthy recipient.

You brought us the *arcā-vigraha* to reveal God as a person. The *śabara* Viśvavāsu purely worshiped the all-perfect form of the Lord by His own causeless mercy. However, by your causeless mercy and transcendental potency you have arranged for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa to come into the midst of the most undeserving.

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the most glorious nectar, which dissipates the materialistic nature of all classes of persons. Even though it is the most exalted literature and is appreciated by the *bhuvi-bhāvukās,* the most elevated souls, you have made it accessible to and appreciated by us, who have no qualification.

To give us the shelter of the Lord’s lotus feet, you have established temples and communities all over the world. They are not only places of worship but strategic points for disseminating the highest standard of devotion.

In these and countless other ways you are not only establishing Lord Caitanya’s mission, but you are demonstrating the most excellent mood of pure devotion to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, how to be fully absorbed in that mood and how to give it to all persons now and for all time to come.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Your unworthy and fallen servant,  
Guruprasāda Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

My Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who have saved the world from total degradation into the lowest modes of material nature. Simply by your grace, millions of conditioned souls are now on the gradual yet progressive path back to Godhead. You will be glorified in the future by Śrīla Vyāsadeva, who will write about your extraordinary feats in creating this ISKCON movement. May I remain your servant always.

This year I have decided to share with you three realizations that I have, and I hope that you will approve of them and give me the blessings required to fulfill them in my life.

**Realization One:** No one can replace you in my life. No one can even partially replace you. I am simply eager to continuously hear from you, your divine lectures and morning walks, and I am eager to read your books, for they give me the greatest inspiration. I cannot share my heart with anyone else. I have given it to you, and you will remain always the center of my life. Thanks to you, I can understand something about Kṛṣṇa, and I pray to you that you continue to guide me through all difficult times, so that I may remain on the straight and narrow path of progressive *bhakti-yoga*.

**Realization Two:** I am a “sold-out animal” to your mission. There is nothing else in my life than this. I know nothing other than your mission, and there is no room in my heart, mind, or intelligence for anything else. I cannot even think about anything else than your mission, for nothing else gives me any taste. Now I am yours, and you can do with me as you like. I know that you want me to increase my preaching activities (not just by giving boring classes, but factually, by bringing more people to the shelter of ISKCON), and I beg you for the strength to do this for your pleasure. I find myself sometimes overburdened by the problems of maintaining your temples and devotees and of continuing the preaching mission, but I will go on accepting this burden on my head for as long as you wish.

**Realization Three:** I am eager to assist your sincere devotees in their efforts to raise many millions of dollars for your temple in Māyāpur. I know that the temple is for Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, the *aṣṭa-sakhīs* and the *paramparā,* but in my mind this temple is yours, because you wanted it to be such. I remember how in March of 1977 you wanted so much to have this temple that you had us lay the cornerstone, and you even wanted us to start digging the foundation, even though we didn’t own a fraction of the land required and there was no design for the temple. This was truly amazing, a testimony to your determination to construct this temple as soon as possible. Of course, it is taking us many years to realize your instruction, but now things are moving along and your temple will manifest within our lifetimes so that we can die knowing you are satisfied.

Books are going out, now more than ever before. Books are continuously being made and offered to you each year. *Prasādam* is being prepared and is going out to people in large quantities. And now finally your temple is rising out of the plain of Māyāpur; it will be the beacon light to attract all the conditioned souls to your lotus feet.

Everyone will ask, “How was this possible?” and the answer will be, “Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted it. Whatever he wanted in his life, he got. Now he is glorified in the proper manner.”

Let us all work together to make Śrīla Prabhupāda’s dream come true. Let ISKCON become strong and united under the banner of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire. Let the whole world understand the need for Kṛṣṇa consciousness in their lives, so that they can surrender to the lotus feet of the Lord.

All glories to Your Divine Grace. Please bless me with the ability to please you with this otherwise wretched life. Thank you for everything.

Your eternal fool,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The Vyāsa-pūjā celebration in honor of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇu-pāda Parama-haṁsa Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedānta Swami Prabhupāda is a splendid opportunity to remember the glories of the Lord’s most confidential servant in this world. Lord Kṛṣṇa states in the *Bhagavad-gītā,*

*janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ*  
*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ’rjuna*

**“One who thus knows in truth My divine birth and activities does not, upon giving up the body, come again to material birth, for he comes to Me, O Arjuna.”**

The pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa is on the same spiritual platform as the Lord Himself, and thus surely by knowing in truth the transcendental birth and activities of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Lord’s eternally liberated associate, we will not come again to birth in the material world. To go back home, back to Śrīla Prabhupāda, means to go back home, back to Godhead, for Prabhupāda is the transparent via media of the Lord. Thus it is stated in Vedic *śāstra:*

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthā prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“If one who has transcendental devotion for God, and as much for the** ***guru*** **as for God, then surely to such a great soul all the declared meanings of the** ***Vedas*** **reveal themselves.”**

Once Śrīla Prabhupāda stated, “Do not try to see Kṛṣṇa without the *guru,* and do not try to see the *guru* without Kṛṣṇa.” Thus the holy appearance day of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda is a unique opportunity to remember Lord Kṛṣṇa by remembering His pure devotee. The many offerings in this book provide us the best opportunity to remember Śrīla Prabhupāda today by hearing from those who have given their lives to him and serve him directly, intimately, and with love.

Aspiring to become the servant of his servants,  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances.

Another year has passed, and due to the approach of the grand celebrations in honor of the hundredth anniversary of your appearance, more and more details of your manifest pastimes are being revealed. This is your causeless mercy; you so kindly appeared in this world in order to reveal the spiritual world. Everyone is absorbed in *grāmya-kathā,* hearing, speaking, and remembering some mundane news. But you exhibited such sublime features that we were distracted from such foolishness and turned to hearing about the unlimited forms, pastimes, and qualities of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Your appearance was so auspicious that even such a place as Calcutta has become a holy place of pilgrimage. This year I was fortunate to visit the Rādhā-Govinda Mandira where you worshiped in your childhood. I also saw the Scottish Churches’ College, where you excelled in Sanskrit studies. And I was especially blessed to visit the house on Ultadanga Road, where you met your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. I pray that I can follow in your footsteps and be dedicated to your service, as you are to that of your Guru Mahārāja. There are so many places of your pastimes, and all are important to the world. It is to the great credit of your followers that everywhere your rooms are being kept very nicely. Everyone who visits them is able to feel your presence and become inspired.

You told us of the power of the chaste wife who was able to stop the sunrise. If just being faithful to an ordinary husband can be so wonderful, I cannot imagine the value of being faithful to the bona fide spiritual master. There are so many allurements, many of them seemingly spiritual, which might deviate us from simple faithfulness to your service. Please keep us on the right path. A devotee should be simple. That does not mean stupid, but not complicated. Let me simply be your faithful follower, like a dog to his master.

As a perfect follower of *guru, śāstra* and *sādhu,* you gave us the worship of both Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You gave us the teachings of the Gosvāmīs, both by extracting the essential teachings from their books and writing them in your books, and by your personal example. Rūpa Gosvāmī prayed to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, *namo mahā-vadānyāya kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāyate:* “You are the most magnanimous, for You are giving what no other *avatāra* has given—*kṛṣṇa-prema,* pure love for Kṛṣṇa.” It follows that the devotees of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu must also be magnanimous. The dictionary defines magnanimous as follows: *mag-nan-i-mous, adj.*  
1\. generous in forgiving an insult or injury; free from pettiness.  
2\. showing noble sensibility; high-minded.

These are qualities of a devotee. High-minded ultimately means thinking of Kṛṣṇa. You always talk and write about Kṛṣṇa, so obviously you are always thinking of Kṛṣṇa. Once, during a lecture in Montreal, one man began insulting you even while you were speaking. You tolerated him and stopped the devotees from chastising him. Afterwards you explained that you must have insulted him in a past life, and therefore he was now insulting you. This is your noble sensibility. No matter what happens, you see that Kṛṣṇa is there. You are surely not petty-minded, for you only think of the greatest and most important thing, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. A *mahātmā* is broad-minded, always thinking of the welfare of everyone.

Śrīnivāsa Ācārya describes that the six Gosvāmīs were always in the mood of the *gopīs*. As their perfect representative, you are also in that mood. When Mahārāja Pratāparudra met Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he recited the prayers of the *gopīs* from the Chapter 31 of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* Tenth Canto. Eventually he recited this verse:

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁ śrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

At this point Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu jumped up and embraced him, shouting, “You are the most munificent! You are the most munificent!” In your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* You translate this verse as follows: “My Lord, the nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities are the life and soul of those who are always aggrieved in this material world. These narrations are transmitted by exalted personalities, and they eradicate all sinful reactions. Whoever hears these narrations attains all good fortune. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Those who spread the message of Godhead are certainly the most munificent welfare workers.” (*Bhāg.* 10.31.9)

From the beginning you taught us that preaching was the highest welfare work. Your conviction in this mood is so strong that you have inspired thousands of followers to go on with the preaching work, particularly the distribution of your books. “Munificent” is defined as follows: *mu-nif-i-cent, adj.*  
characterized by or displaying great generosity

You said that we are not just begging but are giving the highest knowledge. You taught us to freely distribute the holy name by performing constant *hari-nāma* festivals. You taught us to freely distribute Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*.
