**Prabhupāda:** So? Why? Then the answer will be: “Why there shall not be existence?” First of all you answer this. If you question like that—“Why there is existence?”—then I shall inquire, “Why there shall not be existence?” Therefore the decision should be taken from the Absolute. Your question, my answer, will not solve. If you say, “Why there is existence?” I can ask you, “Why there shall not be existence?” And who will decide this?

The purpose is … that is experienced by every one of us, what is the purpose of life, what is the purpose, anything. That, everyone, we can understand very easily. The purpose is *ānanda*. Pleasure. That is the purpose. There is no difficulty to understand what is the purpose. The purpose is pleasure-seeking. Or purpose is pleasure. One who hasn’t got the pleasure, he’s seeking after it. That is the purpose. Purpose is *ānanda*. *Ānanda-mayo ’bhyāsāt*.

That is the *Vedānta-sūtra*. Everyone of us, seeking *ānanda*. The scientific knowledge, philosophy, or even driving the car or whatever you are doing—the purpose is *ānanda*. That is a common factor. Purpose is … Why I am eating palatable dishes? I can eat anything, but I am seeking that “This sort of foodstuff will please me.” That is *ānanda*.

**Guest (1):** That is driving force and motivation of most human activities. But the question, purpose, which Leibnitz was asking for, he was asking on higher plane, in abstraction.

**Prabhupāda:** Higher plane means you are seeking after pleasure, but that is being obstructed. That is your position. You are seeking pleasure, but it is not unobstructed. Therefore you are seeking higher, where there is no obstruction. Pleasure is the purpose, but when you speak of higher plane, that means you are experiencing obstruction in getting pleasure. So you are seeking a platform where there is no obstruction. But the purpose is the same.

Note: As Śrīla Prabhupāda so often said, “Variety is the mother of enjoyment.” That is why anything and everything is displayed in this world. Unfortunately, the variety displayed on the lower or material plane of existence is “a falsehood always changing but never getting near the truth.” Material variety is *nirviśeṣa,* without quality, because it is temporary. The varieties of this cosmos sooner or later collapse into chaos. Attempting to adjust consciousness to the *nirviśeṣa* position by intellectual abstraction will not bring one to the higher plane. To ascend to the higher plane, the idea of “I am the original enjoyer” must be given up, not the idea of enjoyment itself. On the lower plane, the individual soul posits himself as the original enjoyer. But he is not original. The only obstruction to the happiness of the soul is his failure to admit that because he is not the origin of the variety he is attracted to enjoy, he is not free to do as he likes. Kṛṣṇa is the origin. Real enjoyment is therefore Kṛṣṇa-centered. On the higher plane the soul achieves eternal enjoyment of the endless variety of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental qualities.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, Lord Caitanya said to Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī that the purpose of Vedānta philosophy is very difficult for an ordinary person to understand. But, *ye sūtra-kartā, se yadi karaye vyākhyāna tabe sūtrera mūla artha lokera haya jñāna:* if the person who wrote *Vedānta-sūtra* explains it, its original meaning can be understood by the people in general. The intention of my offering is to establish that you are, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the *sūtra-kartā,* Śrīla Vyāsadeva, whom we worship on this holy day of your merciful appearance in this world. I know this is true because, though I am an ordinary fallen soul, by hearing your explanations of the Vedic scriptures a glimmer of attraction to real philosophy has taken root in my heart. Now I prostrate myself in the dust of your lotus feet and humbly submit before you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day a request. As a wise man declared, “To be a philosopher is not merely to have subtle thoughts, but so to love wisdom as to live according to its dictates.” Today I pray for such love of this wisdom imparted by you throughout the world, for this wisdom is the Lord Himself (*jñānaṁ jñeyaṁ jñāna-gamyam*). Please make manifest this transcendental knowledge in my soul, thoughts, words, and deeds, life after life. For then I may be sure to serve you with fixed attention, eternally.

Your ever-insignificant, ever-aspiring servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

tāhā pravartāilā tumi,—ei ta ’pramāṇa’  
kṛṣṇa-śakti dhara tumi,—ithe nāhi āna

**“The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement. You have spread the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore it is evident that You have been empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. There is no question about it.”**

Purport: “The true *ācārya,* the spiritual master of the entire world, must be considered an incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. Indeed, He is personally embracing Kṛṣṇa. He is therefore the spiritual master of all the *varṇas …* and all the *āśramas*. … Since he is understood to be the most advanced devotee, he is called Paramahaṁsa Ṭhākura” (Cc. *Antya* 7.11–12).

Śrī Advaita Ācārya appeared some time before Lord Caitanya. He found the world devoid of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. Seeing everyone engaged in materialistic activity, the Ācārya felt great compassion and began to consider what he could do for the people’s benefit. He thought, “If Śrī Kṛṣṇa were to appear as an incarnation, He Himself could preach devotion by His personal example. In this Age of Kali there is no religion other than the chanting of the holy name of the Lord, but how in this age will the Lord appear as an incarnation? I shall worship Kṛṣṇa in a purified state of mind. I shall constantly petition Him in humbleness. My name, ‘Advaita,’ will be fitting if I am able to induce Kṛṣṇa to inaugurate the movement of the chanting of the holy name’” (Cc. *Ādi* 3.99–102).

At the same time, Lord Kṛṣṇa himself was thinking thus: “‘For a long time I have not bestowed unalloyed loving service to Me upon the inhabitants of the world. Without such loving attachment, the existence of the material world is useless. … I shall personally inaugurate the religion of the age—*nāma-saṅkīrtana,* the congregational chanting of the holy name. I shall make the world dance in ecstasy, realizing the four mellows of loving devotional service’” (Cc.. *Ādi* 3.14-19).

Thus the Lord decided to advent disguised as His own devotee and teach pure devotional service by practicing it Himself. Accompanied by His associates, servants, weapons, and confidential companions, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, appeared in Nadia some five hundred years ago. The Lord thought, “‘My name is Viśvambhara, “one who maintains the entire universe.” Its meaning will be actualized if I can fill the whole universe with love of Godhead.’ Thinking in this way, He accepted the duty of a planter and began to grow a garden in Navadvīpa. Thus the Lord brought the desire tree of devotional service to this earth and became its gardener. He sowed the seed and sprinkled upon it the water of His will. … As a big fig tree bears fruits all over its body, each part of the tree of devotional service bore fruit. … All the wealth in the three worlds cannot equal the value of one such nectarean fruit of devotional service. The transcendental gardener, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, distributed handful after handful of fruit in all directions, and when the poor, hungry people ate the fruit, the gardener smiled with great pleasure” (Cc. *Ādi* 9.7–9, 25, 28, 30).

So eager was the Lord to distribute the fruits of love of God that He expressed transcendental anxiety, feeling that these fruits would not be distributed widely enough. He said, “‘I am the only gardener. How many places can I go? How many fruits can I pick and distribute? It would certainly be a very laborious task to pick the fruits and distribute them alone, and still I suspect that some would receive them and others would not. Therefore I order every man within this universe to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and distribute it everywhere. … If the fruits are distributed all over the world, My reputation as a pious man will be known everywhere, and thus all people will glorify My name with great pleasure’” (Cc. *Ādi* 9.34–36, 40).

Hearing the Lord’s words, His followers worked with unlimited enthusiasm. Under the Lord’s direction, they broke open the storehouse of love of God and distributed the fruits without consideration of who was fit or unfit to receive them. They personally ate the fruit and induced whomever they met to do the same. But they met with opposition. Kali-yuga was progressing. Time passed, and the number of conditioned souls evading the Lord’s net of mercy increased. As the nineteenth century headed to a close, the brilliant moonbeams of Lord Caitanya’s mercy seemed all but eclipsed by the sinister Kali. Numerous *sahajiyā* groups proliferated, shadows of the glorious *saṅkīrtana* parties of the past. Only the appearance of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura saved the situation. He re-established Lord Caitanya’s movement, then placed it in the hands of his faithful son Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, who expanded it to every corner of India.

Alas! No sooner had these two empowered persons left this earthly world than the mission again faltered. Their efforts to organize a dynamic movement were nearly undone. Instead of working unitedly, their followers split into factions and the movement lost its momentum. Kali’s followers rejoiced. Having survived two world wars, they seized control of the world and plundered her resources. Even India, the bastion of religion, discarded her spiritual destiny in favor of temporal objectives. As the materialists gained strength, the devotees seemed to lose theirs. The unconquerable forward march of Śrīla Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s legions ground to a halt, jeopardizing the legacy of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda. Timely ringing of the temple bells and a full belly became the new measure of success. Lord Caitanya’s prophecy of a worldwide *saṅkīrtana* movement seemed no more than a wishful dream, the fanciful words of some medieval poet.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu sat amongst His closest associates, appearing like the full moon amidst a starry firmament. He had stopped their dance of ecstasy to consider the plight of His *saṅkīrtana* movement. They heard Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī’s report of their mission’s stagnation. The Lord’s eyes welled with tears. He had come to benefit the whole world, not just to change a few. “You have made so many disciples,” Śrīla Bhaktivinoda interjected. “Will none dare cross the ocean?” “A few have, but they returned unsuccessful. The Yavanas are in control everywhere, even in India.” Tears washed the Lord’s golden cheeks. He turned to Nāmācārya Haridāsa Ṭhākura: “My dear Haridāsa, what about your promise? You said the holy name would deliver everyone. Now it seems not even a semblance of the holy name remains?” Haridāsa stared downcast as all the Lord’s associates felt the burden of His concern.

Haridāsa Ṭhākura finally took courage: “My Lord, You know everything, so what can we say? Although You are the master of all, You willingly place Yourself under Your Yogamāyā potency, who causes forgetfulness. With this apparent human frailty You draw us ever closer. My dear Lord, everyone knows You recently emptied the universe by delivering all the conditioned souls; then again You filled it up. If You like, You can easily deliver the conditioned souls time after time. But You prefer to have Your devotee take the credit by performing this miraculous task. My Lord, may I therefore remind You that some time ago, knowing Kali’s influence would spread, You dispatched Your dearest devotee, the servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī, to earth. He is busy preparing himself at that very place where Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī once performed his *bhajana*. My Lord, surely You know this, and so does Rūpa Gosvāmī. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, who predicted his coming, and of course his son Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, are also well aware. Perhaps due to humility none will remind You. My Lord, may Your anxiety be vanquished, for the deliverance of the world is near at hand.”

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu turned to Rūpa Gosvāmī: “What do you know of this?” “My Lord, what Haridāsa Ṭhākura has said is true. He is speaking of Śrī Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami, the humble servant and true follower of Śrīmad Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. Night and day he tirelessly translates *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* into the language of the Yavanas.” The Lord’s imploring glance induced Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī to finally speak: “He is following the instruction I gave him at our first meeting. I told him to preach Your Lordship’s message all over the world. I repeated this to him at Rādhā-kuṇḍa ten years later and reconfirmed it by letter just before my departure. That order is now ripe for fulfillment. But my Lord knows all of this, for it is You who actually made all the arrangements. It was You who arranged his birth in a pure, aristocratic Vaiṣṇava family. By the time You sent him to me, his father Gaura Mohan De had already impressed upon him all the essential elements of Your mission. I only needed to stress the importance of publishing literature and to command him to spread Your movement worldwide.

“Then Your Lordship did what You alone can do: though fortune and happiness were his destiny, You made his business fail and his family attachments evaporate. Sensing your Lordship’s intention, I appeared repeatedly in his dreams and ordered him to take *sannyāsa*. Now humanity is his family and its resources are his inherited wealth. He is fearlessly reposed at Your lotus feet and awaits Your final command.”

Waves of enthusiasm swept away the Lord’s anxiety. He turned to Lord Nityānanda and embraced Him strongly. Śrī Nityānanda Rāma at once jumped up and began to dance so forcefully that the ground beneath His lotus feet shook. Others rose to join, but Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu bade them be patient while He calmed His brother’s ecstasy. When Lord Nityānanda again became peaceful, Lord Caitanya asked Him to sit beside Him. Then, looking at His circle of intimate associates, the Lord requested each to bestow their unconditional mercy upon the best servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī.

Lord Caitanya called first upon Śrī Advaita Ācārya, saying that the Ācārya was such a great personality that Lord Kṛṣṇa would surely fulfill His prayers. The exalted Śrī Advaita, the incarnation of both Lord Mahā-Viṣṇu and Lord Śiva and the shelter for all conditioned souls, offered the first benediction: “My Lord, since You are not scheduled to appear again until Brahmā’s next day, countless *jīvas* will have to suffer endless births awaiting Your next appearance. I cannot bear this. Therefore I pray that You now empower Sarasvatī Gosvāmī’s able disciple to deliver them on Your behalf.”

All the assembled devotees roared their approval. The Lord looked lovingly at Śrī Gadādhara Paṇḍita, His personified pleasure potency. Śrī Gadādhara responded appropriately: “Who can understand the Lord’s and His devotees’ reciprocal exchanges? When old age prevented my worship of beloved Gopīnātha, He sat down to receive my garlands and other services. Those who claim the Deity is stone are themselves stone-hearted. May my Lord melt the hearts of those stonelike *jīvas* by Your loving dealings with Śrī Abhay Charanaravinda.”

Then the Lord turned to Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura, the incarnation of Śrī Nārada Muni. Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura took his cue and pronounced, “As You have enabled me to travel everywhere to preach Your glories, please give him this same facility. Give him the key to every city of the world and to each and every heart that dwells therein.” The circle of confidential associates beamed with joy.

“Bhaṭṭācārya, you are expert in the six philosophies. What boon will you grant?” “That he drive impersonalism and voidism from the face of this earth,” Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya confidently replied, drawing joyous approval from all. Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya offered his blessings next: “May You speak through his lips as You spoke through mine. May You allow him to reveal the most confidential truth concerning the spontaneous devotional service of the residents of Vṛndāvana.” The Lord turned to Ṭhākura Haridāsa. “Nāmācārya, Your blessing is most important of all. What boon will you bestow on my fearless servitor?” Haridāsa Ṭhākura replied without hesitation, “The power to give Your pure holy name to everyone, no matter how lowly or fallen they may be.” Loud chants of “Haribol! Haribol!” soared to the heavens.

Lord Caitanya surveyed His dearest followers, the six Gosvāmīs. Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī was first to speak: “He will carry Vṛndāvana-dhāma wherever he goes. He will establish the standard behavior of a Vaiṣṇava by his words and deeds.” Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī promised, “His *saṅkīrtana* activities will churn the ocean of *bhakti,* producing a nectarean beverage which will intoxicate the entire world.” Śrīla Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī said, “*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is our life and soul. We never tire of hearing its narration. When I recite the *Bhāgavatam* Śrī Rūpa and Śrī Sanātana derive such pleasure that they insist I sing every verse in three different tunes. Therefore when Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami causes the *Bhāgavatam* to be sung in every language of the world, who can imagine how much pleasure it will give the Lord and His associates?” Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī said, “My Lord, You saved me from the dark well of household life, but Your departure left me burning in the fire of separation. Since You also delivered Bhaktivedanta Swami in much the same way, may that same bittersweet mood of separation for Your lotus feet always burn within his heart as well.” Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī said, “His books will establish the philosophy of *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva* as the pre-eminent philosophical position.” Śrīla Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī said, “He will establish the worship of Śrī Vigraha on every continent, in every nation, and in every town and village of the world.”

After hearing from the six Gosvāmīs, the Lord turned to the best of poets. Śrī Kavirāja Gosvāmī said, “May he arrange to have Your Lordships pastimes, *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* glorified in every human language of the world.” Lord Caitanya looked at the golden form of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, the embodiment of His *prema*. “Narottama, what blessing will you bestow?” Ṭhākura Narottama vowed, “Love for Your lotus feet and for the lotus feet of the divine couple will color his thoughts, words, and deeds. He will spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement with the same energy with which You empowered Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Śyāmānanda Prabhu, and myself.”

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura was seated by his side. “Abhay Charanaravinda has been studying my Tenth Canto *Bhāgavatam* commentary for decades, and the wonderful pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa are constantly appearing in his heart. May his presentation of these pastimes for the general populace be so suitable that it removes the disease of lust which now affects their hearts.” Śrīla Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa said, “As Lord Govindadeva dictated my *Vedānta-sūtra* commentary, may the Lord similarly sit by his side and dictate his Bhaktivedanta purports.”

Then Jagannātha dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja ensured that Sarasvatī Gosvāmī’s disciple would always maintain the infinite humility required to serve the Vaiṣṇavas. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, who had previously rescued the mission single-handedly, prayed that his great-granddisciple be granted the strength and skill to organize the *saṅkīrtana* movement on a worldwide scale. Śrīla Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja blessed his foremost granddisciple with his own spirit of renunciation, born out of his unlimited love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He blessed him to remain detached even amidst unlimited opulence.

At last, after everyone had spoken, Lord Caitanya turned to Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu. “You are my elder brother, and I fully depend upon you. You are the spiritual master of all the worlds and the upholder of all the universes. The blessings which these confidential associates have bestowed are beyond any mortal person’s ability to hold. Never before has such an assembly of personalities bestowed such unlimited blessings upon a single person. Now I am asking You to give the servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī the required strength to sustain these blessings, for without Your empowerment, it will be impossible.”

Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu smiled broadly, then nodded His head affirmatively, drawing a great roar of “Haribol! Haribol!” from everyone. When they had quieted, He spoke in a deep voice: “I will infuse his mission with My own personal energy. I will personally support his temples, *āśramas,* conveyances—in fact every aspect of his and his followers’ mission. Due to My presence, nothing will check the progress of this movement.” The joyous response to Śrī Nityānanda’s decree was deafening. The august assembly appeared to have lost their gravity. As they tried to estimate the effect of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy, their minds became boggled. Some cried, others laughed, and pandemonium would have broken out had Lord Caitanya not suddenly noticed Śrī Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī. “Oh? Have you escaped without giving a blessing?” The Lord smiled at His secretary’s silence. “Come now!” He said. “Tell us, what will it be?”

Śrī Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, whose transcendental intelligence was no less than that of the Lord Himself, would not be drawn in. “When Nityānanda Prabhu has made such a promise,” he said “there is nothing more for anyone to do. No, I am not holding back, but someone amongst us is.” Everyone looked puzzled, for they had all given boons; they had no idea who he was referring to. Then at last Advaita Ācārya understood. “My Lord, You have proven once again Your supreme intelligence. That great person Svarūpa Dāmodara is referring to is none other then You. You have made us each give our blessings—all, that is, except for Yourself! What value are our blessings if You don’t add Yours?” Everyone waited with bated breath to hear the Lord’s response. The Lord remained silent, contemplating the Ācārya’s words.

Then at last, He who is ever bound to His devotee’s words, soberly declared, “So be it! I shall personally enter the heart of my dearest devotee and unfailingly guide his every thought, word, and deed. My mission to bestow *prema-bhakti* trough the *saṅkīrtana* movement will thus be fulfilled.” With this promise He sealed destiny. It was more than the devotees could tolerate. Lead by the indomitable Lord Nityānanda, they sprang up with unbounded joy and began a performance of *saṅkīrtana* whose vibrations reached the far corners of the universe. Accompanied by Their associates, servants, and confidential companions, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Nityānanda danced with such abandon that the storehouse of love of God broke open and the nectar-liquid of *prema-bhakti* once again began to flood the universe.

The ship *Jaladuta,* tossed by the Atlantic’s waves, seemed to sway to the rhythmic *kīrtana* of Lord Gaurāṅga and His associates. Onboard, Śrī Śrīmad Abhay Charan Bhaktivedanta Swami, the recipient of the rarest mercy ever bestowed, wrote the following poem:

“Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who is very dear to Lord Gaurāṅga, the son of Mother Śacī , is unparalleled in his service to the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. He is the great, saintly spiritual master who bestows intense devotion to Kṛṣṇa at different places throughout the world.

“By his strong desire, the holy name of Lord Gaurāṅga will spread throughout all the countries of the Western world. In all the cities, towns and villages on the earth, from all the oceans, seas, rivers, and springs, everyone will chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

“As the vast mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu conquers all directions, a flood of transcendental ecstasy will certainly cover the land. When all the sinful, miserable living entities become happy, the Vaiṣṇavas’ desire is then fulfilled.

“Although my Guru Mahārāja ordered me to accomplish this mission, I am not worthy or fit to do it. I am very fallen and insignificant. Therefore, O Lord, now I am begging for Your mercy so that I may become worthy, for You are the wisest and most experienced of all.”

The *kīrtana* of Lord Gaurāṅga and His associates continued on and on without end. The Jaladuta plied forward until it reached Boston harbor. As Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami’s lotus feet touched Western soil for the first time, he prayed:

“O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.”

Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You are always in my thoughts as my protector and master. My obsession is to follow you, and although it is difficult for me to obey you, my humble desire is to serve you. In spite of my incompetence, I propose to deepen my comprehension of your message and to improve my behavior. Being loyal to you is my greatest satisfaction. My greatest happiness is to be your disciple. To help you in your preaching mission, and to sacrifice my life in order to please you, is my greatest treasure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is you who encourage me to go on living. It is you who save my life from moment to moment, accepting my service to you though I am the most useless member of your movement. I do not deserve you. I lack sincerity, determination, and purity of intent. And though I acknowledge you as my only master, I still don’t know if I tell you this because it is advantageous to me, or because I am convinced.

Being part of your movement is the greatest privilege that anyone can imagine. The International Society for Krishna Consciousness is made up of an ever-growing number of sincere devotees, great souls, knowers of the Absolute Truth, who work tirelessly for the spiritual well being of all humanity.

Nowadays, due to the vanity with which he uses it, man is the victim of the so-called progress of his own so-called knowledge. Neither the rules and regulations of the educational system nor the norms of our age contribute to the development of the better qualities of the human species. Corruption has invaded all areas of the world, putting all governmental, civil, and religious institutions in crisis. Cheating is the norm. As most people do not accept their own deficiencies, they care little for any renovation of consciousness. Victims of the reigning misinformation, humanity is devoid of the proper vision of their true duty and real destiny. When a person doesn’t rely on the necessary means for spiritual development, he knows only frustration, pain, and misery. Not following God’s laws, he is a prisoner of his own karma.

As you have taught us, the spiritual evolution of consciousness requires a sustained attitude of material renunciation, as well as a sincere submission to God’s will. As long as one does not wipe out one’s possessive desires, one continues to be bound by the law of karma. Definitely, the movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is a war declared against these miserable conditions of materialistic life. You are the incarnation of such a movement.

We recognize that you are the faithful spokesman for the Supreme Absolute Truth. Unlike the imitator who simply repeats the ideas and words of others, without having understood them, you are illuminated and honest. Your genuine greatness as spiritual master is that you guide everyone on the path that you yourself have walked. For those, like myself, who are trapped in the net of material desires, your exemplary life constitutes the necessary inspiration to achieve spiritual perfection. The goal of human life is spiritual fulfillment, and this is easily obtainable for those who take refuge of your sage teachings.

Your books emanate a light that makes the sublime world visible, and transcendence fascinating. Through your incomparable books the entire world can now discover the true essence and identity of God. Your Bhaktivedanta purports can save the sincere souls from the labyrinth of temporal desires and identifications. The uncontested purity and authority of your arguments free us from the prison of doubt, and liberate the spirit from being confined there. The transcendental magic of your preaching transforms undeveloped caterpillars like me into joyous butterflies that incessantly spin from the attractive aroma of the presence of Kṛṣṇa that is perceived in your heart.

With the generosity that so characterizes you, you make flow into the heart of the seriously inquisitive person the ineffable knowledge that has been revealed to you. Without such knowledge, human life is devoid of feeling. The content of your teachings enobles even great sages. The non-Vaiṣṇavas do not know that if knowledge is not translated into devotional works about Kṛṣṇa, then the reason for its being is never achieved, and this so-called knowledge only confuses, instead of helping. Fortunately for me, you are always near to help me in the form of your incomparable books and recordings. Whoever wishes to understand the depth of the transcendental nature of his own self, along with that of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, has only to read your books and hear your classes. Your books are like written symphonies that delight and transport one to the spiritual world whenever one listens to them. Whoever wishes to preach with success has only to faithfully transmit what you say in them.

By your mercy the world can now rely upon talented and admirable preachers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, who bless the world with your transcendental revelations. The history of your movement, ISKCON, is a history of unequaled triumphs, thanks to your perfect leadership as the unequaled *ācārya*. Every year there are new advances with respect to the propagation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the world, thanks to the presence of your books. The seeds that you planted fructify abundantly, and they are even planting new seeds themselves. By your grace the fertility of preaching fields grow, and their range extends to the five continents. Your teachings penetrate the sincere hearts, which grow in knowledge and spiritual maturity. New generations of devotees acclaim and adore you. And although some of your followers are fatally obsessed with inquiring after their spiritual status quo, the majority innocently continue serving your lotus feet, as in the old days.

You are the uncontested Pole Star that continues to spiritually orient the masses. The universe of ISKCON expands unlimitedly in spite of the inevitable torments of speculation and prejudice. The fact is that your movement’s spiritual atmosphere is culturally inundating planet Earth. Everything now appears ready to carry out your prediction and multiply your miracles. The celebration of your Centennial represents the start of a new era in the bosom of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, and in the history of the world. In time, everyone will realize that your appearance on earth has marked a turning point in the spiritual evolution of its inhabitants.

Happy to be your aspiring servant,  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances to your lotus feet.

Your feet are the goal of all of my devotional service. They are like rare and precious *cintāmaṇi* gems, and their service bestows love for Lord Kṛṣṇa. I take shelter of your lotus feet.

Again we are blessed to gather for your glorification. It is only fitting, for there is no other personality who can compare to you. You have rendered the most glorious service to your divine master and to our entire *paramparā.* You have strictly followed the orders and instructions of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and have given the entire world the treasure of *kṛṣṇa-prema* through the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement. You have taught the most confidential knowledge of the Vedic literatures by showing us how to absorb our minds in thought of Kṛṣṇa.

To this very day you are spreading the waves of the ocean of love of God. Everyone is taking the nectar of the Lord's sweet names and diving deeply into that ocean. This is all going on by your desire and mercy. In Africa, Russia, Muslim countries, China—throughout the entire world—people are relishing sweet *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana,* dancing in ecstasy, tasting the Lord’s *prasādam*, and reading your books. By this sublime process you are purifying all the inhabitants of the earth and preparing their hearts for their return to the spiritual world. You are the true *ācārya*.

So I am approaching you again on this most holy of days, as I have done so many times before. I am coming in the traditional role of a beggar. I am begging for your mercy. I know that I am shameless, but for me there is no other hope. Please let your mercy fall on me, and by doing so, please allow me to become like you.

You always have given such good examples. We all know the example of the iron rod in the fire. It illustrates the concept of being one with, or absorbed in, an object, not in some impersonal sense, but by being completely imbued with the same mood as someone else. You coined the expression “Krishnized.” Please let me become “Prabhupādized,” and by so doing develop a fraction of your wonderful qualities.

I am begging that by my serving you, you will remove my hundreds and millions of disqualifications. Allow me simply to be absorbed in you. Let me preach as you have preached, and let me serve the Vaiṣṇava community in the way you have served the whole world. Let me exhibit tolerance, kindness, and forgiveness in the way you have shown. Allow me to be strict and uncompromising as you have shown. Let me be fair, kind, and truly merciful as you have shown. Please allow me to develop one drop of genuine humility—not that I only smile and fold my palms. By my serving you, please let me understand that I really am a fallen soul and I am really not a Vaiṣṇava. Then let all my actions, words, and deeds correspond to that realization.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your spiritual force please enter my heart and drive away the lust that is burning there. You are the Lord’s most precious, dear, and empowered associate. You have His *śakti.* Please destroy this demon that keeps me from becoming a real devotee.

I would like to be exactly as you are, but this desire is like a child’s crying to have the moon. Since I have rendered you some service, these very big hopes have come to my heart. Due to my fallen position and due to the exalted position of these hopes and desires, I am also feeling a burning pain in my heart. The only medicine for that pain is exclusive surrender to your lotus feet and your causelessly bestowing your mercy.

I officially waved the white flag of surrender a quarter of a century ago. Now let me fully and truly and faithfully surrender to your mission and to your lotus feet. I will preach and serve your glorious mission until the last breath of my life. I will give my life for bringing others to the path of *bhakti* and training them in this science. I will increase the quality of all of my devotional practices. But again, you are my actual goal, and your pleasure is the essence of all these activities. So that I may attempt to serve and please you, I beg you to allow your causeless mercy to descend upon me.

Some feel they know my shameless nature, but the fact is that you know better than all. Therefore I beg you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: Please excuse me for all of my mistakes and please disregard my shameless nature. Let me become a real devotee and take me into your service for all of eternity. I have no other place to go.

Begging to become your servant,  
Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your merciful lotus feet.

I thank you for again keeping me engaged in your service for another year, and thank you for giving me the opportunity to do more service in the future. What you have given us, and what you continue to give us, is the most valuable thing: the priceless opportunity to achieve pure devotional service.

Pure devotional service is not a cheap thing, and you were particularly careful to show that through your own personal example. You worked tirelessly to please your spiritual master and the Supreme Personality of Godhead, preaching their message to whomever you met.

We have now inherited that mission, and we have also inherited the great boon it can give: access to the lotus feet of the Lord.By continuing to preach up to the last moments of your stay with us, you showed us very clearly what the process is. If we dedicate our lives to preaching what you have told us, we will help people to come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we will purify our hearts of all contamination, and we will be able to endear ourselves to the Lord.

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ*

*na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*  
*bhavitā na ca me tasmād anyaḥ priyataro bhuvi*

**“For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear." (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **18.68–69)**

As you explained many times, even the conditioned souls are fallen devotees, so you applied this verse to all types of preaching. We can preach among the devotees and nondevotees, and by doing this we will earn the right to the great gift of pure love of God. And all this is simply by your divine grace.

So thank you again and again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for making everything so clear. You ensured that there would be no room for doubt as to what is to be done and what is not to he done. All we have to do is submissively accept our positions as your servants and dedicate our lives to fulfilling your desire and that of Lord Caitanya:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**Lord Caitanya said, “In as many villages and towns as there are on the surface of the globe, My holy name \[the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mantra*****\] will be preached.”**

You commented on this verse, “The devotees of Lord Caitanya must preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in every village and town in the world. That will satisfy the Lord. It is not that one should act whimsically for his own personal satisfaction. This order comes down through the *paramparā* system, and the spiritual master presents these orders to the disciple so that he can spread the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It is the duty of every disciple to carry out the order of the bona fide spiritual master and spread Lord Caitanya’s message all over the world.”

*Trying to become your eternal servant,*  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At this auspicious time I remember my early encounters with you in 1968, the time I was attending high school. I first saw you at 26 Second Avenue in April of that year. After a week, you went to Boston. I continued coming to the temple every other day. Another week passed. On Friday, May 5, after school recessed for the weekend, I took the train to New York with my younger brother. As we traversed the last block before the temple, I could see devotees coming out of the temple carrying boxes and loading them into an old, dilapidated green bus. My heart throbbed frantically and I almost fainted. I quickly dashed the last block to the front of the temple and exclaimed to the busy devotees: “Where are you moving to?”

They looked at me with puzzled expressions and someone said, “We’re not moving. We’re just going to Boston for the weekend to see Swamiji. Why don’t you come with us.”

As a wave of relief swept over my visibly distraught body, I mustered a weak rejection: “But I have to go to school on Monday. And besides, my Mom will freak out if I don’t come home tonight.”

A devotee said, “Just tell her that you lost your wallet and that you’ll be back on Monday.”

I turned to my younger brother and said, “Well, do you want to go to Boston to see Swamiji?” In his easy-going way he said, “Yes.” I went into the temple to use the telephone. When my mother answered the phone, I said, “Mom, I lost my wallet with my ticket and all my money, but don’t worry. I’m staying with friends in New York City here. Oh, and I’ll be back in time for school on Monday.” Click! It was the first time in my life that I had done anything like that, but I wanted so much to go to Boston to see you.

My brother and I helped the devotees finish loading things on the bus, and then we boarded the bus to leave. It was only when I was going to sit down that I realized how bad the bus was. All the seats had been removed, leaving gaping holes in the thin metal floor. These allowed us to see the pavement and smell the exhaust of the bus as it rumbled down the road. The bus shook so much that I thought it would fall apart at any time. I tried to chant *japa,* but it was very difficult to concentrate with all the noise generated by the bus, as well as the exhaust fumes coming up through the floor. My ankles were getting sore from sitting on the hard metal. A devotee named Advaita noticed my discomfort and handed me a blanket and a small booklet. After sitting comfortably, I looked at the booklet. It was entitled *Easy Journey to Other Planets*. This was your original edition, printed in India before coming to the West. I read the booklet from cover to cover. I was impressed by your scientific presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It was this small booklet which convinced me that I should dedicate my life to the practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Kṛṣṇa consciousness was not a Hindu thing, or some weird concoction; it was the scientific process of God realization. For the rest of the trip I chanted *japa* and meditated on your picture in the booklet. Sporting a pleasant smile, you looked very fresh, and your wire-rim glasses gave you a scholarly appearance. I passed my time wondering about how I could become your full-time disciple.

After some hours we reached the Boston temple, which was just a storefront on Glenville Avenue. Shortly after we arrived, one of your servants, Devānanda dāsa, asked me if I could break up some orange crates for firewood. You were going to give several disciples *brāhmaṇa* initiation on Saturday. This was the first time you were giving disciples initiation as *brāhmaṇas*. I hacked at the wood with a cleaver as quickly as I could, for the sun was slowly sinking toward the horizon. It did not take long to reduce the crates to a pile of kindling wood. Finishing that, I went into the temple. Everyone was excited. The whole storefront temple was bustling with activity. Devotees were in the kitchen cooking for the feast that would be served after the initiation. The temple’s air was thick with the fragrance of aromatic spices, and sometimes it was hard to breathe due to the stinging sensation caused by pungent spices fried in ghee. Many of the devotees present I recognized from when you were at the temple in New York City. The atmosphere was stimulating and festive. When we finally lay down to take rest that night, I could hardly sleep in anticipation of the next day’s events.

After the devotees conducted the usual morning program, Pradyumna and several others prepared everything for the fire sacrifice. There was a low-lying dais piled with assorted pillows for you to sit on, and in front of it was a mound of bricks and sand. When it was time for the ceremony to begin, you arrived in a dark-colored car. One devotee who was waiting outside the temple spied your bright saffron robes in the approaching car and shouted out, “Swamiji’s coming!” When the car reached the front of the temple, a devotee opened the car door for you, and you got out. We all bowed down, touching our heads to the sidewalk. Some devotees cried out “Swamiji!” others shouted “Hare Kṛṣṇa!” I was simply awestruck by your transcendental effulgence, which rivaled the morning sun, and was therefore unable to utter anything. Following you into the temple, we sat on the floor space before you. Those who were going to be initiated sat around the fire mound. I chose a spot in the back, on your left. I did not understand what was going on exactly. It was the first time I was witnessing an initiation, and this was the first time that *brāhmaṇa* initiation was being given to Westerners. I decided that I should simply attend as an observer. Actually, I thought that I might do something wrong or commit some offense and therefore kept my distance from the center of activity.

First you led *kīrtana,* which was extremely ecstatic, and then you began speaking. You mentioned that one professor was present. He was a young fellow—about twenty-six, it seemed—who had specialized in Bengali. You exchanged a few words with him in Bengali. You then began talking about *brāhmaṇa* initiation. You said one has to accept a spiritual master. One who is transcendentally situated can be a spiritual master. He must also know the conclusive truths of the Vedic literatures. One who is inquisitive about the highest perfectional stage of life should approach a spiritual master. You said that you were not forcing anyone to be your disciple. After some time, those who chanted would automatically approach you for initiation. Then you would request them to avoid the four sinful activities. Later, there could be a second initiation. You also mentioned that there was a third initiation for taking *sannyāsa*. You asked the professor if he was familiar with the *brāhmaṇa* caste in India—how a person became *brāhmaṇa* by birth in a *brāhmaṇa* family. You then said that this was rascaldom and that according to scripture a *brāhmaṇa* is determined by qualification. You then said that when someone becomes a Vaiṣṇava he is automatically *brāhmaṇa,* and even surpasses ordinary *brāhmaṇas*. You also asked the professor if he had read these things in the *Hari-bhakti-vilāsa* or *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. He had not, and so you quoted him some *ślokas* in Bengali. I remember my surprise that you were talking with a complete stranger, discussing such issues as *brāhmaṇa* initiation with quotes from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

With deft hand strokes, you sprinkled colored rice flour over the sand mound to create a criss-cross pattern, over which you lit the sacrificial fire. You fed the fire with ghee-soaked wood until it was blazing ferociously, casting its dancing, brilliant light upon the onlookers’ faces. You wore a beautiful garland as the master of ceremonies, and you looked very exceptional as the light of the fire rippled across your person. As thick, gray smoke billowed upward, bouncing across the ceiling, you began intoning Sanskrit prayers, which everyone repeated in unison. Each time you chanted “*svāhā!”* you poured ladlefuls of ghee into the flames. The audience tossed barley and sesame seeds toward the fire, most of which landed everywhere but in the fire. The fire responded by reaching higher and higher, leaping above the heads of everyone. Occasionally it spat out burnt sesame seeds. One hit me in the cheek, stinging me like an angry insect. I was glad it did not hit me in the eye. When you stopped reciting *mantras,* everyone stood up, and you directed the initiates to place their bananas in the fire. The devotees started chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and danced around the sacred fire. I refrained from doing so because I was embarrassed. Besides, I wanted to watch you as you sat on your *vyāsāsana,* eyes closed, playing *karatālas* rhythmically as you listened to the chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy names. Eventually the *kīrtana* ended. Thereafter *prasādam* was served. You directed the serving. This was my first feast. The temple room was full of dense smoke. I and others coughed frequently. But you breathed in deeply and announced that this smoke was purifying. I pondered how such a thing could be so, and then directed my full attention to the *prasādam*.

In the evening you returned to the temple, which was again decorated for a fire sacrifice. This time you initiated my dear Godbrother Ṛṣi Kumāra, who, being a high school student like myself, had already become my close friend. After this I knew what my goals were. I had to get initiated as your disciple, and someday come up to the standard of a *brāhmaṇa*.

The next morning you decided to take the devotees to the Commonwealth Pier, where you first landed in America. The mode of transportation was the very same green bus in which we had come from New York. Before you arrived from your apartment, we boarded the bus and sat down. Because the only seat was the driver’s, someone brought in a metal folding chair. After entering the bus and taking your seat, we departed for the pier. Experiencing the terrible condition of the bus, you remarked that it was just like an Indian bus. In our naivete, we took it as a compliment. While the bus was in motion, I was in constant anxiety for your well-being. I was afraid that you would fall off the flimsy chair. When we reached the destination, we disembarked from the bus. You then led us to Commonwealth Pier. As we walked to the end of the pier and then back, you talked about your arrival there in 1965. You also pointed out that one of the buildings that had been standing nearby had been torn down. You remarked, “Just see. In this country the *karmīs* are working very hard to build a big, big building. Then, after some time, someone comes along and knocks it down to build a bigger one.” I was amazed by your clear memory of so many details about the pier when you had landed.

After breakfast you gave a *darśana* in the apartment where you were staying. You began by quoting from the Second Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam—*how there was no need to work hard like camels and asses when the necessities of life were readily available from nature. You then pointed out that you had noticed so many things—shirts, trousers, coats, mattresses, and shoes—lying abandoned in the streets of American cities. Better to use our precious time for realizing God. After the philosophical discussion, you mentioned how Lord Caitanya had predicted that this movement would spread to every town and village of the world. You said you wanted some devotees to go to England, Germany, France, and Russia. I wondered why you did not also instruct them to study the necessary foreign languages before going. I also wondered, considering the size and assets of the movement, how it would be able to spread to every town and village. “Maybe Swamiji is just trying to encourage us to do the best we can in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness,” I thought. “He couldn’t mean that this movement would literally spread to every town and village.” At the time I could not see the potency which you bore, nor the utility of the movement you had founded. When you finished speaking, you bid the devotees farewell, and they began filing out of the room. I thought that since I was so new I should let the other devotees leave first. Thus it happened that I soon found myself in the room with only you and Brahmānanda. Your eyes alighted on me and you asked, “What is your name?” I thought, “Does he think I have an initiated name? Should I tell him that I’m not initiated? Should I tell him my *karmī* name?” With my mouth agape, I stood there stuttering, “Uh, uh uh.” Brahmānanda jabbed his elbow into my side and said, “His name’s Robert.” Prabhupāda replied, “Oh, he is very nice boy.” Hearing these words was the most wonderful thing in my life. Those simple words gave me the encouragement I needed to surrender myself as your disciple.

When a few well-directed words could have such a profound effect on one person, I cannot imagine the transformation possible through contact with the many volumes of carefully composed literatures which you created for turning the nondevotee population of the world into Vaiṣṇavas. Generally people are impressed by power. But you always insisted that if the power is great, how much greater is the source of the power. Kṛṣṇa is the all-powerful, but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the all-empowered. Only you were able to implement the necessary program to fulfill the prediction of Lord Caitanya. At this time I beg to assist you in that fulfillment in any way I can.

Your humble servant,  
Bhaktivaidurya Mādhava Mahārāja

## Bhakti Vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your unlimited mercy on the conditioned souls in this Age of Kali. You showered your mercy on the whole world without consideration of race, class, or creed.

Although during the manifested pastimes of Your Divine Grace it was not possible to enter Khasadeśa, you did not neglect its people. You visited Hong Kong, initiated the first Khasa devotee, and had the *Bhagavad-gītā* printed in the Khasa language. Then you assigned devotees to distribute the books and even instructed some of your leading *sannyāsīs* to go and preach there.

In your *praṇāma mantra* we glorify you for delivering the Western World from impersonalism and voidism, but we know that you are also preaching here to the people of Khasadeśa. Today you are preaching through the pages of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Your powerful purports destroy the arguments of all voidists and speculators and plant the seed of *bhakti* in their hearts.

Although our Society has been trying for many years to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Taiwan, we have never been successful. To date there is only one initiated devotee here, and the mass of people have never heard of Kṛṣṇa or the *mahā-mantra*. Consequently, last Sunday, the appearance day of Lord Rāmacandra, we canceled the regular Sunday program to visit the countryside *āśrama* of a prominent local “meditation society.” With the presence of their master and a recent change to warmer weather, a crowd of 10,000 was expected.

We have heard of your early days in New York when you were staying with Dr. Mishra and singing *bhajans* to his students. Also, when you were establishing our Society in India you participated in various gatherings of *sādhus,* accepting them as opportunities to preach and show how Kṛṣṇa consciousness can attract everyone. In 1977 you attended the Kumbha-melā, reminding the devotees that such events are not very important for us but provide a good opportunity to preach and distribute books.

So last Sunday we took advantage of the opportunity to distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the voidists and speculators. We taught them the *mahā-mantra* and then gave everyone *prasādam*. Most significant of all, we sold all our *Bhagavad-gītās,* more than a hundred copies, as well as many small books. We are hopeful that, by your causeless mercy, it won’t be long before Kṛṣṇa consciousness becomes accepted here.

We know that this will be very pleasing to Your Divine Grace, and to give pleasure to you is the goal of our life.

Your unworthy servant,  
Bhakti Vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda was always writing, speaking, and singing about Kṛṣṇa, remaining absorbed in His service in full ecstasy and realization. By his mercy, we also write, speak, and sing for Kṛṣṇa and engage in His and Prabhupāda’s service. On his order, we “do as he did.” But our writing, singing, and serving cannot compare to his, for Prabhupāda’s purity and realization far surpass ours.

Therefore for this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā I am offering a collection of Prabhupāda’s writings and sayings. Although Śrīla Prabhupāda spoke on a wide variety of subjects, all connected to Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I have chosen a topic particularly relevant at the present time: Śrīla Prabhupāda’s explanation of his own unique position.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was the ideal of a pure devotee. Although his achievements in Kṛṣṇa’s service were unprecedented and inimitable, he remained humble and gave the credit to his spiritual master. Still, considering our rascal tendency to be misled by bluffers and cheaters, Śrīla Prabhupāda sometimes delineated his actual position, that he is the special representative of Lord Caitanya, a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* and the true disciple of his *guru.* Just as Lord Caitanya sometimes behaved like a common man and sometimes manifested His Godly opulence (cf. Cc. *Antya* 8.93), so similarly Śrīla Prabhupāda sometimes clearly let us know that he was a special devotee sent by Lord Caitanya to spread His name all over the world.

As Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in his purport to Cc. *Madhya* 25.9, quoted below, by sincere study and appreciation of the personal characteristics of devotees we can understand who is actually authorized and especially empowered.

As Gaṅgā is offered to Gaṅgā, I offer the following quotes for the pleasure of Śrīla Prabhupāda and the enlightenment of his followers:

“If one saw the personal characteristics and activities of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, one would certainly be convinced that He was the Supreme Personality of Godhead. One can ascertain this by following in the footsteps of the śāstric injunctions. This sincere study and appreciation of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is also applicable to His authorized devotees, and it is clearly stated in the *Caitanya caritāmṛta* (*Antya-līlā*, 7.11):

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**‘In this Age of Kali, real religious propaganda should induce people to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra*****. This is possible for someone who is especially empowered by Kṛṣṇa. No one can do this without being especially favored by Kṛṣṇa.’**

“Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura comments in this regard in his *Anubhāṣya,* wherein he quotes a verse from the *Nārāyaṇa-saṁhitā: dvāparīyair janair viṣṇuḥ pañcarātrais tu kevalaiḥ/ kalau tu nāma-mātreṇa pūjyate bhagavān hariḥ.*

“‘In Dvāpara-yuga, devotees of Lord Viṣṇu and Kṛṣṇa rendered devotional service according to the principles of *Pañcarātra*. In this Age of Kali, the Supreme Personality of Godhead is worshipped simply by the chanting of His names.’ Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura then comments, ‘Without being empowered by the direct potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa to fulfill His desire and without being specifically favored by the Lord, no human being can become the spiritual master of the whole world. He certainly cannot succeed by mental concoction, which is not meant for devotees or religious people. Only an empowered personality can distribute the holy name of the Lord and enjoin all fallen souls to worship Kṛṣṇa. By distributing the holy name of the Lord, he cleanses the hearts of the most fallen people; therefore he extinguishes the blazing fire of the material world. Not only that, he broadcasts the shining brightness of Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence throughout the world. Such an *ācārya,* or spiritual master, should be considered nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa—that is, he should be considered the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency. Such a personality is *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha*—that is, he is always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Such a person is above the considerations of the *varṇāśrama* institution. He is the *guru* or spiritual master for the entire world, a devotee on the topmost platform, the *mahā-bhāgavata* stage, and a *paramahaṁsa-ṭhākura,* a spiritual form only fit to be addressed as *paramahaṁsa* or *ṭhākura*.’ Nonetheless, there are many people who are just like owls but never open their eyes to see the sunshine. These owlish personalities are inferior to the Māyāvādī *sannyāsīs,* who cannot see the brilliance of Kṛṣṇa’s favor upon the *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee. They are prepared to criticize the person engaged in distributing the holy name all over the world and following in the footsteps of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who wanted Kṛṣṇa consciousness preached in every town and city” (Cc. *Madhya* 25.9, purport).

“When King Pṛthu is described as an incarnation of Lord Viṣṇu, it should be understood that he is a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* part and parcel of Lord Viṣṇu, and is specifically empowered by Him. Any living being acting as the incarnation of Lord Viṣṇu is thus empowered by Lord Viṣṇu to preach the *bhakti* cult. Such a person can act like Lord Viṣṇu and defeat demons by arguments and preach the *bhakti* cult exactly according to the principles of *śāstra*. As indicated in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* whenever we find someone extraordinary preaching the *bhakti* cult, we should know that he is especially empowered by Lord Viṣṇu, or Lord Kṛṣṇa. As confirmed in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 7.11), *kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana:* one cannot explain the glories of the holy name of the Lord without being specifically empowered by Him. If one criticizes or finds fault with such an empowered personality, one is to be considered an offender against Lord Viṣṇu and is punishable. Even though such offenders may dress as Vaiṣṇavas with false *tilaka* and *mālā,* they are never forgiven by the Lord if they offend a pure Vaiṣṇava. There are many instances of this in the *śāstras*” (*Bhāg*. 4.19.37).

“A devotee, by his personal devotional service, can influence all the people of the whole world to become devotees of the Lord. If there is only one pure devotee in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he can change the total consciousness of the world into Kṛṣṇa consciousness” (*Bhāg.* 4.8.80 purport).

“Just as Kṛṣṇa does not take a step away from Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa’s devotee also does not like to leave Vṛndāvana. However, when he has to tend to Kṛṣṇa’s business, he leaves Vṛndāvana. After finishing his mission, a pure devotee returns home, back to Vṛndāvana, back to Godhead” (Cc. *Madhya* 13.156, purport).

“When a Vaiṣṇava comes, when God Himself comes, or when God’s sons or His very confidential devotees come, their only mission is to save sinful men who are suffering. They have knowledge of how to do this” (*Science of Self-realization,* p. 7).

**Translation:**

“O my worshipable Lord, because the seed of lusty desires, which is the root cause of material existence, is within the core of everyone’s heart, You have sent me to this material world to exhibit the symptoms of a pure devotee.”

**Purport:**

“The *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* has given considerable discussion about *nitya-siddha* and *sādhana-siddha* devotees. *Nitya-siddha* devotees come from Vaikuṇṭha to this material world to teach, by their personal example, how to become a devotee. The living entities in this material world can take lessons from such *nitya-siddha* devotees and thus become inclined to return home, back to Godhead. A *nitya-siddha* devotee comes from Vaikuṇṭha upon the order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and shows by his example how to become a pure devotee (*anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyam*). In spite of coming to this material world, the *nitya-siddha* devotee is never attracted by the allurements of material enjoyment. A perfect example is Prahlāda Mahārāja, who was a *nitya-siddha,* a *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee. Although Prahlāda was born in the family of Hiraṇyakaśipu, an atheist, he was never attached to any kind of materialistic enjoyment. Desiring to exhibit the symptoms of a pure devotee, the Lord tried to induce Prahlāda Mahārāja to take material benedictions, but Prahlāda Mahārāja did not accept them. On the contrary, by his personal example he showed the symptoms of a pure devotee. In other words, the Lord Himself has no desire to send His pure devotee to this material world, nor does a devotee have any material purpose in coming. When the Lord Himself appears as an incarnation within this material world, He is not allured by the material atmosphere, and He has nothing to do with material activity, yet by his example He teaches the common man how to become a devotee. Similarly, a devotee who comes here in accordance with the order of the Supreme Lord shows by his personal behavior how to become a pure devotee. A pure devotee, therefore, is a practical example for all living entities, including Lord Brahmā” (*Bhāg.* 7.10.3, purport).

“Just as a condemned person can be relieved by a special favor of the chief executive head, the president or king, so the condemned people of this Kali-yuga can be delivered only by the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself or a person especially empowered for this purpose” (Cc. *Ādi* 13.69, purport).

“Sometimes the Lord, as the child of Mother Yaśodā, requests His devotee for some food, as if He were very hungry. Sometimes He tells His devotee in a dream that His temple and His garden are now very old and that He cannot enjoy them very nicely. Thus He requests the devotee to repair them. Sometimes He is buried in the earth, and as if unable to come out Himself, He requests His devotee to rescue Him. Sometime He requests His devotee to preach His glories all over the world, although He alone is quite competent to perform this task. Even though the Supreme Personality of Godhead is endowed with all possessions and is self-sufficient, He depends on his devotees. Therefore the relationship of the Lord with his devotees is extremely confidential. Only the devotees can perceive how the Lord, although full in Himself, depends on His devotee for some particular work. This is explained in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (11.33), where the Lord tells Arjuna, *nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savya-sācin:* ‘O Arjuna, merely be an instrument in the fight.’ Lord Kṛṣṇa had the competence to win the Battle of Kurukṣetra, but nonetheless He induced His devotee Arjuna to fight and become the cause of victory. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was quite competent enough to spread His name and mission all over the world, but still He depended upon His devotee to do this work” (*Bhāg.* 6.19.5, purport).

Let us always remember that ISKCON is not just another movement. It is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda’s movement for fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s inner desire to save the world. As Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in his concluding words to his *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* commentary: “The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has been started to fulfill the desire of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda.” Because Śrīla Prabhupāda had cent percent faith in the words of his spiritual master, he was uniquely successful in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. If we have similar faith in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s words and try to follow the unequaled and unsurpassed example he set of tirelessly working to propagate Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, we shall achieve all success in our own spiritual endeavors and properly serve our glorious founder-*ācārya*.

Submitted by the worst of your disciples,  
Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

## Candra Mukha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, a thousand obeisances at your lotus feet.

Like a great sugar-candy mountain,  
O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came down from Goloka  
to cure the ignorance of this jaundiced world.

Your Bhaktivedanta purports are potent instructions that,  
combined with the sweetness of your *bhajans,* full of universal compassion,  
are the perfect medicine  
for any jaundiced man, ignorant like myself.

I am so unfortunate that  
I could not personally know you,  
but by spending my life in contemplation of your divine pictures,  
I see your eyes always flooded with tears of love,  
and I can understand that, certainly,  
you are not of this world.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you exist in the three phases of time.  
Please let me live in your world.

I am so unfortunate that  
I could not personally know you,  
but I am convinced that, by spending my life hearing your divine *bhajans,*  
the pure vibration of your manifested spiritual emotion,  
I will be at your side in the safest position in the universe  
and will be protected  
from the whirlpool of the sinful activities that threaten me.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you are the biggest distributor of *kṛṣṇa-nāma*!  
Please let me be always at your side,  
and let me taste the ever-increasing nectar of the Lord’s holy name.

I am so unfortunate that  
I could not personally know you,  
but I am sure that  
by offering *pūjā* to your divine *mūrti*  
and constantly chanting your sacred name,  
I will be at your side in the safest position in the universe  
and will wake from this awful nightmare.

I am sure that  
by spending my life reading your transcendental books,  
I will live happily with you,  
under your paternal protection.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so powerful, like the sun!  
The effulgent rays of your association cast all sins far, far away  
and transform this tasteless world into a wonderful place.

I am convinced that by distributing your transcendental books  
and working very hard for your mission,  
I will be at your side in the safest position in the universe  
and will accept your complete mercy, as it is.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are very affectionate to the devotees.  
Please let me reside in your world of *bhakti*.  
Let me swim in the nectarean water of your *saṅkīrtana* ocean,  
and with the help and the support of your dearmost devotees,  
let me participate eternally in your wonderful movement.

I am so unfortunate that  
I could not personally know you  
but fortunately,  
like a maximum sign of your causeless mercy,  
I can know your servants and become attached to their lotus feet.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please hear my prayer:  
Let me always live with your devotees,  
and let me always dance at the sound of your holy name.  
Let me serve them and reach the perfection of life:  
to obtain the most valuable sword of your remembrance.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me be with them  
so they may protect me from all doubt and fear,  
and let them carry me to you.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me  
the privilege of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness,  
and, please, let me eternally belong to you.

The insignificant servant of your devotees,  
Candra Mukha Swami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet.

I am sorry you have to deal with such an impetuous son, who, like a wild horse, wheels his head this way and that whenever you tug on the reins.

My position is very clear. That is, when you don’t tell me what to do, I complain, and when you do tell me what to do, I complain. Ignore me and I curse the heavens. Seek to enlist me in a duty that boggles my puny brain, and I roll my eyes in protest. If I perceive not even the slightest self-interest for me in your plans, I back out your door—of course, with consummate reverence and politeness. But you don’t let me slip away.

In spite of my juvenile behavior, you astound me when you place your Goloka lotus feet deep within my sordid heart. For this astonishing grace, I tearfully beg to thank you. Certainly you are present much more in the hearts of your other sons and daughters. Nevertheless, because you skillfully and affectionately navigate the depths of my tempestuous heart, I fall at your feet in utter amazement and gratitude.

You are the master and I am the servant—we have no other relationship than this. True, sometimes I live in fear of your seemingly inconceivable requests. Skulking in the hallways, I may shudder at the thought of another impossible mission. Therefore, on your holy Vyāsa-pūjā day, I implore you to please overlook any petty foolishness and momentary wavering. Dearest Eternal Father, kindly bless me with the mercy of always living in complete friendship with your desires.

Trying to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Repeating the words of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and praying that I may imbibe his mood of devotion for the spiritual master, I approach your lotus feet for the power to properly glorify you.

“If you examine me, you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. If you are not merciful to me, I can only weep, and I will not be able to maintain my life.”

The spiritual master bestows upon a faithful and submissive disciple his full blessings in the form of the knowledge and devotion that flows in the teacher’s spiritual lineage, the *guru-paramparā*. You were cent percent faithful to your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. The precepts and qualities of the Brahmā-Gauḍīya-Mādhva sampradāya are thus resting in your heart, and exemplified in your life.

So that I may glorify you on this auspicious day, please give me the eyes to see how the wonderful qualities of the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, as described in the Śrī Śrī *Ṣaḍ-gosvāmy-aṣṭaka,* are perfectly manifested in your life.

*kṛṣṇotkīrtana-gāna-nartana-parau premāmṛtāmbho-nidhī*

In the very beginning of his *Ṣaḍ-gosvāmy-aṣṭaka,* Śrīla Śrīnivāsa Ācārya describes the most important attribute of a *gosvāmī*—he is attached to *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*.

When reading the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* we marvel at how the six Gosvāmīs were so absorbed in chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and dancing that they even neglected such basic physical necessities as eating and sleeping.

You describe how absorbed a *gosvāmī* is in *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana:* “Above all, a *gosvāmī* is always busy with *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana* (*kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*). *Kīrtana* does not only refer to chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and playing cymbals. *Kīrtana* also means writing about Kṛṣṇa, as well as reading books about Him. *Kīrtana* also includes talking of Kṛṣṇa, thinking of Him, worshiping Him, cooking for Him, and eating for Him. All that is *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. A *gosvāmī,* therefore, should always engage in *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana* this way or that, twenty-four hours a day.” (*Back to Godhead,* 1974, No. 65)

You also describe *premāmṛtāmbho-nidhi* (the nectar-ocean of love of God): “Therefore *gosvāmī* means twenty-four hours engaged in *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana,* in this way or that way. How? *Premāmṛtāmbho-nidhi*. Because they were merged into the ocean of *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Unless we have got *kṛṣṇa-prema,* love for Kṛṣṇa, how can we remain satisfied simply in the business of Kṛṣṇa? That is not possible. Those who have not developed love for Kṛṣṇa, they can not be engaged twenty-four hours in the business of Kṛṣṇa.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, March 17, 1974, Vṛndāvana)

While reading these descriptions, we are struck by how aptly they describe your absorption in *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. Like the six Gosvāmīs, you were so merged in the business of Kṛṣṇa that you hardly slept but a few hours a day!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you were so merged in the ocean of love of God, the holy name is now chanted in every town and village. We sit at your feet, awed by your commitment to *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. We beg for a drop of your taste for the holy name.

*dhīrādhīra-jana-priyau priya-karau nirmatsarau pūjitau*

One of the characteristics of pure devotional service is that one becomes attractive to everyone. You describe how the six Gosvāmīs exemplified this:

“They were affectionate and popular both to the gentle as well as to the ruffians, these Gosvāmīs. Everyone liked them. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so nice that unless one is a staunch atheist everyone will like this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement” (Lecture, November 18, 1968, Los Angeles).

You describe Sanātana Gosvāmī as especially embodying this quality. “When the six Gosvāmīs were in Vṛndāvana they were popular. If a man had a quarrel with his wife, he would go to Sanātana Gosvāmī, who would give him directions by which he could settle his problems. When Sanātana Gosvāmī gave his judgment—be it true or not—people would accept it. The Gosvāmīs were that popular.” (*Back to Godhead,* 1974, No. 65)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, everyone liked you. You did not just enter a village in Vraja but the asphalt jungle of New York. The people there are by nature not very respectful. You were so kind to them that you gave them *prasādam* and invited them to come and hear the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. With your affection you won their hearts, and by abiding by your orders they became Vaiṣṇavas, or devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the soft heart of Sanātana Gosvāmī is embodied in you. I pray to your compassionate nature to melt my heart so I can become a selfless preacher.

*śrī-caitanya-kṛpā-bharau bhuvi bhuvo bhārāvahantārakau*

When Rāmānanda Rāya heard the wonderful poetic expressions explaining devotional service that were written by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, he expressed to Lord Caitanya that such depth of expression is not possible unless one is directly empowered by the Lord. Lord Caitanya replied that when He met Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī at Prayāga, He was attracted and satisfied by his qualities, and thus the Lord empowered him to expand the literature of *bhakti*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how attracted and satisfied Lord Caitanya must be with your qualities to empower you to spread the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement throughout the world! Who can be more fully blessed by Lord Caitanya?

*vande rūpa-sanātanau raghu-yugau śrī-jīva-gopālakau*

Because the six Gosvāmīs were attached to *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*, merged in an ocean of love of God, kind to everyone, nonenvious, and empowered by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to deliver the conditioned souls, they are most worthy of worship. What could be a more perfect description of your transcendental personality?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me not just worship you with my words, but let me give my life for your service.

All the jewels of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy that are reflected in the hearts of the six Gosvāmīs are also reflected in yours. The Śrī Śrī Ṣaḍ-Gosvāmy-aṣṭaka is thus also indirectly the Śrīla Prabhupāda Gosvāmy-aṣṭaka, as each word of praise for them also expounds your glories. I desire to offer all the jewels of Śrīla Śrīnivāsa Ācārya’s eight prayers at your lotus feet, but it is beyond the scope of a single Vyāsa-pūjā offering. For your pleasure, allow me to offer at your feet just a few more of his precious expressions.

*nānā-śāstra-vicāraṇaika-nipunau sad-dharma-saṁsthāpakau*

**“\[I offer my respectful obeisances unto the six Gosvāmīs,\] who are very expert in scrutinizingly studying all the revealed scriptures with the aim of establishing eternal religious principles for the benefit of all human beings.”**

In this second verse, the words *lokānāṁ-hita-kāriṇau* are especially significant for your glorification. *Lokānāṁ-hita-kāriṇau* means “doing welfare work for the people of the world.” Who has ever or will ever surpass you in expertly presenting the essence of the revealed scriptures for the benefit of all humanity?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me that I may understand your books and present them expertly to help others advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*śrī-gaurāṅga-guṇānuvarṇana-vidhau śraddhā-samṛddhy-anvitau*
