*pāpottāpa-nikṛntanau tanu-bhṛtāṁ govinda-gānāmṛtaiḥ*

**“\[I offer my respectful obeisances unto the six Gosvāmīs,\] who are very much enriched in understanding Lord Caitanya, and who are thus expert in narrating His transcendental qualities. They can purify all conditioned souls from the reactions of their sinful activities by pouring upon them songs about Govinda.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you so expertly gave to the world the pastimes of Lord Caitanya in the form of *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. You are purifying all the conditioned souls by inundating the world with *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which simply sings the glories of Govinda.

*tyaktvā tūrṇam aśeṣa-maṇḍala-pati-śreṇīṁ sadā tuccha-vat*

**“\[I offer my respectful obeisances unto the six Gosvāmīs,\] who kicked off all aristocracy as insignificant.”**

Although you were an elderly, learned, devotional, and aristocratic Bengali gentleman, you came to spend your so-called retired years in the Bowery and the Lower East Side of New York City. In the history of India, has any other saint so thoroughly kicked off all aristocracy? Why did you and the six Gosvāmīs do it? Śrīnivāsa Ācārya explains, “In order to deliver the poor conditioned souls, they accepted loincloths, treating themselves as mendicants.” Out of compassion only you lived among us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me kick off all pretensions and maintain the simple life of a *sannyāsī* to help others.

In the *Ṣaḍ-gosvāmy-aṣṭaka* there are several descriptions of the more advanced and esoteric moods of the six Gosvāmīs. I lack the realization to deeply elaborate on them. There is no doubt that these verses are also descriptions of your inner mood of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I would, however, like to describe something about the last verse of *Śrī Śrī Ṣaḍ-Gosvāmy-aṣṭaka* by retelling a wonderful story about how you explained it. This story was told to me personally last summer by your senior disciple Śrīmatī Yamunā Devī.

*he rādhe vraja-devike ca lalite he nanda-sūno kutaḥ*

A life member once gave Sarasvatī, the four-year-old daughter of Śyāmasundara and Mālatī, your personal assistants, a beautiful silver Deity of Kṛṣṇa. When Śrīla Prabhupāda was informed that Sarasvatī was worshiping a Deity made of silver, he told her parents that if such a small child would keep such a Deity while they were traveling, then it would certainly be stolen. He offered the practical suggestion that it could be replaced by a simple brass one.

The next day when Sarasvatī came to worship her Deity and saw a brass one instead, she began to cry hysterically. “Where is my Kṛṣṇa! I want my Kṛṣṇa!” Her parents tried to pacify her by explaining that Kṛṣṇa was still there in the form of the brass Deity, but young Sarasvatī became even more infuriated. Sitting on the floor and flopping her arms against her sides in a tantrum she wailed, “That’s not my Kṛṣṇa! I want my Kṛṣṇa!”

The child’s rage continued for days despite her parents’ attempts to pacify her. The child was causing such a commotion that her parents’ service was being affected. They had no recourse but to seek the advice of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda immediately called for Sarasvatī. Śyāmasundara, Mālatī, and Yamunā peaked in from the door and watched in amazement as Śrīla Prabhupāda attempted to pacify the child by explaining the last verse of the *Ṣaḍ-gosvāmy-aṣṭaka*.

“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the six Gosvāmīs … who were chanting very loudly everywhere in Vṛndāvana, shouting, ‘Queen of Vṛndāvana, Rādhārāṇī! O Lalitā! O son of Nanda Mahārāja! Where are you all now? Are you just on the hill of Govardhana, or are you under the trees on the bank of the Yamunā? Where are you? These were their moods in executing Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

For one hour Śrīla Prabhupāda instructed the young but attentive child on how the best devotees never think they have Kṛṣṇa but think, “Where is Kṛṣṇa?!” By carefully explaining this verse, Śrīla Prabhupāda was able to convince Sarasvatī to be satisfied with her mood of separation: “Where is my Kṛṣṇa?”

Yamunā Devī told me that the devotees were so amazed by Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ability to communicate such a high concept to such a young child that it remained the talk of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s entourage for several weeks.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your heart is the treasury of the wealth of our *guru-paramparā,* from the basic practices of *sādhana-bhakti* to the deep moods of separation of the six Gosvāmīs. I pray to qualify for your legacy by becoming a faithful and gentle disciple, surrendered to your mission.

Your humble servant,  
Dhanurdhara Swami

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

All glories to you!

All glories to you, the most merciful devotee of the Lord!

Thank you very, very much for giving me the chance to serve your *saṅkīrtana* movement. This movement is giving *guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpa* to every living entity. I am one of the insignificant living entities who has gotten a drop of the mercy given by your movement.

All glories to your Society!

All glories to your mission!

I am very happy among your disciples and followers. Please never kick me out.

Your eternal servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

**In How Many Ways Do I Perceive You?**

**(1) Bhaktivedanta purports**

“I will never die. I shall live forever in my books, and you will utilize.” (San Francisco, 1975)

If not in your books,  
where else to look?  
where else to look?  
But the unfortunate lament, “No time,”  
as if we had too much to do.  
The whole world depends upon us—  
“France is still there—where has Napoleon gone to?”

“If they come to sense some day, they’ll read all these books, and they will come to know how to love life.” (Bombay, 1977)

**(2) Prabhupādānugas**

“He lives forever in his instructions, and the follower lives with him.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Dedication)

Thousands of disciples—  
branches of the tree.  
Disciples of disciples—  
fruit of twigs as sweet.  
How many dreams,  
strangers be blessed?  
Guru is one—  
but voices numberless.

“You are all my limbs of my body. Unless you cooperate, my life will be useless.” (Letter, July 7, 1968)

**(3) Rādhā-Dāmodara Temple**

“\[These rooms are my\] eternal residence” (*Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta,* volume 5, p. 122)

Śrī Rūpa’s bright ray  
shone upon you in dream;  
right behind you he’d be,  
but did you ever leave?  
Plans for Mahāprabhu’s movement  
still are formulating;  
ecstatic meetings—  
Rūpa, Jīva, and thee.

“These *samādhis* are the best in Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism. They actually inspired me to go to the West. Now I have hundreds of temples to go to, but I still like it here best of all.” (*Vrindavan Days,* p. 47)

**(4) Hare Kṛṣṇa Mantra**

“Prabhupāda, will you please chant one round with me?”

“You should know that I am always chanting with you.”

*(Prabhupāda-Līlā,* p. 169)

The only letter  
you ever benedicted me with:  
“Chant Maha-Mantra—  
see how Maya flees.”  
Material energy  
cannot long stand  
with you in my heart  
and sword in my hand.

“In the future, when you are not always with us, how can we get direction from you?”

“Kṛṣṇa’s name is not different from Kṛṣṇa. Do you understand?”

(*Prabhupāda-līlā,* p. 79)

**(5) Bṛhad-Mṛdaṅga**

“He said that the best use of intelligence is to accept *sannyāsa* and go preach. It is also the best way to associate with the *guru*.”

(*A Transcendental Diary,* vol. 1, p. 325)

In spite of all the ways  
mentioned just above,  
one emerges foremost—  
spreading the “Mission of Love.”  
Driving down the interstate,  
book table at university,  
your smile is all-pervading—  
what more could a servant need?

“If you kindly try to fulfill my mission for which you have been sent there, that will be our constant association.” (Letter, June 22, 1970)

Tomāra kukkura,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Please allow me to submit the following offering on this most divine day of your appearance.

“Distribute this Krishna consciousness movement all over the world. Let people eat these fruits \[of love of Godhead\] and ultimately become free from old age and death. If the fruits are distributed all over the world, My reputation as a pious man will be known everywhere, and thus all people will glorify My name with great pleasure.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 9.39–40)

“One Indian’s humble service”  
Made the above come true, you say.  
“If all Indians had taken this path,”  
This world wouldn’t have gone astray.

It’s “the duty of everyone,”  
“The best welfare work for all.”  
“Help this great movement” end  
The “disaster” that befalls.

Words like yours above,  
And those of Gaura Hari,  
You’ve written time and time again  
In your books for all to see.

Hearing from you daily,  
I thus cannot forget:  
You want my life’s breath used  
To combat Kali’s threat.

Being your disciple  
Means nothing short of sharing  
With others all you’ve taught,  
So that the lust they’re bearing

Will be turned to love of God,  
And ours, too, as we preach.  
The rising moon of Gaura  
Soothes all within its reach.

When we hide in Kali’s shadow  
To escape those soothing rays,  
You push us toward the light,  
And so we spend our days.

Preaching is the essence.  
There is nothing so sublime.  
Your message is the nectar  
That saves us every time.

One thing you always tell us  
To help us stay in line:  
“One stick will break quite easily,  
So together please combine.”

Thus two thoughts you have given  
That I pray to keep in mind  
Are that we preach without a stop  
And that we preach combined.

Your Centennial celebration  
Will see success this way.  
Your fame will spread both far and wide.  
Kali’s night turned to your day.

Your insignificant servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Today we—your disciples, granddisciples, and other followers—gather together to praise you and rededicate our lives to you. By recounting your transcendental pastimes, we mitigate the feelings of separation we maintain for you deep within our hearts.

Your physical presence among us, brief as it was, enabled us to see the miracles of a pure devotee. You amazed us with your empowered preaching and endeared us with your loving devotion to the Lord. Thus today no devotee will find himself bereft of words to describe your glorious pastimes. They are endless. We need only choose one pastime and, as with a verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* elaborate upon and elucidate it forever.

This morning I remembered the time a magician visited you with a magic show at the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir in Vṛndāvana. He proudly displayed his ability to make things appear and disappear. At the end of his little show you smiled and offered him some words to curb his pride. You said, “You can make so many articles disappear, but can you make birth and death disappear?”

The magician answered shyly, “No, sir.”

Then you said, “I can.” He stood there dumbfounded for a moment. Then you continued, “I can make birth and death disappear from the lives of my disciples through teaching them this process of devotional service to the Lord. Therefore I am a greater magician than you.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all your glorious achievements culminate in this unique feat, which you seem to perform year after year. Several months ago my mother passed away in auspicious circumstances, and I would like to share the story with you, since you yourself noted the departure of the mother of one of your disciples in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. You write, “We have had actual experience of how Americans and Europeans, because of their full Kṛṣṇa consciousness, have purified their whole families, so much so that a mother of a devotee, at the time of her death, inquired about Kṛṣṇa with her last breath” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.10, purport).

By material standards my mother was highly intelligent, graduating at the top of her class in one of America’s most prestigious universities in the 1930’s. But as material benedictions sometimes are, her education was an obstacle to her understanding spiritual life. When I first became a devotee she opposed my decision with mundane scientific arguments. Only after a long time did she reluctantly resign herself to the fact that I had accepted the path of devotional service to the Lord. But she never gave up her own belief that life came from matter and everything was finished at death.

As the years went by she softened her stand somewhat, if only to keep our relationship intact. From time to time she would send me money and gifts, signing her letters “Hare Krishna” to encourage me to call home. Once I visited her and found the books of yours that I’d sent her during the last twenty-five years collecting dust on a shelf at the back of the garage.

Then some months ago she learned she had terminal cancer. She kept it secret from me, and in the months that passed we exchanged only one letter. In that letter she asked if I was still interested in taking her to India, an offer I had made several times throughout the years. Curious as to why she suddenly expressed such a desire, I called her. It happened to be the day before she died. She informed me of her cancer and in a weak voice said, “Don’t worry about me; now everything is all right.” After hanging up the telephone, I wondered how she could say that everything was all right. Most people are extremely frightened by the prospect of death. The next day my brother phoned to inform me that she’d passed away.

I asked him if she’d left any message for me, or had said anything significant before she died. He replied, “Yes, her last words to us were ‘Don’t lament for me. I’m not this body, I’m an eternal spirit soul. For the soul there is never birth nor death.’”

I said to my brother, “That’s amazing! Mother never believed in these things! Where did she learn this?”

He replied, “It’s not amazing at all. For the last five months she’d been reading the books you’d sent her through the years. When she learned she was going to die, she took them off the shelf one by one and read them every day. She kept notes and marked the important points with a pen. When she found she couldn’t understand some things, she bought a tape recorder and began listening to your lectures, which she ordered from your tape ministry. She listened to two tapes every day. She changed so much by reading those books and listening to those tapes. She had no fear at the moment of death. We were speaking about the *Bhagavad-gītā* and Kṛṣṇa until the moment she died.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I tried for so many years to interest her in spiritual life, without much success. But you arranged everything at the end through your books and gave her faith in this wonderful process, which saved her at the moment of death. I could only manage to fulfill her final request to me to take her to India by placing her ashes in the Yamunā River. There I prayed that you would continue to guide her in her progress toward the Lord. You are truly the greatest magician, for you are making birth and death disappear from the lives of those who take shelter of your lotus feet.

Your eternal servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Īśvara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and to the powerful movement you have created.

Life is this world is like a drop of water on the leaf of a tree, and we may always wonder how we are still living. Actually to live (and what to speak of having the association of Your Divine Grace!) is a very special gift, but unfortunately the easiest thing in this world is to die. Materialistic people are living in a dead condition, and when they die they are unprepared for eternal blissful life. What a waste of time!

Analyzing myself, I find that I have not been taking advantage of your holy association, and I am one of these dead living entities. I am dying at every moment, but still, rascal that I am, I keep pretending I am living. But I long for one thing: I want to live on your instructions, your remembrance, your movement, and your pastimes. You came here and brought life to everything, giving a meaning to this material nature, which otherwise would be dead. The love you have, together with your mercy and strong desire to spread this movement, is the most wonderful thing.

Please let me live eternally with you. I wish I can keep always fresh within my mind the rare moments of your personal association. Unexpectedly you sometimes come, in realizations or dreams, and you allow me the *darśana* of your divine self, supplying me the nectar that can keep me living forever. I do not want to go on dying; I want to live always in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, having strong faith in you and working always for you. Combining my sentiments with the passion for work is surely the eternal and blissful life I have been always hankering for.

Please let me be a part of your movement, helping your other disciples and followers to build a better world, based on the meaning of life.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your eternal servant,  
Īśvara Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

Not just another guru.  
Not just another guru.  
Not just another guru.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Today we honor the representative of Śrīla Vyāsa. Śrīla Vyāsadeva has many representatives. And so we may think of Śrīla Prabhupāda as one of them. And of course he is.

Yet Śrīla Prabhupāda stands distinct, distinguished, in a class by himself. He is not merely “a guru,” our guru, one among many, just as the *Bhāgavatam* is not just another book, Hare Kṛṣṇa not just another *mantra*, and Lord Kṛṣṇa not just another god.

To study Śrīla Prabhupāda is to study superexcellence. Part of his superexcellence is that he *did* act just as though “another guru.” He did things any guru should do, or at least aspire to; he set an example for us to follow. (Harikeśa Mahārāja has written about this very nicely for the Vyāsa-pūjā of a previous year.)

Yet Śrīla Prabhupāda also did the extraordinary, and was extraordinary. And we need to keep his singularity in mind.

Some people, it seems, suppose that any guru, to be bona fide, must come up to the same level, or nearly the same, as Śrīla Prabhupāda. But that is neither necessary nor possible.

It is not necessary because Śrīla Prabhupāda far excelled the qualifications every bona fide guru must have. There may be many fragrant flowers, but among them there is extraordinary excellence in the *campaka* or the rose; there may be many birds, but among them special excellence in the peacock or the swan; many stars, but among them special excellence in the moon.

Among gurus, Śrīla Prabhupāda shines with extraordinary brilliance. To expect that sort of brilliance from *every* guru is to expect the impossible—and to lose sight of how extraordinary Śrīla Prabhupāda is.

Then again, if by virtue of serving as guru one thinks one has become as good as Prabhupāda, or nearly so, again one has lost one’s vision. We’ve had our experience: The little mice who became tigers and cast hungry eyes on the yogi turned into little mice again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda stood in a class apart, uniquely blessed by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Anyone who wants to cover the same territory, take on the same task, but not follow in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footsteps is an offender and a fool. And anyone who sincerely dedicates his life to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s service will always come out successful, overcoming even the most subtle and difficult obstacles.

This year at Māyāpur, Kṛṣṇa reaffirmed to us the depth and purity of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings. When Śrīla Prabhupāda conveyed to us the conclusions of the previous *ācāryas,* he did so perfectly, preserving and transmitting the philosophy exactly as it is, neither watering anything down, nor covering anything over, nor leaving anything out. He gave us the essence of everything, exactly as we ought to receive it.

We therefore don’t need to add anything, subtract anything, or change anything. We need only faithfully serve Śrīla Prabhupāda’s orders, and everything will be revealed.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

My Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. Another year has passed, which has brought about a couple of memorable events, thus inspiring me to continue preaching with enthusiasm by your mercy.

First of all, we feel grateful to all your disciples and granddisciples involved in designing and constructing the splendid *samādhi* in Śrī Māyāpur *dhāma,* a feature already famous in Bhāratavarṣa that will soon attract the attention of the whole world.

What took many years to complete has turned into a most fitting monument establishing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the saint of saints, a preacher extraordinaire and the savior of the whole world. The huge tall dome symbolizes your greatest achievement, i.e., successfully spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. The beautiful blue mosaic work decorating the interior of the dome compares to the vast shelter that you have provided to protect us from the onslaught of Kali-yuga. The *samādhi* walls, the floor, the arches and columns made of marble reminded us of the many temples you have given us, such as the ones in Bombay and Vṛndāvana.

However, the most pleasing sight occurred during the *abhiṣeka* ceremony, when your disciples attending the inaugural celebration enthusiastically gathered around your life-size bronze *mūrti* for the installation and eagerly pushed past each other in order to get closer to you, to rub and bathe your beautiful golden form with ghee, yogurt, milk, etc. From their happy faces it appeared as if a group of young children had gotten a rare opportunity to bathe their beloved father. After witnessing the beautiful architecture, I am confident that many visitors will be coming to Śrī Māyāpur Dhāma to witness the marvel and also to purify themselves in the process. It is an apt offering by ISKCON towards your Centennial celebrations

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while preaching in different countries, I have experienced your mercy and the fruits of your hard labor. You have set a strong foundation for the preaching work, and all we have to do is sincerely follow in your footsteps. For example, since your last Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I along with His Holiness Bhaktitīrtha Swami had the opportunity to host the new president of South Africa, His Excellency Dr. Nelson Mandela, at the Temple of Understanding in Durban. The president was very impressed with the temple, your books, and your movement. When taking *darśana* of the Deities, the president paid full *daṇḍavats,* delighting everyone present.

Later on, although not on the schedule, Dr. Mandela decided to stay longer in order to honor the Lord’s *prasādam*. This gave us the opportunity to talk about vegetarianism, and the president listened intently to what we had to say. The news of his visit was broadcast locally and internationally, thus making it a popular news item.

These types of encounters are very encouraging for further preaching work, and as time will tell, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you will be recognized throughout the world as the greatest Vaiṣṇava of our times, as that great soul by whose mercy the world received the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the science of *bhakti*. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the savior of the whole world, our eternal spiritual master residing eternally in your beautiful books. My prayer is only that you will please keep engaging me in your preaching work forever.

In the service of our ever well-wisher,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. Today is your auspicious 99th appearance day. Just four months remain before your Centennial year begins, and only one year before your 1996 Mahā-vyāsa-pūjā. The atmosphere at the “Centennial House” in Delhi is surcharged with activity, and we are filled with anticipation and excitement.

I remember first thinking and speaking of your Centennial celebration in March 1986. We had arrived in Māyāpur with the Padayātrā celebrating Lord Caitanya’s fifth Centennial, and I was asked to speak on the main stage. Someone asked, “Now that the walk for the 500th anniversary is over, what will be the new theme of the Padayātrā?” I replied, “Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial.” With this in mind we embarked on a long journey to last until 1996.

In 1989, when *padayātrīs* were about to set foot on Western roads, we set our sights on conducting Padayātrās in one hundred countries by 1996. Later that year we began promoting the idea of having a Centennial celebration in India.

In 1991 a Centennial committee was formed in India, and the first Centennial newsletter, *Prabhupāda Smṛti*, was published and distributed. Then in March 1992, the Centennial Global Ministry was established by the GBC to prepare for worldwide celebrations. The initial Centennial plans were put on paper, and the concept of a ten-petal lotus came into being.

In 1993, the GBC Body resolved that “the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness shall make the campaign for and celebration of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Centennial a focus of all its activities through 1996.” The following year the GBC finalized the Centennial plan, which had matured owing to the input of many devotees. Now twelve petals blossomed on the Centennial lotus. These are divided into three different categories of activities:

1\. Internal Growth

Increasing Prabhupāda consciousness  
Raising spiritual standards  
Uniting Prabhupāda’s family  
Strengthening ISKCON management

2\. Expanding the Mission

Spreading the Holy Name  
Producing and distributing books  
Expanding *prasādam* distribution  
Building congregations

3\. Celebrating the Event

Offering special achievements  
Establishing memorials  
Enhancing worldwide reputation  
Celebrating 1996 events

For some of us, these past years have been very intense and action-packed, focused on the Centennial celebrations. We have felt great pleasure throughout these hard-working days. Thank you for engaging all of us in spreading your glories worldwide.

A few years ago, while offering the Centennial Ministry report to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava in Māyāpur, I prayed in an unusual way: *Dhanaṁ dehi, janaṁ dehi*—“My dear Lord, please give us wealth! Give us manpower!” Soon thereafter, the Lord revealed to us a fund-raising scheme: The Sahasra-tīrtha-jala-mahā-abhiṣeka.

On the occasion of your Mahā-vyāsa-pūjā, on September 6, 1996, we shall offer you a *mahā-abhiṣeka* with sacred waters gathered from 1,008 holy *tīrthas*. This bathing will be performed in Calcutta, your birth city, and at ISKCON centers all over the world. The scheme is that every *kalaśa* of water will be sponsored. In this way, these invaluable sacred waters, which are emanating from the Lord’s lotus feet, will be turning into gold! If everything goes as planned, the Sahasra-tīrtha-jala sponsorship program alone will provide sufficient funds for our worldwide Centennial Celebration needs.

We thought it would be a herculean task to collect waters from the holy places, but it is proving to be very easy. It seems that all the holy waters are eagerly awaiting the water collectors, as if they had been ordered by Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava to take part in your Vyāsa-pūjā Mahā-abhiṣeka.

In regards to *janaṁ dehi,* the Lord, who is sitting in everyone’s heart, by now has inspired your followers all over the world to take up Centennial services. No more skepticism, no procrastination! The question, “Why Centennial?” is no longer asked. Rather, everyone is asking how they can celebrate the Centennial.

The Centennial campaign has boosted the morale of your Society. It is bringing unity and renewed dynamism. We want to continue assisting you to build the house in which the whole world can live, and to accomplish this we want to take practical steps toward global preaching strategies and internal development. A strong ISKCON will undoubtedly achieve the goal of enhancing your worldwide reputation and establishing ISKCON as a powerful spiritual force. We are more than convinced that the Centennial will do good for ISKCON, which is dedicated to the topmost welfare of humanity at large. A rejuvenated movement will emerge at the dawn of the twenty-first century.

Kṛṣṇa is at the top of the Centennial organization because He wants to glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, His pure devotee. He has already surprised us with many wonderful developments, and we expect more before the end of 1996. We thank the Lord again and again for using us as a small instrument to glorify you.

Now the questions arise: “How will the Centennial team be employed after 1996? What will be our next project? What will we campaign for?” The leaders of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement seem to know the answer. Prabhupāda, I am submitting myself unto you. Please convey your desire to me and remove all my doubts regarding the direction I should take to further the *saṅkīrtana* mission.

On this auspicious occasion we pray that the Lord may remove all obstacles to the successful completion of our Centennial campaign and celebration.

May your fame spread all over the three worlds!

Your Centennial servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahānidhi Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

At initiation I took vows promising to chant a minimum of sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* every day, and to follow the four regulative principles. These vows inspire steady progress on the path of devotional service. Besides affirming these vows daily, I add more vows as the years pass.

I vow to love my spiritual master and all Kṛṣṇa’s devotees on the path of pure devotion. I vow to love and honor all the sacred days connected with Śrī Kṛṣṇa, such as Ekādaśī, Janmāṣṭamī, and the Vaiṣṇava Tirobhāvas. I vow to love the holy land of Vṛndāvana, where Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa eternally enjoy Their pastimes of love. I vow to love all-merciful Māyāpur *dhāma,* where Caitanya Mahāprabhu freely gives His divine service.

I vow to love the holy names of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and to distribute them to searching souls eager for divine love. I vow to love the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* serve it always, and to share its secrets with the sincere. I vow to love Śrī Śrī Gāndharvikā-Giridhārī and serve Them always with every thought, word, and deed. I vow to love Giri-Govardhana, Rādhā-kuṇḍa, Śyāma-kuṇḍa, and all the wonderful pastime places of Rādhā-Mādhava.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I vow to love your books, which you lovingly gave to me. And I vow to give this love to the world, which so much needs this transcendental love. I vow to love the nine forms of devotional service, and through them to offer my love to Śrī Guru, Śrī Rādhā, and Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

I vow to love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and live eternally in your divine service. Śrīla Prabhupāda, only by your mercy can I follow and realize these transcendental vows of love. Please bestow your mercy upon me, and fill me with pure love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Your aspiring servant,  
Mahānidhi Swami

## Maha-Viṣṇu Swami (the younger)

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.”**

His Divine Grace Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is the representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and he is delivering His mercy.

The countless living entities (the Lord’s *taṭa-stha śakti*), desiring imaginary independence (*māyā*), forgetting their eternal spiritual relationship with the Lord, are now helplessly afflicted with the threefold miseries, just as in a forest fire (*dāva-anala-līḍha*) so much burning heat, pain, and fear is there. By buckets of water or fire engines the forest fire cannot be extinguished, nor can the fire of inevitable birth, old age, disease, and death be extinguished, since their so-called remedial measures are also miserable (*duḥkhauṣadaṁ tad api duḥkham,* in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.17, spoken by Prahlāda Mahārāja).

The rain-pouring cloud is the carrier of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, the merciful spiritual master who extinguishes the overpowering flames of lust (*duṣpurenānalena ca, Bhagavad-gītā* 3.39).

By the spiritual master’s preaching of devotional service in the form of the chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—the fire of material life (*mahā-dāvāgni, Śikṣāṣṭaka* 1) is extinguished.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, your success in spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement worldwide has revealed you to be the Lord’s own *sena-pati bhakta.* In the *Caitanya-maṅgala,* by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura, the Lord Himself predicted the advent of such a *bhakta,* who would deliver those rascals fleeing to foreign countries in order to avoid Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

I can therefore never repay my debt to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for I was burning in the forest fire of eternal dark forgetfulness of the Lord, and you have given me the light of the Lord’s eternal blissful loving service.

I am praying now, on this occasion of the 99th anniversary of your blessed appearance, that in the remainder of my life I can do something to please you very much by delivering Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, humbly following in you footsteps.

I am certainly a person to be rightfully rejected, but you have given me a chance to glorify you till this life ends and forever, just as one might offer up a flame to glorify the sun.

Your most humble servant,  
Maha-Viṣṇu Swami (the younger)

## Navayogendra Swami

My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept my most humble obeisances unto the dust of your lotuslike feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who is the ocean of mercy and who is very kind upon the fallen souls like myself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is the most auspicious day, your divine appearance day. Śrīla Prabhupāda, when I was young I used to pray that my Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa would please allow me to meet His pure devotee, of whom I could take shelter. The Lord is so merciful that out of His causeless mercy He allowed me to meet Your Divine Grace. After meeting Your Divine Grace I was really pleased. My *ātmā* became very satisfied. Śrīla Prabhupāda you are the true representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and I am so fortunate that you are my spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy I have no regrets in my life, though I am devoid of intelligence and no good quality do I possess. This *bhajan* from *Śaraṇāgati* is completely befitting to my character. Kindly bestow your causeless mercy upon me and deliver me from the nescience of the material world and make me your humble servant.

“Gurudeva” (from *Śaraṇāgati*)

(1) gurudev!  
kṛpā-bindu diyā, koro’ ei dāse,  
tṛṇāpekhā ati hīna  
sakala sahane, bala diyā koro’,  
nija-māne spṛhā-hīna

(2) sakala sammāna korite śakati  
deho’ nātha! Jathājatha  
tabe to’ gāibo, hari-nāma-sukhe,  
aparādha ha’be hata

(3) kabe heno kṛpā, labhiyā e jana,  
kṛtārtha hoibe, nātha!  
śakti-buddhi-hīna, āmi ati dīna,  
koro’ more ātma-sātha

(4) jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi,  
tomāra karuṇā—sāra  
karuṇā nā hoile, kāndiyā kāndiyā,  
prāṇa nā rākhibo āra

Gurudeva, give to this servant just one drop of mercy. I am lower than a blade of grass. Give me all help. Give me strength. Let me be as you are, without desires or aspirations.

I offer you all respects, for thus I may have the energy to know you correctly. Then, by my chanting the holy name in ecstasy, all my offenses will cease.

When will such mercy fall to one who is weak and devoid of intelligence? Allow me to be with you.

If you examine me, you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. If you are not merciful unto me, I can only weep, and I will not be able to maintain my life.

Your humble servant,  
Navayogendra Swami

## Nityodita Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, I would like to beg forgiveness for straying from your lotus feet and offer this extremely grateful heart to your divine self.

Your one-pointed determination to please the previous *ācāryas* is the energy that created ISKCON, and it is that same energy that is expanding it, like a wonderful lotus flower. Unfortunately, I listened to the propaganda “ISKCON is dead” and became an outsider. You always said, “ISKCON is my body,” meaning that you invested all of your life force into spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement, using ISKCON as the vehicle. The life force of the spiritual master is not material but fully spiritual, eternal, full of knowledge and bliss. Your desires for preaching are dynamic ideas that are blossoming into incredible projects right before our eyes, engaging so many souls in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, recently your living presence and infinite mercy has lifted your New Vrindavan project and its residents out of the dangerous ocean of deviation and placed them safely on the shore of the previous *ācāryas*. I was drowning in that ocean, but I was so dull that I did not even know I was in danger! When the life preserver of your instructions, thrown by your faithful servants, kept hitting me in the head, I became angry. Instead of grabbing it, I tried to swim. But no matter how hard I swam, I never got anywhere! What a fool I was! Only when I finally became exhausted did I try to catch hold of the boat of your lotus feet, which are the only means to cross beyond the ocean of nescience. Because of your loving kindness, the determination to follow your very simple and clear instructions is once again arising in my heart.

At the Māyāpur festival this year, I met so many of your devoted followers from around the world and saw how they are working very hard, trying to pay you back for the wonderful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you so freely gave to us. However, no matter now hard we work to try to pay you back by spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, our debt to you only gets bigger and bigger and bigger! The more we serve your order, the more Kṛṣṇa reveals your unlimited glories. Then, the more we appreciate your actual position as Kṛṣṇa’s most dear servant, the more we want to do for you in order to pay off our increased debt. No materialist can ever comprehend why this “UNFAIR” arrangement is giving us so much happiness. We are being cheated; and we know it, but we love it! Our earnest prayer is this: “Please continue to cheat us, yes, cheat us out of the illusion that we are the enjoyers and awaken us to our real life of eternal divine service.”

Your lowly servant,  
Nityodita Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Only by our trying to understand your instructions and obeying them enthusiastically can our lives become successful. As we gain more experience it becomes clearer that you are not an ordinary person whose instructions can be subject to our speculative interpretations. When you visited Buffalo in 1969, a devotee named Nanda Kumāra dāsa came there from Canada to receive initiation. At that time, you were staying in Rūpānuga Prabhu’s apartment. While speaking to a gathering of devotees, you gave Nanda Kumāra a letter to put in the mailbox. You cautioned him not to put it in his back pocket, or otherwise he would forget to mail it. Several times you repeated this instruction to him. Later Nanda Kumāra met me in a laundry and said, “I forgot to mail the letter that Śrīla Prabhupāda gave me. I put it in my back pocket.” Without following the orders of the spiritual master, we are sure to be defeated by the illusory energy and to commit mistakes.

Although steady faith in the spiritual master is not easily obtained, when you were physically present Lord Caitanya invested you with the potency to distribute this faith. Many times in your association my faith in you and Kṛṣṇa consciousness causelessly increased. On the day celebrating the disappearance of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī in December 1969, when I saw you walking up the stairs to the temple room in Boston, I was struck with wonder and appreciation. Your transcendental effulgence dissipated the gloom of a dark winter day and pervaded the temple room with a celestial atmosphere, sweeter than any spring morning. When, sitting on your *vyāsāsana,* you glanced at me, I saw that with your spiritual vision you could understand everything about me, all the way to the soul.

Sometimes when I attended your *darśanas* or accompanied you on morning walks, I could to some degree perceive your exalted spiritual position. I often wondered how you had achieved such a marvelous state of transcendental existence. However, as you always stressed, it is an open secret that all perfection comes from following the instructions of the spiritual master. You encouraged us that although we may not have any spiritual assets from our birth or upbringing, if we surrender to the spiritual master, Lord Kṛṣṇa will create our spiritual assets. Sometimes my mind still deludes me into thinking that I should be immediately granted spiritual advancement without any austerity or sacrifice. But by your mercy I struggle to follow the process, and I look forward to the day when I can distribute your books in continuous joy and when I can concentrate on my *sādhana* and not only on *prasāda*.

Any service done under your guidance and with faith and devotion is ever fresh and new. If a devotee sincerely tries to please you, Lord Kṛṣṇa becomes pleased and reveals Himself to the devotee. Lord Kṛṣṇa’s name, vibrated according to your instructions, becomes the holy name, no longer simply letters of the alphabet. If we follow your instructions, our concentrated and careful hearing and chanting about Lord Kṛṣṇa in the association of devotees brings us immediately to the spiritual world. If I therefore could ask you for any benediction, it would be that you please give me faith in the service of your wonderful lotus feet.

Prahlādānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On the day of your all-auspicious appearance I feel great happiness. Please let me try to glorify your divine personality. I know that I will never be able to properly express my profound gratitude for all you have done for me and are still doing. I see with gratefulness how you are unfailingly watching over me.

In glorification of your divine personality, I would like to remember today the times when I personally met you. As a farmer is naturally interested to see whether the seeds he sowed have properly sprouted, so the spiritual master naturally desires to know whether his disciple has properly understood his teachings. Although these moments were brief, they have left deep impressions in my heart, which guide me during my whole life. The reason for this is that you are the greatest personality, and everything you do and say has profound meaning. I sincerely hope that you will be a little pleased by my simple glorification.

1\) 1971, a cool summer night, Heathrow Airport, U.K.  
I threw myself prostrate on the floor  
when I saw you beaming with Kṛṣṇa’s presence in your heart.  
As I was in the process of getting up—still on the floor—  
I saw you standing before me—acknowledging your lost son  
with a welcoming smile on your face.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the all-merciful, dearmost servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who collects the fallen souls.

2\) Even when I was completely new,  
I could see your great enthusiasm for preaching:  
In London, when you raised your hands in *kīrtana*  
to lift souls out of the ocean of *māyā;*  
In Paris, when you charmingly drowned an opponent  
in the laughter of the audience;  
In Stockholm, when you told the drunkards,  
“And even if you love wine  
you can still meditate on Kṛṣṇa,  
that He is the taste of wine”;  
Or in Germany, when you told a packed hall  
about repeating the holy name:  
“No, it is not hypnotism, it is purification.”  
And: “You can become Kṛṣṇa conscious in suit and tie.”  
And although you were the busiest world-preacher,  
your heart was so full of causeless compassion  
that you kindly gave your association  
to everyone who came in contact with you,  
even to that unphilosophical man from Turkey  
whom we had foolishly brought to your room—  
You simply inquired about his family!

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most empowered preacher, who could give Kṛṣṇa to one and all. All glories to your compassion for the fallen souls.

3\) Once in the Vondelpark, Amsterdam, at 6 a.m.,  
you brought us to a tree  
to observe the Supersoul.  
A baby bird flew  
from its nest to the ground,  
and you asked,  
“How does it know?”  
“Intuition,” someone said.  
“No, Supersoul,” you answered,  
totally sure about the presence of your Lord.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who saw Kṛṣṇa everywhere and in everything, and taught us how to do the same.

4\) Or in Paris, when we came unannounced,  
stealthily opening your door  
to say good-bye.  
You were totally absorbed  
in chanting the holy name  
on your *japa-mālā*  
while gently rocking in your rocking chair—  
a divine atmosphere all around you.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who brought us the nectar of the holy name and who personally relished it.

5\) 1974, Germany:  
I stood in your room,  
my hands covered with massage oil,  
totally nervous.  
You smiled encouragingly and instructed,  
“Just do your service.”  
Then you confidently told me,  
“A devotee should be four things:  
a good disciple, a *guru,* a water-carrier, and a cook.”

6\) Once during a morning walk you were asked,  
“Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
was it not difficult for you to always follow  
all the instructions of your spiritual master?”  
You firmly put your cane on the ground:  
“To follow the order of the spiritual master  
is the life and soul of the disciple.”

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for personally instructing your disciples and granddisciples by precepts and example.

You imparted all these wonderful instructions to me during your physical presence. Your wonderful personality, however, is not limited to the brief moment of your stay in this world. Not even death can separate our relationship; only *māyā* can temporarily cover it. I find great solace in your words in the Fourth Canto:

“The disciple and spiritual master are never separated, because the spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master. … When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. As explained before, this means meeting the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the instruction of the spiritual master. This is technically called *vāṇī-sevā*.”

Today I remember your personal care, your love and attention, your patience with me, your inspiration, your constant accessibility, your always giving strength, and countless other qualities.

Please always shower your mercy on this insignificant beggar, and please teach me how to love Kṛṣṇa by always remembering Him and never forgetting Him. If you study my heart carefully, you will find no good qualifications. Instead you will find a coal mine; although the process of my purification in devotional service is going on, still more and more dirt comes out. But I sincerely beg you to never give up on me. I really want to become your good disciple and an instrument in your hands, simply because I love you.

Your most insignificant beggar-disciple,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

To him who called my name …

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I heard you call my name this morning. I was listening to a tape of a morning walk in the summer of 1974, in Germany. You were speaking about transmigration. You asked me to define it. I recited *dehino ’smin yathā dehe,* but you interrupted: “That is *śloka,* but how does it happen?” I tried to explain it in my own words, but again you interrupted: “That is all right, but how does it happen?” Another devotee ventured an explanation. Still you waited. Then I said, “The subtle body carries the soul.”

“Ah, that is it.”

Later on the walk, you exposed the weakness of the opponents’ arguments. They say that they cannot perceive the soul’s transmigration, but there are so many things that we cannot perceive and yet we know exist. For example, you said, “I cannot see your mind or what you are thinking, and you cannot see my mind. But does that mean our minds do not exist? They are so dull they cannot understand. What is the possible objection to this argument?”

And then you said my name. You were asking for an objection from a nondevotee and you said, “Satsvarūpa?”

When I heard it this morning, it touched me. Later, I explained it to a Godbrother and said, “It’s like your lover calling your name.” So much is aroused when a dear one says your name. It’s as if you come into being.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave me that name. You created it. Someone once told me that my name is not saturated with *mādhurya.* It’s a name of God in grandeur, “Kṛṣṇa, the form of truth.” All I know is that it sounds sweet when you say it, when you call me.

When you say my name, it means that you are calling for *me. You* want *me* to do something. During that morning walk, you wanted me to advance an argument so you could smash it. That is how you trained us.

I replied, “I was thinking of a different argument … ”

“What is that?”

I advanced the argument that a person might say he doesn’t care much for transmigration because if you forget everything anyway at death, what’s the difference? So what if you become a dog, if you don’t remember being anything else?

“Then let me kill you right away and you will have to become a dog. Is that all right?”

“No, it’s not all right.”

“Why not? If you forget, there is no problem.”

You called me to action on that morning. I wasn’t always aware why you called me.

You called me to be your servant and secretary. You called my name. Now I can only hope that I will again hear you call my name.

Some people call my name in jest or they say it with a condescending or critical tone. I don’t expect people to *worship* me, but still my name gets passed around like common property. A name on a piece of paper in a hat. A name on a list. One of a number of wrong-doers. One of the “senior devotees.” But it’s never the same as when you say it. Even when you say “Satsvarūpa is expert,” and speak sarcastically about my financial mismanagement, I realize that you have the right to use my name however you desire. I fully trust you. You have named me, and that was not an arbitrary act. You breathed life into my spiritual self.

Satsvarūpa dāsa. That is the name you will remember me by. You won’t say, “Satsvarūpa? I don’t remember. When did he join?”

Or maybe you will. But I can refresh your memory. I shouldn’t think I’m the only Satsvarūpa, just as Brahmā thought he was the only Brahmā. When Lord Brahmā of our universe needed to be further identified, he said, “Tell Kṛṣṇa that it’s the four-headed Brahmā, the father of the Kumāras.”

Similarly, I might say, “Tell him it’s Satsvarūpa dāsa who came to him at 26 Second Avenue in 1966. Tell him I used to type for him and give him the money from my office job. I used to bring him a mango. I went to Boston to open the temple there. Later he awarded me *sannyāsa*.”

Prabhupāda, sometimes you would call for me and give me the instruction to cook *halavā* because the morning was cold. You did that once when you were walking in the Hong Kong hills. You called me and told me to write a book about how all things fail without Kṛṣṇa. You told me to take dictation of a letter that your disciples should read the *Kṛṣṇa* book section on King Nṛga. You called me and asked me to close the curtains in the evening, to tell you how I was doing on the library party, and to go and find someone else for you. You called my name many times over the years, but toward the end, in Vṛndāvana, there was not much external communication between us. You went inside yourself at that time and then you left us here. Maybe you are preaching somewhere with the same gravity and drive that you displayed when you were in this world. Or you are relishing your *līlā* with Lord Caitanya and Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Maybe you will call me to you again.

These thoughts and feelings were triggered off when I heard you call me this morning on that tape. You said it lovingly, expecting me to respond. I was awkward and afraid, but always ready to try and respond. I am still afraid—afraid of offenses and afraid of failing, afraid I may not like what you tell me when you call, afraid I may start to cry. I want so much to be a good disciple, but it’s so hard to be fully and simply surrendered. You know that about me already—not only about me, but about so many of your followers. Still, you keep us with you and call us forward, sometimes one by one and sometimes in groups. You ask us to cooperate and push on Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You call us into action. You call us by our names. It is love, the love of a *guru,* a spiritual father, for his offspring.

Since hearing your call this morning, I am listening more—even as I hear the pleasing sounds of the creek crossing over the old rocks and of the sparrows at dawn—for the sound of you calling my name. When you called me this morning, you put a question mark at the end, although it was a command. Would I actually turn to you? You were confident that I would. It was your way of calling, “Satsvarūpa?”

Yes, Prabhupāda, here I am. I will do the needful.

Your servant,  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Krishna Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord, because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari.**

In the introduction to *Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* you write the following:

“Thus there are two processes by which one may receive knowledge: One depends on the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is situated as the Supersoul within the heart of all living entities, and the other depends upon the *guru,* or spiritual master, who is an expansion of Kṛṣṇa. Thus Kṛṣṇa transmits information both from within and without. We simply have to receive it.”

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 1.58) it is stated, “Since one cannot visually experience the presence of the Supersoul, He appears before us as a liberated devotee. Such a spiritual master is none other than Kṛṣṇa Himself.”

Thus I understand you to be the most intimate servant of the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and as such you are a direct manifestation of the Supersoul and Kṛṣṇa Himself. You are the Servitor Personality of Godhead. Kṛṣṇa speaks from within as *caittya-guru* and from without as you, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Without you there would be only confusion in my life—with you there is hope and direction. My right to serve the Lord I have gotten from you. Without your permission and instruction I could not serve and chant the holy names of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Thus you have empowered me to serve the Lord, but it is not my power—it is yours. You can give unlimitedly, but my power of receiving is limited.

You alone are lighting up the darkness of the night of Kali, just like a full moon. That darkness of night cannot be dispelled by so many stars, but the full moon alone eradicates the darkness and cools down the heat of the day.

In this dark night of Kali-yuga the conditioned souls get overheated by lust, greed, and anger, but by your merciful instructions, like the rays of the full moon, that heat is cooled down and we experience some peace and harmony.

The light of the full moon tends to inspire the hearts of the conditioned souls with amorous feelings towards the opposite sex, but the light of your full moon inspires our hearts with divine love of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Therefore I pay my humble obeisances and *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious day and beg you to have patience with me and bestow your mercy on this endeavoring servant of yours.

Your insignificant servant,  
Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

## Somaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. The sweet sound of the Holy Name comes out of my mouth and enters my ears, and with every word that comes out so much contamination is taken away, and with every word that comes in so much purification is brought inside. One day the hard covering of my heart will crack, and the ocean of love of Godhead will come out.

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. I look at your photo hanging on the wall; you are chanting, your meditation so deep—Kṛṣṇa in your heart, Kṛṣṇa in your mind, Kṛṣṇa on your lips, Kṛṣṇa in your closed eyes, Kṛṣṇa consciousness emanating from you and pervading everywhere. Looking at you, I think of Vṛndāvana. With the mind I fly to Vṛndāvana, and when I get there the sound of *kīrtana* from a group of pilgrims doing Vṛndāvana *parikrama* welcomes me. I look around. Before the gate of the Rādhā-ramaṇa temple, two *sādhus* are talking. I listen. They are talking about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. In the shop of a *mukutwala,* two ladies are choosing crowns for their home Deities. In another shop some ladies are choosing dresses for their Deities. In a brass shop a man is buying a pair of *karatālas* and an *ārotika* plate; in the market a young girl is making garlands that people buy to offer to their Deities. I keep flying over, and in the courtyard of an *āśrama* some *bhaktas* are doing *bhajan;* I hear the sweet sound of the harmonium, the sweet *mṛdaṅgas* and *karatālas, śrī-rūpa-mañjari-pāda …*

I keep flying. In the Yamunā pilgrims are bathing, others are putting on *tilaka*. In Sevakuñja some pilgrims are paying *daṇḍavats.* All over Vṛndāvana the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s name is heard. A *sādhu* is screaming “Rādhe! Rādhe!” At Imlitāla a *sādhu* is silently chanting Gāyatrī, and in a courtyard other *sādhus* are absorbing knowledge from the *śāstra*. At the *samādhi* of Sanātana Gosvāmī, a *svāmī* is lecturing on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. In the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple a pilgrim is looking with deep concentration at the Deities and two *sādhus* are sitting and chanting on *japa* beads. I go in your kitchen, and you are sitting there looking at Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *samādhi* and gently chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. Your golden form is shining and you are meditating on the order of your spiritual master: “Go to the West to preach.” In the middle of that beautiful meditation, from the open window comes the sound of the neighbor’s radio, and the sound of some rock music takes me away from there and I am suddenly thrown West, and I find myself flying over New York City. The ear-splitting sound of a siren welcomes me. It is a fire rescue truck running to try to save a guy who is screaming from a 32nd floor window that he is going to jump. Two drunkards are screaming and yelling bad words at each other. In one corner of the park a young boy is taking drugs. In another corner of the park some boys are raping a young girl; she is screaming but her voice is covered by the siren of a police car that is trying to catch a guy who just killed a man in the bar. In another bar a prostitute is smiling at a client while she takes him to her room. In a shop two ladies are choosing some sexy robes, in the chemist store a teenager is buying contraceptives, and on the street corner a man is buying some lottery tickets. Two boys push an old lady on the ground and take her purse. While I am flying over this horrible scene, my attention is attracted by the sound of the siren of a steam-boat that is entering the harbor. I go there, and from the boat you come out, your body shining, your bead bag on your hand, your lips murmuring “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa … ” A group of reporters come running to you: “Swami, why do you have a shaven head? Where do you get your money? What do you think will happen if all of New York City becomes Hare Kṛṣṇa?” The intoxicated boy from the park comes roaring at you, and you had to run away from him. At this point the sound of the conchshell comes from the temple room, and that sound brings me back to 1995. I go down to the temple room for *ārotika,* and after I sit down to give class I read from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 20.99-104, 108-09):

*nīca jāti, nīca-saṅgī, patita adhama*  
*kuviṣaya-kūpe paḍi’ goṅāinu janama!*

**Sanātana Gosvāmī said, “I was born in a low family, and my associates are all low-class men. I myself am fallen and am the lowest of men. Indeed, I have passed my whole life fallen in the well of sinful materialism.”**

*āpanāra hitāhita kichui nā jāni!*  
*grāmya-vyavahāre paṇḍita, tāi satya māni*

**“I do not know what is beneficial for me and what is detrimental. Nonetheless, in ordinary dealings people consider me a learned scholar, and I am also thinking of myself as such.”**

*kṛpā kari’ yadi more kariyācha uddhāra*  
*āpana-kṛpāte kaha ‘kartavya’ āmāra*

**“Out of Your causeless mercy, You have delivered me from the materialistic path. Now, by the same causeless mercy, please tell me what my duty is.”**

*‘ke āmi’, ‘kene āmāya jāre tāpa-traya’*  
*ihā nāhi jāni—‘kemane hita haya’*

**“Who am I? Why do the threefold miseries always give me trouble? If I do not know this, how can I be benefited?”**

*‘sādhya’-‘sādhana’-tattva puchite nā jāni*  
*kṛpā kari’ saba tattva kaha ta’ āpani”*

**“Actually I do not know how to inquire about the goal of life and the process for obtaining it. Being merciful upon me, please explain all these truths.”**

*jīvera ‘svarūpa’ haya—kṛṣṇera ‘nitya-dāsa’*  
*kṛṣṇera ‘taṭasthā-śakti’ ‘bhedābheda-prakāśa’*  
*sūryāṁśa-kiraṇa, yaiche agni-jvālā-caya*  
*svābhāvika kṛṣṇera tina-prakāra ‘śakti’ haya*

**“It is the living entity’s constitutional position to be an eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa because he is the marginal energy of Kṛṣṇa and a manifestation simultaneously one with and different from the Lord, like a molecular particle of sunshine or fire. Kṛṣṇa has three varieties of energy.”**

PURPORT: Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura paraphrases these verses as follows: Śrī Sanātana Gosvāmī asked Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “Who am I?” In answer, the Lord replied, “You are a pure living entity. You are neither the material body nor the subtle body composed of mind and intelligence. Actually you are a spirit soul, eternal part and parcel of the Supreme Soul, Kṛṣṇa. Therefore you are His eternal servant.”

After class I looked at your *mūrti* sitting on the *vyāsāsana,* and I said, “Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I thank you from the depth of my heart for coming to this horrible material world, to this dark Western world, to bring the light, to bring the nectar of nectar, the holy name of the Lord.” Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. In this dark age and in the Western materialistic society, no human could succeed in doing what you have done. Five hundred years ago in India, the Supreme Personality of Godhead Lord Caitanya advented in order to spread the Holy Name with all his eternal associates. But in the Western world five hundred years later, you alone did the gigantic task. There is no doubt that you are a very intimate eternal associate of the Lord, and out of your causeless mercy you have come in an empowered manifestation to give light to the darkest regions. It is only because of your causeless mercy that a wretch like me had the opportunity to have your saintly association. Just by seeing you one gets purified from all sins, what to speak of hearing you, serving you, washing your feet. And you so mercifully walked all over this fallen world to give millions this opportunity. Please accept millions of my humble obeisances. And millions of times I say, “Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming; without your coming we would be in complete darkness.”

Your humble servant,  
Somaka Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I was passing my days in mirth and joy,  
without any thought or care,  
Looking for happiness here and there,  
Wherever it might appear—  
In the thrill of a gambling match  
Or the crowd at a rock concert,  
In the company of a woman  
Or the high in a party with drugs,  
In the taste of the flesh of beasts  
Or the flavor of wine.  
I was rushing willy nilly  
To enjoy this life of mine.

But with all the pleasure I was able to squeeze  
From my youth, with its delights,  
I still was left with a horrible thought:  
“My life is going for naught, simply going for naught.”  
“Something is wrong, something is wrong,”  
A voice inside me said.  
“How can life be meant for this?”  
It echoed in my head.  
Though at the time I was in great deception,  
This thought led me toward the right conception  
Because it made me stop and ponder,  
“Who I am, and why do I suffer?”

Then by some unknown good fortune  
I had the chance to meet  
A disciple of yours, so sincere,  
In an encounter on the street.  
He told me a little about you and  
The glories of the holy name,  
And made me promise to come and see  
If I would feel the same.

I ventured a chance and came  
To your temple on the Main.  
Little did I know at the time  
that I would never be the same.  
The chanting and dancing were sweet, and  
The *prasādam* an outstanding treat.  
And the devotees were so kind  
That they made me feel nice and sublime,  
And when the *Bhagavad-gītā* was spoken,  
I was happily obliged to admit  
That grasping this knowledge of spirit  
Was bound to make one fit.

And Kṛṣṇa too was explained, with  
His wonderful nature and fame,  
So that anyone could see that He  
Was the Supreme Personality.  
But the best was yet to come,  
For the glories of the Lord’s devotee  
Was now to be sung.

The devotee of the Lord is even kinder than the Lord,  
And in your person it was clearly seen  
That this was fact indeed.  
For in old age you dared to come  
To the land of the setting sun,  
Where ignorance and passion  
were held in fashion  
And Māyā ruled all as she pleased.  
And you wrote a wonderful poem  
As you arrived on the Western shore,  
Which revealed your sweet and meek nature,  
Your love for the Lord and more.  
Then you struggled alone for a year  
And lived with the hippies without fear  
So that ISKCON could be founded—  
A house for all the world.

With the spirit of a youth  
And the patience of a saint,  
You worked tirelessly and selflessly  
To see your ISKCON grow.  
And you translated your books  
In the middle of the night,  
Just to give vision  
To those without sight.  
And you printed them and distributed them  
To all, both left and right,  
Thus fulfilling the order  
Of your spiritual master,  
To his great and full delight.

And you opened centers everywhere  
And traveled to make them strong.  
And trained up your disciples  
And taught them right from wrong.  
And you formed a GBC  
So that ISKCON would remain,  
And told us in your final days  
To cooperate the same.

Then you left to rejoin Kṛṣṇa,  
But still we know you’re there  
And always looking down on us  
With tender loving care.  
Your glories go on and on,  
But my ability is small.  
To praise you in full measure  
Is not within my call.  
But there is a little thing  
That I did not mention yet:  
You saved me, you saved me—  
I’m eternally in your debt.

Your insignificant servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Varṣānā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories unto you.

In this dark and dreadful age, when irreligion reigns, nothing short of a deluge of mercy could save this world from the clutches of Kali. As the world becomes more and more infested with multitudes of “isms” based on bodily concepts and the principle of scarcity, the friction of separate conflicting interests causes envy, hatred, fire, and war. And as the artificial takes the place of the real on all levels of existence, where is the truth to be found?

The truth, Lord Caitanya informs us, is found in the heart of an unalloyed devotee of Kṛṣṇa. How timely it was for you to descend on this darkest night of our soul. It was only due to your effulgent appearance in this world that the horizon of our prospects began to brighten.

Finding the world situation beyond redemption by the efforts of man or *avatāra,* you preached the glories of the holy name as the only antidote to the perils of the age. Like nectar, your words revived us from the snakebite of death, the poison of Kali. You brought Kṛṣṇa into our lives and showed practically that by watering the root of all existence by *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana,* every part and parcel will be properly nourished. You emphasized love and trust between your disciples, knowing that by loving Kṛṣṇa we would love each other, too.

You brought the spirit of Vṛndāvana to this darkened world. You created a replica of Goloka where men and animals, gods and nature, could work together for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa, each with love and trust for one another.

You taught us to sacrifice false ego in the fire of *saṅkīrtana,* creating harmonious song in glorification of Kṛṣṇa. You brought Kṛṣṇa into our lives because He lives and plays in your heart.

Like the rising sun of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* you caused the lotuslike hearts of devotees to blossom. And then, you left us—or so it seemed at first. The sun who used to enliven the lotus now began to burn instead when the delicate flower was bereft of its shelter. The reservoir seemed dry without the rains of mercy, and the new blossoms all began to wither.

And yet, if we endure that separation as our final test, then so many lessons will uplift our consciousness. If we can only stand firm and fearless upon the memory of our *gurudeva,* taking your instructions to heart, then we enter into deeper communion with you.

And whatever difficulties enter our lives will be overcome by keeping you there—in the center of our lives, in the center of our community, where we worship you as the founder-*ācārya* for the next 10,000 years. And your words form the lawbooks for the whole *prema-yuga*.

So now, rather than offering you flowery words, let us offer you our hearts made pure like the lotus. May the nectar of your words and the remembrance of your pastimes penetrate to the core of our hearts, making them clean, bright, and holy. Then only will your lotus feet be properly enshrined there. Then only will we understand the Absolute Truth, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. And then only will our words and deeds have potency to continue the spiritual legacy which you so kindly bequeathed to us.

May we serve you eternally in spreading the cooling rays of your mercy. Let there be, as you desired, from one extremity of the world to the other, one religion, one purpose, one great philosophy of spiritual equality to embrace all souls in its cooling rays. In that great mission of Lord Gaurāṅga, we pray to remain your servant eternally.

Varṣānā Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**Is This Māyā, Śrīla Prabhupāda?**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble *sahasra-praṇāmas* in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

It is with great humility and confusion that I approach you on this most auspicious occasion of the observance of the 99th anniversary of your appearance day.

*kārpaṇya-doṣopahata-svabhāvaḥ*  
*pṛcchāmi tvāṁ dharma-sammūḍha-cetāḥ*  
*yac chreyaḥ syān niścitaṁ brūhi tan me*  
*śiṣyas te ’haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam*  
*(Bg 2.7)*

Somewhere deep down, within me, there is a great storm taking place. Occasionally this storm generates a big wave, and I become overwhelmed and cannot speak properly. Then I become embarrassed and think I have become a showbottle devotee.

“In India we have got some showbottles in the medicine shop. Some red water put into the big bottle and with electric light. That means advertisement: ‘Here is a bottle of medicine.’ But that is a showbottle, red water. So red water will not cure the disease. You must have really, actually, a mixture, fever mixture. But that fever mixture is very difficult” (*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, Sept. 14, 1966)

Aside from that, I am not overwhelmed with love of Kṛṣṇa. Rather, I am confused and bewildered. I must be in *māyā,* because I am getting conflicting messages. Therefore, I am approaching you, for you are the representative of the Lord.

On the one hand, you once wrote in a letter to Śatadhanya on the 20th of February, 1972, “So far personal association with the Guru is concerned, I was only with my Guru Maharaja four or five times, but I have never left his association, not even for a moment. Because I am following his instructions, I have never felt any separation.”

On the other hand, this statement of yours completely bewilders me, because I do feel the pinch of separation from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Is that *māyā*? Does it mean that I am not following your instructions? Strangely, when I follow your instructions diligently, the feeling increases, it does not diminish. Someone please help me, for I am suffering. If I talk about it I become choked. Is it *māyā* for me to tell it here, Śrīla Prabhupāda? I am not telling it for that reason. I am telling it because I am confused. *Śiṣyas te ’haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam*. When I become afraid, I neglect it out of fear of the depths that might lurk there. I think it is too painful for me to bear. Mostly, when I go about my daily duties, I become forgetful. If I act foolishly, I become covered. But sooner or later it comes back to haunt me when I least expect it—in a class, speaking about separation from Kṛṣṇa, I am reminded of your pastimes, and I have to stop. Embarrassed. It was not for show. It “boiled over.” I tried to conceal.

What is this? Philosophically, I know I am feeling the pain of separation from my Gurudeva. But, Prabhupāda, you said you never felt any separation. “I have never felt any separation.” I feel separation from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I am not proud of it. I am ashamed of myself. But it does not go away. It increases. Is this *māyā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda?

I open the Vyāsa-pūjā book from last year to see what my Godbrothers have written. A dear *sannyāsī* Godbrother wrote, “Yes, O dear Śrīla Gurudeva, you have never left us. You, out of your causeless mercy, eternally remain with us, watch us, correct us, guide us, and lead us on the ever-illuminated path of perfect spiritual realization.” I believe that you are still with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda; I understand this part of our philosophy quite well enough.

But still I lament. Oh, what I would give to see you walk into the temple room once again, surrounded by devotees! I would stand in the back, unseen and unheard, in total bliss, tears in my eyes. Excuse me for saying so, but who cares for all this philosophy? Is that *māyā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda?

Sometimes, I also feel your presence when I follow your instructions. But I can’t say, as you said, “I have never felt any separation,” because as soon as I start reading or remembering your pastimes, I become choked up. For me this statement “I have never felt any separation” is not a reality. Is that *māyā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Bitter and sweet, all rolled into one. Tears of joy, and yet sadness too. There are no words to express how I feel. So, let me turn to your books. You are present in your books. I always knew that. I get shelter from them. I can turn to find you there.

“A pure devotee of the Lord of the standard of Uddhava constantly associates with the Lord in the double perception of simultaneous separation and meeting” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.4.21, purport). I am obviously not a pure devotee, and certainly not of the standard of Uddhava. So this is irrelevant. But I can certainly begin to understand this point of “double perception of simultaneous separation and meeting.” That must be why you never felt separation from your Guru Mahārāja. That must be why my Godbrothers also do not feel separation. They are doubly perceiving simultaneous separation and meeting. And because I am not so advanced, I am feeling this pinch of separation. Is that understanding *māyā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda?

And also, am I all alone here in this feeling of separation?
