Among my many Godbrothers and Godsisters, are they all feeling your presence more and more, leaving me alone to rot in this material world, birth after birth, forgotten and withered up? Will all be reunited with you, but I alone will be left behind, eternally lamenting my pitiable plight?

I don’t have any feelings of separation from Śrī Kṛṣṇa, but only from you, His dearmost devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Is that feeling *māyā*? I always thought that attachment to the pure devotee of the Lord was as good as attachment to the Lord Himself. And therefore, if I study the science of separation, I find the following passage: “The spontaneous attraction of Śrī Kṛṣṇa for His dearest parts and parcels generates an enthusiasm that obliges Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the *gopīs* to meet together. To celebrate this transcendental enthusiasm, there is need of a sentiment of separation between the lover and beloved” (Cc. *Ādi* 4.31, purport). So never mind the *gopīs* or taking shelter of “other” *śikṣā-gurus* that teach of the *gopīs—*all I want is to develop my unalloyed devotion to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I want to expand that feeling of separation to celebrate this transcendental enthusiasm. Is that desire *māyā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda?

“Is my desire for exclusive devotion to you unrealistic?” wrote another *sannyāsī* Godbrother in last year’s Vyāsa-pūjā book. “Well, what can I say? I desire it.” I like that mood, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are my lord and master. You are my only shelter. You are the object of my devotion. You are the object of love of Rādhā and Vṛndāvanacandra. I only heard rumors about Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda. But you I know something about. I saw you with my own two eyes. You touched me, externally and internally. You personally placed the *brāhmaṇa* thread on my useless body and thereby accepted this insignificant, doglike worm as your disciple, and you touched me internally in such a way that my life is eternally changed. Though I was a nobody in your ever-expanding movement, you took the time to personally encourage me several times in Vṛndāvana, and sent me out to preach.

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I weep for want of your association again! I yearn to be with you again, even though I know you have never left us. Is that *māyā*? Why am I so deficient that I am feeling this separation so deeply at times, when you never felt separation from your Guru Maharaja?

And it was in this mood that I decided to alter one verse originally written by Lord Caitanya. It goes like this, please forgive me: “I know no one but Śrīla Prabhupāda as my lord, and he shall remain so even if he handles me roughly in his embrace or makes me brokenhearted by not being present before me. He is completely free to do anything and everything, for he is always a servant of his worshipful Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, unconditionally.” Is that *māyā,* Śrīla Prabhupāda? Because if it is, I’m a hopeless case. Please instruct me. And if you agree I am such a completely lost case, please don’t hesitate to come personally to instruct me. I won’t complain a bit.

Your doglike disciple,  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which are especially designed to give cooling shade to all the afflicted souls in the material world. Your lotus feet derive their cooling power from the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda, which are as cool as ten million full moons (*nitāi-pada-kamala, koṭī-candra-suśītala*).

The most secretly guarded hidden treasure of love of Kṛṣṇa is available to those who take shelter of His confidential devotee who preaches his glories to the fallen conditioned souls.

*Kṛṣṇa yadi chuṭe bhukti mukti diyā*. “Kṛṣṇa, if pleased, may easily bestow heavenly pleasures and liberation upon the aspiring candidate, but pure devotional service He keeps hidden.”

In this horrible age of Kali it is hard to find a person, even one in a thousand, who knows what is sinful and what is not sinful. What to speak, then, of knowing pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the activity of the pure, sinless heart. Such pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness is available only through the mercy of a pure devotee.

*mahat-kṛpā vinā kona krame bhakti naya*  
*kṛṣṇa bhakti dūre raha saṁsāra nahe kṣaya*

**“Unless one is favored by a pure devotee, he can not attain the platform of devotional service. To say nothing of** ***kṛṣṇa-bhakti,*** **one cannot even be relieved from the bondage of material existence.”**

So if there is a single most powerful factor that is responsible for one’s becoming a devotee of the Lord, that is *mahat-kṛpā,* the mercy of a pure devotee. Therefore, to meet or recognize such a pure devotee, appreciate him and surrender to him fully, is indeed the success of one’s life. It is so kind of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you have trained us and explained to us the science of recognizing a pure devotee.

Even though your heart is always dripping with the mellows of loving ecstasy, even though your eyes are always decorated with tears of love and your throat chokes up while chanting the holy name, you covered all your ecstasies just to train us, your infant children, how to distinguish a genuine saint from a charlatan meditator. It was always a great joy to see you and hear you blasting and devastating bogus *gurus,* Māyāvādīs, atheistic scientists, characterless politicians, and senseless scholars. Your frequent use of words such as “rascals,” “demons,” “men like cats and dogs,” and “hogs,” “frogs,” “jackals,” and “asses” is just as sanctified as the “Prayers of Personified *Vedas”* and the *Puruṣa-sūkta* hymn. Here in India we often see persons chanting the *Viṣṇu-sahasra-nāma* and even the *Bhagavad-gītā* without believing that Viṣṇu or Kṛṣṇa is actually the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

You protected us by repeatedly warning us against so many likely impediments on the path of pure devotional service, yet any of us, even today, can still be overpowered by bogus philosophy and bogus ideas, as well as be defeated by unbridled senses and material desires. In one conversation you said, “Most dangerous … Those who are neophytes, they will always be in danger … just like a child is always in danger. So how you can save them? He is always in danger. So as far as possible, let us try. He is going to the fire. He is going to the water. He’s going to the animal. He’s eating some poison. So always in danger. The childish age is dangerous. Therefore mother takes care. Danger is already there because he is neophyte, *kaniṣṭha-adhikārī*. Therefore we have to abide by the injunction of the *śāstra* and guided by *guru*. That’s all. That is our secure position. And otherwise danger always. … Now I’ll say from my practical life … it is not pride. Actually everyone knows that my Guru Mahārāja had thousands of disciples. So out of thousands of disciples, practically I am little successful. That everyone knows. Why? Because I firmly believed in the words of my *guru*. That’s all.”

Yes, you demonstrated by your practical example how to recognize and take full shelter of a pure devotee, and you also demonstrated what wonderful results await the sincere soul who does this.

It is impossible to measure the depth of the heart of a pure devotee or to know the magnitude of his emotions, but we know one thing: you are an ocean of mercy. You constantly meditated on how to bring to life *śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭam,* how to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya to inundate the whole world with love of God. The mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is wonderfully described by Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 9.34-41), where he quotes Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself:

“‘I am the only gardener. How many places can I go? How many fruits can I pick and distribute? It would certainly be a very laborious task to pick the fruits and distribute them alone, and still I suspect that some would receive them and others would not.

“‘Therefore I order every man within this universe to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and distribute it everywhere. I am the only gardener. If I do not distribute these fruits, what shall I do with them? How many fruits can I alone eat? By the transcendental desire of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, water has been sprinkled all over the tree, and thus there are innumerable fruits of love of Godhead. Distribute this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Let people eat these fruits and ultimately become free from old age and death.

“‘If the fruits are distributed all over the world, My reputation as a pious man will be known everywhere, and thus all people will glorify My name with great pleasure. One who has taken his birth as a human being in the land of India \[Bhārata-varṣa\] should make his life successful and work for the benefit of all other people.’”

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, hundreds of millions of people have been born in Bhārata-varṣa, but you alone have fulfilled the order of Lord Caitanya: *janma sārthaka kari’ kara para-upakāra*. This is your mission, This is your *mantra*. *Prāṇinām upakāraya*. The following two verses are my favorites because they describe your personality perfectly:

*etāvāj janma-sāphalyaṁ dehinām iha dehiṣu*  
*prāṇair arthair dhiyā vācā śreya-ācaraṇaṁ sadā*

**“It is the duty of every living being to perform welfare activities for the benefit of others with his life, wealth, intelligence, and words” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.22.35).**

*praṇiṇām upakāraya yad eveha paratra ca*  
*karmaṇā manasā vācā tad eva matimān bhajet*

**“By his work, thoughts and words, an intelligent man must perform actions which will be beneficial for all living entities in this life and next” (*****Viṣṇu Purāṇa*** **3.12.45).**

Lord Caitanya is indeed *mahā-vadānya-avatāra,* the most munificent incarnation in the history of mankind; who else could even dream of such exalted ideas (Cc. *Ādi-līlā* 9.44–48):

“‘I am merely a gardener. I have neither a kingdom nor very great riches. I simply have some fruits and flowers that I wish to utilize to achieve piety in My life. Although I am acting as a gardener, I also want to be the tree, for thus I can bestow benefit upon all.

“‘“Just see how these trees are maintaining every living entity! Their birth is successful. Their behavior is just like that of great personalities, for anyone who asks anything from a tree never goes away disappointed.”’

“The descendants of the tree \[the devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu\] were very glad to receive this order directly from the Lord. The fruit of love of God is so tasteful that wherever a devotee distributes it, those who relish the fruit anywhere in the world immediately become intoxicated.”

When the Lord eats something, the remnants of His food are called *mahā-prasādam,* and when a pure devotee eats this *mahā-prasādam,* his remnants are called *mahā-mahā-prasādam*. Similarly, Lord Caitanya is *mahā-vadānya* and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are *mahā-mahā-vadānya*.

We do not glorify you only because you are our “sectarian *guru*” but because all the scriptures glorify a devotee who takes risks on behalf of the Lord, who, like a tree, sacrifices everything for the benefit of others. We would only be ungrateful *guru-drohī* if we did not try to properly broadcast your glories. In your pastime as *jagad-guru* for twelve short years, somehow or other you and Kṛṣṇa picked us as your servitors. We witnessed this most wonderful event of history, your preaching, which is evidence of the efficacy of pure devotional service, a most magnificent display of the potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. If we do not appreciate this good fortune, which is rare even for Lord Brahmā and Lord Śiva, who would be more unfortunate than us? As Locana dāsa Ṭhākura says, *saṁsāre majjila rahila paḍiya / se pade nahila āśa / āpana karama bhuñjaya samāna / kahaye locana dāsa*.” (“Due to our own past misdeeds, we have remained absorbed in materialistic life and not developed attachment for your lotus feet.”)

Since next year marks the hundredth anniversary of your appearance in this world, we beg you to give a few drops of your mercy so that in the mood of Prahlāda Mahārāja we may never forget how you and only you have saved us from *prabhavāhi-kūpa,* the blind well full of snakes, which is our material life of repeated birth and death:

*evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe*  
*kāmābhikāmam anu yaḥ prapatan prasaṅgāt*  
*kṛtvātmasāt surarṣiṇā bhagavan gṛhītaḥ*  
*so ’haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sevām*

**“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?”**

By some unprecedented good fortune, the seed of this *prāṇinām upakāraya* consciousness has been planted in our hearts by Your Divine Grace. But the flood of our material desires is not allowing this auspicious seed to sprout. Today on this auspicious day, we humbly pray and seek your mercy so that the flood of material desires—which are always placing obstacles against our desire to execute your orders—may become converted to the flood of spiritual desires of love of Godhead and destroy the seed of fruitive activities in our heart.

Please give us your blessings so that we may complete the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Temple and Cultural Centre and present it to you as a Centennial offering. We would also like to complete the translation of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* into Gujarati so we can offer it to you as a Centennial gift.

Thank you very much for extending your most merciful divine lotus feet for us to take fearless shelter. May we remain there eternally, life after life.

Your servants in ISKCON Ahmedabad, India.

## Albuquerque

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We are truly thankful to you for bringing Lord Caitanya’s mission of *harināma-saṅkīrtana* to the West. Out of gratitude and indebtedness we humbly offer ISKCON Albuquerque’s Harināma Cintāmaṇi Dhāma and organic environmental farm project to you for your upcoming Centennial celebration.

Thank you for allowing us to serve the Vaiṣṇava family that you have created out of love for your Guru Mahārāja.

Your eternal servants in Albuquerque, New Mexico.

## Athens

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Unlimited glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Śrī Śrī Gurudeva Jayate! Why are there two “Śrī’s”? Because there are meant to be unlimited “Śrī’s” attached to your name. Two are there to indicate unlimited.

*Śrī* means “opulence.” *Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma*. At your lotus feet reside all opulences and mystic and spiritual perfections, given to you directly by the goddess of fortune.

*Śrī* means “treasure.” You are the embodiment of the great unlimited treasure of *prema-bhakti*.

*Śrī* means “beauty.” *Sākṣād-dharitvena*. You are as beautiful as Kṛṣṇa.

*Śrī* means your desires are as beautiful as Kṛṣṇa’s. And your desire is that all conditioned souls regain their eternal beauty at the Lord’s lotus feet.

Because you have the good qualities of Kṛṣṇa, you are also the most humble. You see everyone as serving Kṛṣṇa—except yourself. In your quarters in New York City in 1967 you once told me, from *The Nectar of Devotion,* how the *jīvas* who are pure devotees have 78% of Kṛṣṇa’s qualities. So I smiled and said, “So that means you have 78% of His qualities?”

You looked surprised. “Me?” you questioned. “I am a conditioned soul.”

In the same year you asked one of your newly initiated and newly married disciples to shave his head. His wife objected at first but finally surrendered. “You are the devotee,” she said. “Whatever you say is all right.”

You responded in a soft and serious voice. “No. You are also a devotee. What you decide is also all right.”

In the same year I gave you some praise. Your response was again so humble. “Your praising me is out of love, just like when a mother has a child who is blind. Out of love she calls her child ‘lotus-eyed.’”

*Tṛṇāpekhā ati hīna*—please make us also humble. *Sakala sahane*—please remove all the obstacles to our serving you. *Nija-māne spṛhā-hīna*—without any material desires or aspirations; so we can successfully preach your mission.

Your servants in Athens, Ohio.  
(written by Jadurānī dāsī)

## Atlanta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Let us offer our most humble obeisances unto our revered spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda! All glories to your lotus feet!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your limitless mercy you have picked us up from the morass of materialism and selfishness. We had fallen so deeply into the clutches of the illusory energy of the Lord that any hope of alleviating the pangs of suffering and despair were nil. Mere words cannot convey the gratitude we owe you. A thousand mouths cannot adequately enunciate your accomplishments with the proper justice. Only Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself could demonstrate the importance of respecting and worshiping the spiritual master, as stated in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam (*11.17.27): “One should know the *ācārya* as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.”

You lovingly, patiently taught us the glories of chanting the holy names of the Lord. Only by following your instructions can we properly follow the path of devotional service and self-realization. We stumbled at your lotus feet and begged for your benevolence, although it is quite clear, by our continuous attachment to the various attractions of material life, that we lack any merit. Yet your cooling glance and soothing words ceaselessly stream at our ungrateful selves.

On this most auspicious anniversary of the appearance day of Your Divine Grace, let us reaffirm our vows once more, with tenacious resolution to dedicate our every breath to following your instructions. Let us prostrate snugly under your lotus feet, dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, entreating you for lasting guidance and indulgence of our feeble attempts to carry Lord Caitanya’s sublime *saṅkīrtana* movement into the hearts of all mankind. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Imploring to remain your most humble servants,  
The devotees of greater New Pānihāṭi Dhāma, Atlanta, Georgia.

## Auckland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

To my eternal spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on behalf of the growing devotee community here in Auckland, thank you for giving us Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your first temple at 26 Second Avenue on the Lower East side of New York was formerly a gift shop called “Matchless Gifts.” How transcendentally appropriate that name was!

Your Divine Grace visited Auckland several times. You personally installed Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī in 1972, and then in 1976 Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, along with Jagannātha, Balarāma, and Subhadrā. At that time you asked the temple president, Yaśomatīnandana dāsa, why he wanted to install Lord Jagannātha. He replied, caught off guard, “So that we can have Ratha-yātrā, Śrīla Prabhupāda.” Before agreeing to install the Deities you made him promise that this would indeed take place. This year we observed the tenth annual Ratha-yātrā Festival here in Auckland, with devotees from all over New Zealand and Australia attending.

On another of your visits here you encouraged representatives from the Indian community to help your young Western disciples build a wonderful “Vṛndāvana-style temple” here in Auckland. We are pleased to say that during your Centennial year we hope to move the Deities to Their new home—Bhakti-bhavana. This will certainly require a major effort from the entire devotee community, as there is much work to do. In order to fulfill this instruction of Your Divine Grace, a groundswell of spiritual enthusiasm is rising to help us overcome the final obstacles.

On other preaching fronts, devotees eager to serve and glorify Your Divine Grace are organizing the biggest summer festival tour ever. It will begin in January 1996, last for six weeks, and visit dozens of cities and towns in the middle and lower North Island. An ongoing Pada-yātrā, as well as Ratha-yātrās in Auckland, Christchurch, and perhaps other cities, are just a few of the ways we hope to enthuse people with an awareness that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa are indeed their greatest well-wishers.

This struggling son of yours hopes that he will also be caught up in this tidal wave of blissful Prabhupāda consciousness and drown happily in the midst of your devotees as they chant “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!”

Hoping one day or lifetime to be so fortunate, we beg to remain your eternal servants,  
The devotees at New Varshan, Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Ālālanātha dāsa)

## Baltimore

On behalf of the Baltimore temple community, I offer my respectful obeisances to the lotus feet of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, on the auspicious anniversary day of his appearance in the material world. Had he not appeared on this blessed day, my whereabouts would be uncertain, and very likely I would not be in my present body.

Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā means various things to various devotees. This is a time to remember Śrīla Prabhupāda’s manifest pastimes, glorifying his achievements and enlightening gifts for the world’s welfare. It is a time to celebrate, to give thanks for being recipients of his kindness and mercy, and to reaffirm our commitment to serving his mission.

To me, this is especially a time to reflect on my relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda and my strengths and weaknesses in following his instructions. I feel the urge to get out of my usual routine, to adopt a mood of intense inner reflection, to ask myself piercing questions, and to overturn stones in hidden recesses of my consciousness that I would rather not look at.

Since Śrīla Prabhupāda is the representative of Kṛṣṇa, what can be unknown to him? I am as if naked before him, yet I understand that in order to really make advancement—to assess the type of devotional medicine I must take and what strategies I should adopt at different times—I also must be able to be naked before myself. Of course, this is not such an easy task. This should, however, be a goal we are always striving and praying for, because the false ego has its cherished conditioned views of who we are. Either I think I am more advanced than I truly am, or else, due to my absorption in a totally negative view of myself, I don’t see any positive qualities that Kṛṣṇa has given me. In the first case I may assume some position I am not fit for, and in the second case I won’t see Kṛṣṇa’s mercy when it is standing right before me (I speak from my personal experience).

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I aspire to take shelter at your lotus feet! As young boys have an attraction for young girls and young girls have an attraction for young boys, please let me be attracted to serving you in that same spontaneous way. Throughout the day, in all circumstances of work, play, family, temple service, etc., please let me remember you—your mercy, instructions, and love for me. Please allow me to love you!

Let me understand the temporary nature of material wealth, fame, and enjoyment. Let me understand that at any moment I may be forced to leave everything behind, including my most dear material body, my field of enjoyment. In all my affairs please let me remember that at the time of death I must review my life again in its entirety: I must re-experience that unkind word to another, that violence or abuse to my spouse, child, friend, or disciple, that sinful activity, that secret theft, that selfish or greedy mentality, that offensive mood or action, that wasted moment—and perhaps … yes … that wasted life!

Oh, no!—not another birth in the world, born ignorant to a new set of parents, another childhood, more pain and misery, unfulfilled desires and new temptations. Certainly I will continue in devotional service where I left off, but there are no guarantees about how I will respond to my material environment—it’s better not to take a chance! O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please save me from that disappointment at the time of death, that incomplete grade with the necessity to repeat another life. Please save me from my causeless unwillingness to serve. Please save me from my lower nature of all descriptions, from my enviousness of the greatness of Kṛṣṇa and you, from my rebelliousness and my desire to be a competitor lord.

*sthitiḥ sevā gatir yātrā smṛtiś cintā stutir vacaḥ*  
*bhūyāt sarvātmanā viṣṇo madīyaṁ tvayi ceṣṭitam*

**“Whatever inauspicious things I have done out of ignorance or unknowingly, please forgive that, and accept me as Your insignificant servant. Let my normal condition be service, let my movements be holy pilgrimage, let my thoughts be remembrance of You, let my words be glorification of You. O Viṣṇu \[and His representative, Śrīla Prabhupāda\], let my activities, with my whole mind and body and soul, be engaged in You.”**

*kṛṣṇo mamaiva sarvatra svasti kuryāt śriyā samam*  
*tathaiva ca sadā kārṣṇiḥ sarva-vighna-vināśanaḥ*

**“May Lord Kṛṣṇa, along with His beloved Rādhā, bring about prosperity and auspiciousness at all times. In the same way, may the devotee of Kṛṣṇa \[Śrīla Prabhupāda\], who is able to destroy all obstacles, always bring about auspiciousness” (*****Viṣṇu-yāmala-saṁhitā*****).**

O divine master, in the prelude to your Centennial celebration, your movement and followers need more than ever to rally around your healing and unifying personality. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please inspire us with compassion, empathy, understanding, and love for one another so we can overcome our obsession with being “right” in the name of following you. Although it is improbable that all different camps of opinions will ever come to the same view, at least we should not be warring factions. Whether this “*vik*” or that “*vik,*” this *ācārya* or that *guru,* this conclusive verse or that one, let us please embrace unity in diversity, and together appreciate your glories as the preeminent *ācārya* who introduced and spread Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world. Please, Prabhupāda, give us faith in the possible, in the miraculous, and in that which is beyond our conception.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! May your glories be sung eternally throughout the material and spiritual worlds!

Your aspiring servants in New Kulīna-grāma Dhāma, Baltimore, Maryland.  
(written by Karṇāmṛta dāsa)

## Barcelona

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**The Great Opportunity**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Another year has passed, and we are here once more taking this opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

But we know that the Vyāsa-pūjā day is not the only opportunity to glorify you. A devotee should see every day as an opportunity to do something for you, to speak about you, to better follow your teachings, etc.

The more we dedicate ourselves to your mission, the more we will take advantage of these opportunities, and the more we take advantage of these opportunities, the closer we will feel to you and the better we will understand you and strongly feel your presence.

But there is now an opportunity that will be unique in our lifetimes and that none of your followers must waste. This opportunity is open to all congregational devotees as well, without consideration of sex, social position, or spiritual advancement. Your disciples especially have the moral obligation to participate. What unfortunate devotee will not take the opportunity to participate in your Centennial?

Actually, the Centennial is not an opportunity—it is “the great opportunity.” And because we are very close to 1996, the year of the great opportunity, we want to show our love for you by co-operating together. That is why we want to unite the family of Śrīla Prabhupāda, knowing that the Centennial is the “great opportunity.” We want to see all the family co-operating with the Centennial preparations, because the Centennial is “the great opportunity.”

We want that somehow or other all the devotees participate in the activities for 1996 (and whoever has pronounced your name even once is a devotee) because the Centennial is “the great opportunity.”

We want to acknowledge the service that all the devotees have offered to your movement, because the Centennial is “the great opportunity.”

Because Vaiṣṇavas are not ungrateful, we want to thank all the devotees for the service they offered in the past, and the Centennial is “the great opportunity.”

Because we really want to acknowledge the efforts and dedication of so many devotees over the years to distribute your books, cook, or perform other services, we will look everywhere for those Vaiṣṇavas and thank them personally, because the Centennial is “the great opportunity.”

We want to bring back all those older devotees and reunite them, as Lord Caitanya used to do with His associates, and explain to all of them the qualities of each of them, because the Centennial is the “great opportunity.”

The least we can do is try to enthuse and engage in your Centennial all the devotees who are lost all over Spain, because this is “the great opportunity.”

Because it is our moral duty, we will contact all those devotees who have been forgotten, because this is “the great opportunity.”

Because they have credit from the past and this is “the great opportunity” to look for them, we will find them and speak to them about the Centennial.

Because the flame of devotional service can decrease but never be extinguished, your Centennial is “the great opportunity” to help rekindle this flame in all members of your family.

Because we already are experiencing some benefits of uniting Prabhupāda’s family, we emphasize again and again that this is “the great opportunity.”

Because to unite “Prabhupāda’s family” benefits not only the devotees, but all the society at large, this is “the great opportunity.”

Because ISKCON will become strong, it is “the great opportunity.”

Because as devotees we need the medicine of the Centennial, this is “the great opportunity.”

To unite Prabhupāda’s family means that the devotees from the temples look for and meet the devotees who are living outside, and the Centennial is “the great opportunity.”

Because we want to hear the ideas and opinions of all the devotees for celebrating your Centennial, this is “the great opportunity”.

Because ISKCON is indebted to so many devotees for the service they performed, this is “the great opportunity” to repay them.

Because the devotees who stopped practicing devotional service never forgot you, we must look for them and make them participate in the Centennial, because this is “the great opportunity”.

Because we now have so much nectar in Spanish, in the form of your books, tapes, magazines, and films, this is “the great opportunity” to invite all members of Prabhupāda’s family to swim in this transcendental ocean.

Because within ourselves we always wanted to reunite Prabhupāda’s family but never knew how to do it, this is “the great opportunity”.

We thank the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, because he sent you to save us and because now we have so near at hand “the great opportunity,” which we will surely not waste.

Your servants at ISKCON Barcelona, Spain.  
(written by Sādhyaśreṣṭha dāsa)

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet again and again.

As the years pass, we continue to hold fast to your teachings and instructions despite the difficulties in doing so. The false attraction for cheap, so-called spiritual advancement is not a new phenomenon. You boldly attacked the bogus *yogīs* and so-called *gurus* for making a show of being liberated and pretending to be God.

Your clear instructions to stay spiritually strong remain in our minds and hearts. You told us, “This is the difference between our movement and the other, so-called spiritual movements. We have got spiritual strength. Chant sixteen rounds, attend *maṅgala-ārati,* follow the four regulative principles, and attend *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class. If you do this, you will keep your spiritual strength.”

It seems—and indeed *śāstras* tell us—that we are constantly under attack from *māyā,* Lord Kṛṣṇa’s illusory energy. You taught us Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mood: *ayi nanda-tanuja kiṅkaraṁ patitaṁ māṁ viṣame bhavāmbudhau / kṛpayā tava pāda-paṅkaja-sthita-dhūlī-sadṛśaṁ vicintaya*. We must never, even for a moment, think that we are out of the clutches of *māyā,* until we leave this material body and enter the spiritual world, whenever Kṛṣṇa desires.

So on this 99th anniversary of the most auspicious day of your appearance, kindly bless us with the intelligence to give up our desires for both gross and subtle sense gratification. Kindly allow us to continue serving your transcendental movement for spreading the holy names of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and make this the only business of our lives.

Your eternal servants at ISKCON Baroda, India.

## Belfast

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This year, for the first time since the temple opened, we are submitting this offering under a continuing cease-fire in our country. During this past year we have been able to make some considerable improvements to the temple here. With your guidance, we hope to be able to better serve you in our new situation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is difficult to glorify you with words alone. The best way to glorify you is to follow your instructions, but we humbly submit to you this poem, which is an appreciation of the causeless mercy you have bestowed upon fallen souls worldwide.

Speaking simply,  
an old man,  
an Indian man come to the West—  
What can you teach us?

Universities we have,  
skyscrapers, modern technology—  
so much advancement.  
Old man with white canvas shoes,  
what can you tell us,  
with our fashion and pride?

Religion—yes, we have that.  
Sunday mass or Bible class.  
We can be God if we want.  
What is this picture in your window?  
Five people with their hands upraised—  
you say this is God?

No, no, you must be wrong.  
Why do we need an Indian song?  
“God is a person, our dearest friend.”  
No, this is not our Western trend.

But Indian man, in your sweet smile  
your absorption is clear.  
You live with this Kṛṣṇa in thought and speech.  
There must be some truth to what you say.

No false happiness in drugs, you propose,  
No killing the innocent creatures,  
No pathetic chancing for happiness.  
It’s nice what you’re saying,  
O sober gentleman.

So tell us some more in this incense-filled room.  
What is this Kṛṣṇa, eternal and Supreme?  
How can we stop the pain of this life?  
Let us sing this Hare Kṛṣṇa song.

Dancing to your rhythmic chant,  
Taking shelter of your loving glance.  
What you tell us is not cheap,  
Repeating faithfully *paramparā.*

Now we have Kṛṣṇa in all that we do.  
Everything we live for, we’ve learnt from you.  
This world is not for a gentleman.  
But Prabhupāda, we’re glad that you came.

Time only separates, it was true what you said.  
Please give us the strength to carry on.  
Let us always see your dancing eyes  
and sing your Hare Kṛṣṇa song.

Your servants at the ISKCON temple and community in Belfast, Northern Ireland..  
(poem written by Bhaktin Mary)

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

**A prayer for the Appearance Day of Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your appearance in this world is the greatest blessing. On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we beg to be immersed in your blessings.

Please bless all the devotees connected with this temple.

Bless all who strive to serve the Deities so nicely, that Their Lordships are always visible in their hearts.

Bless all who extend Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others, that they reap the rewards of compassion, humility, and enlightenment.

Bless all who serve the other devotees, that they receive a place in the spiritual world.

Bless all those who care for the building, the grounds, and all our dependents, that they see Kṛṣṇa in every facet of their devotional service.

Bless all who are anxious in their responsibilities, that their burden becomes their pleasure.

Bless all who show consideration, appreciation, and kindness to all in our community, that they are also nurtured by such loving exchanges.

Bless all who have wounds from their conditioned life, that their determination in Kṛṣṇa consciousness may melt away the past.

Bless all who try to chant the holy names, that, hearing attentively, they perceive the Lord dancing on their tongue.

Bless all who love and worry for ISKCON, that their dreams of a united family are fulfilled.

Bless all who are hurt from dealings with other devotees, that they find forgiveness and peace to transcend the pain.

Bless all who struggle with their devotional life, that they may feel you close as their guide, friend, and strength.

Bless all who think kindly of your Movement, that they may be caught by Lord Caitanya’s unique loving network.

Bless all who pass through the Manor gates, that even casual interest transforms into sincere commitment.

Bless all who contribute to any part of your mission, that they feel rewarded by the Lord’s reciprocation.

Bless all who are arrayed against the temple, that they be pacified in their animosity.

Bless all those who will decide the temple’s future, that they be instruments in your plan.

Bless all who love you here at Bhaktivedanta Manor, that you always appear in their hearts, in their intelligence, in their thoughts, in their words, and in all their activities.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the blessing of your appearance.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, the United Kingdom.  
(written by Akhandadhi dāsa)

## Bogota

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious anniversary of your merciful appearance on earth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my eternal spiritual father, you are the spiritual master of my lowly conditioned self and of so many other “Western \[and Indian\] boys and girls.” But you are also the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, a spiritual institution designed by you under the order of your Guru Mahārāja for the express purpose of engaging unlimited numbers of people in the ultimate auspicious activity: glorifying and serving Kṛṣṇa in devotion. Only by devotional service can He be known, and only by the mercy of a pure devotee can such devotion arise. So “causeless mercy” is the appropriate expression of how it is that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, deemed us worthy of your loving grace.

But alas! I have in my heart another “institution,” one of lust, greed, and anger. Instead of diving into the sweet, nectarean waves among the many wonderful souls active in your ISKCON, I sadly remain marooned on the tiny desert island of obstinate pride. And I am being drowned by the rising tide under the relentless push of Time. Yes, I will certainly die alone here. It is only a question of which wave will wash me away—the one constituted of nectar or the one made of the three modes (or, in my case, the two lower modes) of material energy.

I am getting a sound thrashing. May I somehow end up situated in such a way that I will have no choice but to surrender at your lotus feet. May I intelligently follow your instructions so as to finally please you.

Today (April 22), Brahmānanda Prabhu happened to stop here in the Boston temple. He remembers me well from the days of 26 Second Avenue. He was your first temple president, so he sometimes received me and let me go upstairs and speak with you in your apartment. Whereas I could offer nothing more than disruption, he was already your sold-out servant. May he help me remember you today and remind me of my unfathomable debt to you, my eternal master.

Your servants in Bogota, Colombia.  
(written by Prabhupāda dāsa)

## Boise

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have unlimited glories. If it were not for you, we would be lost in an ocean of *māyā* with not one ray of hope.

We, the spirit soul, are wandering, lost in an ocean of material suffering, traveling through many apartments and cages of the material body. According to what we deserved from our past material activities, we got a specific time on the lease, a period of about eighty years. Then the amount of good or bad activities we had accumulated in our bank balance (*karma*), determined whether we rented an opulent apartment in a rich neighborhood or a shack in a battered, run-down area (cycle of birth and death).

All the way, we (the *ātmā*) ignored our dearmost friend and ever well-wisher (Paramātmā), who has been accompanying us throughout our journey through this lonely, temporary and hellish world (Mṛtyuloka). We have lost our eternal, blissful home (Vaikuṇṭha) and are being battered and thrown about in this desolate world.

In this destitute condition, there was not one ray of hope on the horizon when you, like an expert guide, illuminated the path back home, back to Godhead.

You are taking us on a defined roadway (*bhakti-yoga*) and keeping us from wandering off the course of devotional service. You are warding off the fog (*māyā*) by giving us a protective shield and goggles (the chanting of the holy name, our defense against *māyā*). Sometimes there are potholes and pitfalls (*anarthas*) on the way. Glamorous entertainment (sense gratification) sometimes lures us off the path. However, you bring us back and encourage us on, making sure we follow the rules of the road (*sādhana-bhakti*). Very close to our home, we may mistakenly veer off to another path. This path is very treacherous, with mountains overlooking a great ocean. The path is unclear. We seem to lose our way and cannot see our own selves because of the dense fog on this path (Māyāvāda philosophy, the last snare of illusion). Suddenly, we slip on the rock and fall down the long way toward the vast and violent ocean (suffering of the material world). Fortunately, however, you catch us midway and restore us to the direct, paved, and easy path. Through extensive textbooks (the *Vedas*) and your illuminating purports on them, you teach us how to further avoid such mistaken paths and describe to us the different ways in which to approach Kṛṣṇa (the nine processes of devotional service). By following the processes of devotional service we will finally come to our long-awaited reunion with Kṛṣṇa, after which we will never have horrible material desires again.

We are lame, but at every step you mercifully pick us up and drag us toward Kṛṣṇa. “I do not find the strength to carry on alone the *saṅkīrtana* of the holy name of Hari. Please bless me by giving me just one drop of faith with which to obtain the great treasure of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me. I am simply running behind you, shouting ‘Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!’ “ (*Ohe! Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura*).

So please give us the strength to continue faithfully following your instructions and performing devotional service unto your lotus feet.

Your humble servants at the Bhakti Yoga Center in Boise, Idaho.

## Bombay (Chowpatty)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our millions of *daṇḍavats* at your divine lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day we wish to glorify you, and hence we beg you to give us the intelligence and words to do so in a way which is most pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* explains that one can know the greatness of a Vaiṣṇava by how he has been empowered to transform conditioned souls into devotees of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The pure devotee of the Lord is just like a touchstone. He can transform even the most fallen living beings, who are under the influence of the three modes of nature, into pure Vaiṣṇavas blissfully engaged in devotional service to the Lord. You have not only touched the hearts of so many conditioned souls and transformed them into worshipable Vaiṣṇavas, but you have also inspired and empowered them to preach the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement so as to further expand the family of devotees.

We hear of so many great *ācāryas—*Śrīla Rāmānujācārya, Madhvācārya, and so on—who have extensively preached the glories of the holy name in India. But it was you alone who took this message to the most remote corners of the world, where no one else had ever reached—places rife with impersonalism, voidism, atheism, and hedonistic philosophies. Now these places are filled with the beautiful sound of the holy name. When you resound the divine trumpet of Lord Caitanya’s message, there is no philosophy on earth that can remain undefeated.

You are the perfect embodiment of the crest-jewel of the verses by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

*tṛṇād api su-nīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā*  
*amāninā māna-dena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*

**“One who thinks himself lower than the grass, who is more tolerant than a tree, and who does not expect personal honor but is always prepared to give all respect to others can very easily always chant the holy name of the Lord.”**

Your humility is evident in your prayers on board the Jaladuta: “I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own.”

Your tolerance is seen in your willingness to go to the most degraded of all places—The Bowery in New York City—in the mood of Lord Nityānanda Prabhu, in order to deliver the Jagāis and Mādhāis lying in the street.

Your mood of surrender is seen in Your words: “O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands, so if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.”

Your faith in the power of the holy name is what inspired millions to take to this supreme mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In your own words: “I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.”

Your words, your prayers and your divine service to the previous *ācāryas* declare, more than anything else, that you are filled with all the divine virtues and empowerment seen in the associates of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your exemplary life follows in the footsteps of the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, who worked tirelessly to propagate the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu for the benefit of the suffering living beings of this Age of Kali.

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu would take great pleasure in glorifying His devotees. When He would praise Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura, He would speak as if He had five mouths. Similarly, we would also like to glorify Your Divine Grace endlessly—not only with words, but also by assisting your servants in spreading the holy name:

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

By your causeless mercy, we, the devotees at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir, are fortunate to have rejoined the family of wonderful Vaiṣṇavas of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Please give us your blessings, so that we may work together cooperatively in this great family, with love and trust as the basis of our relationship. Bless us so that we may work in the spirit of becoming the servant of the servants of your servants, so that we may assist you in your mission of fulfilling the dreams of the previous *ācāryas* and Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Begging to remain insignificant servants of your servants,  
The devotees of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir, Chowpatty, Bombay.

## Boston

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glory goes to you! We witness a miracle at the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa when we feel our existence being filled more and more with your purpose of serving and glorifying the Supreme Lord. This miracle is the most profound of miracles, and is beyond the dazzling miracles or “magic” of this world.

Consider the magician of this world. The magician of this world performs tricks or acts that appear to be miraculous, just to impress others. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, performed true miracles not to impress anybody but to selflessly serve everyone lovingly and compassionately, as your service to the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. Those who could see your miracles are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians typically perform absurd, meaningless and trivial tricks that do not accomplish anything. You inspired us in the self-dedication of loving service to God, the ultimate achievement. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians of this world get hired and paid for their services. You already possessed inconceivable spiritual riches that you were just giving away without self-interest. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians of this world depend on the imperfect senses of their audiences for their tricks to work. You teach us how to evolve into our spiritual bodies with spiritual senses that can perceive and appreciate your real miracles more and more. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians of this world learn to perform their tricks from teachers and books that tell them how to fool people, how to cheat people. You gave us your secret sources—your spiritual master’s instructions and the sacred texts—from which you derived your miraculous power of changing people’s hearts. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians of this world are commonplace. One can find them anywhere. Yet you are the most rare among men. You have spread love of God around the globe. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians of this world are bored and unhappy with the same old tricks, and they must learn new ones constantly. You gave us the holy names of God, which can be repeated incessantly with ever-increasing bliss in an ever-deepening love that we develop for the most beautiful and supremely loving Lord. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

For magicians, magic is just a job. All magicians of this world will be retired. For you, *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* is the essence of life itself, and therefore it is an activity that increases, that is uninterrupted, and that goes on eternally. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians think they are the performer of their own silly tricks. Through you, however, comes the performance of God’s will for conditioned souls. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians of this world do not teach anyone anything. They just show off. Your miracles fill us with love and compassion for conditioned souls, the love that you yourself gave to us constantly. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Magicians distract their audience from the real purpose of life, for they know not this purpose themselves. They cause us to forget the Supreme Lord, on whom we depend at every moment. You, however, cause us to forget our selfishness. You cause us to remember Rādhārāṇī and her beloved Kṛṣṇa. You cause us to remember Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s ecstasy in the *saṅkīrtana-līlā*. You cause us to remember our real and exhilarating mission for this life. Those who could see your miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are stunned for lifetimes.

Finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all magicians of this world must die. They know nothing of life eternal. They are suffering in this life and they are suffering beyond this life. Yet if these magicians had had the opportunity to be in your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, even they could have experienced the ecstatic *prema* emanating from you. Had they known you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, they would have known that your departure from this world was still another miracle of yours; because we constantly witness your miracle in this world as it continues to be performed, we know that you are still with us.

In this world, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you performed true miracles and you continue to perform them. Please, don’t let us again become just some useless magicians. Please help us to convince these worldly magicians to give up their tricks. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow us to be a part of your miracle. Until that time when we can be with you, we are attempting to serve your preaching mission in this world. And no matter how long it will take, no matter what we may have to do, we will attempt to offer some contribution to your miracle. Please allow us to be absorbed in your preaching miracle forever. Though selfish it may seem, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all of us want to be with you more, witnessing together the unfolding of this miracle. We just cannot help ourselves from desiring this; we cannot get enough of this miracle. Please grant us this, please.

Your disciples and granddisciple servants in the Boston Temple community.  
(written by Garuḍa dāsa)

## Brisbane

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

Vyāsa-pūjā is the day on which the spiritual master is honored as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. It is not like a mundane celebration in which we gather to glorify a person’s achievements in this world; rather it is meant for showing our gratitude and acknowledging our debt to the spiritual master for forcing open our eyes with the torchlight of transcendental knowledge.

Transcendental knowledge, *divya-jñāna,* cannot be purchased by any materialistic endeavor. It requires the blessings of the *guru-paramparā,* of which you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the pre-eminent representative.

On this most auspicious day we pray to be able to continuously receive your empowerment and blessings. No one else is our savior, and no one else can give us the most prized gift. Love of Godhead comes as a matter of course to one who carries on your mission. Please help us as we struggle to continue expanding this great movement by bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every corner of the universe.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi Mandira, ISKCON Brisbane, Australia.

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

This is the first time that I’m trying to compile an offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration on behalf of the devotees here in Budapest, Hungary. Previously it was done by my beloved Godbrother Baladeva Prabhu, who unexpectedly passed away in a car accident last summer. Our Guru Mahārāja said that whoever came in contact with you in some way or other was the recipient of your mercy and was thus eligible to attain the highest perfection.

“The verdict of all revealed scriptures is that even by a moment’s association with a pure devotee, one can attain all success.” (Cc. *Madhya* 22.54) I hope that by your and our Guru Mahārāja’s mercy he will attain the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

My Guru Mahārāja once said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda never visited Hungary, yet he changed the lives of so many people there through his books. This is a sign of a really great personality—that even without coming here he can change the masses.” Yes, you are becoming more and more famous here, also. We have to realize that you are with us, with those who have faith in you, who want to preach uncompromisingly as you did, whose single aim is to become pure devotees and make others such also. We have to learn how to be as determined as you were and how to risk everything for Kṛṣṇa, as you did.

Yes, Prabhupāda, your books make the world go round! Everything that we have now here in Hungary is solely because of your books. All the devotees, the nice temple, the beautiful Deities of Śrī Śrī Dayal Nitāi Vijaya Gaurāṅga, the exclusive farm project in New Vraja-Dhāma, the Food for Life *prasādam* distribution, and the Budapest Ratha-yātrā festivals—all this is due to the magical influence of your transcendental books.

My Guru Mahārāja once told about a dream that he had. In his dream he was playing with Lord Balarāma and the cowherd boys. They were running here and there in Vṛndāvana forest, and suddenly they came across Kṛṣṇa. He was lying on a bed of flowers and was sleeping. When they approached, He woke up. My Guru Mahārāja bowed down and started to offer prayers in ecstasy. Lord Kṛṣṇa turned to him and said, “If you really want to satisfy Me, translate all the books into Hungarian.” “That’s why I’m here,” concluded my Guru Mahārāja. So with your blessings we are trying to get all your books translated by the time of your centennial celebration, that is, within one year.

Once my Guru Mahārāja said, “Don’t think that book distribution will be stopped at any time. We may all fall into *māyā,* but still the books will go out. Why? Because Prabhupāda’s desire is so strong that it doesn’t know any barriers. *Saṅkīrtana* is going on by his desire, not by ours.” I was amazed at the conviction he was speaking with. And this is the attitude of all your empowered disciples who visit Hungary to preach. They seem to have devotion to your lotus feet comparable only to that of the *gopīs’* love for Kṛṣṇa:

*āsām aho caraṇa-reṇu-juṣām ahaṁ syāṁ*  
*vṛndāvane kim api gulma-latauṣadhīnām*  
*yā dustyajaṁ sva-janam ārya-pathaṁ ca hitvā*  
*bhejur mukunda-padavīṁ śrutibhir vimṛgyām*

**“Oh, when will that day be mine when I can take on my head the dust of the lotus feet of those great souls known as the** ***gopīs*****? When will the day come when I shall take birth as a creeper in Vṛndāvana so that I can take the dust of the lotus feet of the** ***gopīs*** **on my head? Those great souls gave up society, friendship, love, their very relatives—even the Vedic principles—to surrender to Kṛṣṇa, who is known as Mukunda. Such devotion as exhibited by these exalted inhabitants of Vṛndāvana is only hinted at in the** ***Vedas*****.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.47.61)**

I remember a lecture that was given once on the anniversary of your disappearance day by one of your disciples. He was remembering how you had once scolded him for not reading your books. You had said, “Why don’t you read my books? Even I read them when I have spare time. Why do you think I read them? Do you think that these books were written by me? No! They were written by Kṛṣṇa. That’s why I read them. Every time when I start writing, Kṛṣṇa comes and sits beside me. He inspires each and every word that I write down. These books are not ordinary writings! This is transcendental literature!”

So I think that there are no more perfect words to glorify you than those found in your own books, because Kṛṣṇa didn’t forget to glorify you, His pure devotee.

“Without being empowered by the direct potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa to fulfill His desire and without being specifically favored by the Lord, no human being can become the spiritual master of the whole world. He certainly cannot succeed by mental concoction, which is not meant for devotees or religious people. Only an empowered personality can distribute the holy name of the Lord and enjoin all fallen souls to worship Kṛṣṇa. By distributing the holy name of the Lord, he cleanses the hearts of the most fallen people; therefore he extinguishes the blazing fire of the material world. Not only that, he broadcasts the shining brightness of Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence throughout the world. Such an *ācārya,* or spiritual master, should be considered nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa—that is, he should be considered the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency. Such a personality is *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha—*that is, he is always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa.” (Cc. *Madhya* 25.9, purport)

The form of Rādhārāṇī is just like a creeper embracing the tree of Kṛṣṇa, and the damsels of Vraja, the associates of Rādhārāṇī, are just like the leaves and flowers of that creeper. When a creeper embraces a tree, the leaves and flowers, as well as the creeper, automatically embrace it. The *Govinda-līlāmṛta* (10.16) confirms that Rādhārāṇī is the expansion of the pleasure potency of Kṛṣṇa, that She is like a creeper of love of Godhead, and that Her associates, the damsels of Vraja, are like the flowers and leaves of that creeper. When the damsels of Vraja help Rādhārāṇī and Kṛṣṇa enjoy Themselves, they relish more pleasure than when they enjoy with Kṛṣṇa themselves.

Lord Caitanya appeared in this world to distribute this pleasure to every fallen soul. To bless those whom He couldn’t get to, He sent you, either personally or in the form of your books.

Finally I would like to express the desire of all the devotees here in the Budapest *yātrā*. My Guru Mahārāja said in one lecture that many of your disciples left the movement after your disappearance because they were inspired by the association of your *vapu* but didn’t have enough faith in your *vāṇī,* although your *vāṇī* is eternal while your *vapu* is only temporarily manifest. So we believe that by having strong faith in your *vāṇī* and following such great souls as our GBC secretary, His Holiness Śivarāma Swami Mahārāja, and all your other disciples, we will very soon get your *darśana,* the association of your divine *vapu*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our eternal leader. Please eternally guide us back home, back to Godhead!

*Your granddisciples in Budapest, Hungary.*  
(written by Gaurāṅga dāsa)

## Calcutta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we bow down to you. O Master, our life has been graced by your appearance, and your instructions are the life support system of our existence.

We are totally unqualified to glorify your transcendental qualities and character, and yet by your causeless mercy you are giving us another opportunity to reveal our hearts by writing this Vyāsa-pūjā homage on the most auspicious day of the calendar.

Your orders are the life and soul of your followers. You many a times said that along with the order of the spiritual master comes the strength to execute such an order. How true this is can only be understood by the sincere and dutiful devotees who dedicate their lives to fulfilling your instructions. Just as a person cannot taste honey by licking the bottle, similarly no one can understand the purport of your instructions unless he wholeheartedly commits himself to working to fulfill those instructions without personal motivation.

Arjuna became the dearest disciple of Droṇācārya because in the contest to see who among Droṇa’s students was the best marksman, he saw only the bird on the tree and nothing else. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the dearmost disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura because you kept his order in your heart. Thus by both the strength of your faith and his blessings you were able to create a spiritual revolution in the hearts of your followers. In this way you proved by your own example that to accept and execute the order of the spiritual master is the only way to enter into the confidential association of the pure devotees. Therefore we, as your followers, can become dear to you by focusing our attention on your order and executing it with faith and enthusiasm. That is the key to success.

Here in Calcutta we have the opportunity to see this spiritual truth at work by working to manifest your vision of a beautiful temple that will attract devotees and tourists from all over the world.

By your mercy, the land you very much desired has now been secured, and this achievement is giving us strength to go forward with full confidence that all your desires will be fulfilled. Lord Kṛṣṇa asked Arjuna to become the instrument for accomplishing the Lord’s purpose. That he did so was Arjuna’s greatness. He placed himself at the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa and surrendered to His order. Thus he was recognized by the Lord as His most intimate devotee and friend.

No one can claim to be your intimate follower unless he has tirelessly worked to please you in advancing the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu with all humility, being free from the desire for profit and recognition for his service. Arjuna wanted to go to the forest rather than fight his own relatives. He had no desire for his personal comfort. You glorified Arjuna for this selfless mentality. However, his real glory is that he gave up his own vision and became the perfect instrument in the hands of the Lord. This is the real qualification of your followers.

There is certainly a long way to go, with many obstacles to cross before we come to this perfect stage. But on this day we would like to invoke your merciful glance and beg a boon from you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda: Kindly force us to develop the proper attitude of submission and surrender to your order, thus perfecting our insignificant existence. Just as a father sometimes forces his son to take medicine for his own benefit, kindly do what is necessary to bring us to the platform of full surrender to your instructions.

Thank you very much for being so patient with us all these years. All glories to your lotus feet!

*Your insignificant servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda temple, Calcutta*, *India*.  
(written by Sattvic dāsa)

## Chicago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, the true shelter of the distressed living entities in this material world. All glories to your perfect and eternal service to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda.

As we celebrate the ninety-ninth year of your merciful appearance on this earth, the entire community of devotees is absorbed in your Centennial Celebration next year. This worldwide celebration of your life, your legacy, your accomplishments, your qualities, and your pastimes is an event that you more than deserve but certainly do not personally need. Rather, it is we, your ever-forgetful and easily distracted servants, who are being granted a special opportunity to glorify and remember you.

Your appearance on this planet can certainly be seen retrospectively, and many enlivening volumes have chronicled your pastimes and kept your legacy alive for your disciples and grand-disciples. But we can also view your appearance and activities progressively, meditating on your eternal spiritual presence. We see that you are still spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness and that you will continue to do so far into the future. That many conditioned souls are still taking up devotional service is certainly not our accomplishment, but rather evidence of your inconceivable spiritual power, which transcends time and space. If we look within ourselves with honesty and humility, we see that any spark of devotion we possess was ignited by your presence, any ability to perform service was instilled by your potency, and any good qualities we have developed were granted by your mercy.

Thus our glorification of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is not merely a faithful remembrance of a past gift, unfathomably great as that may be. Rather, our tribute to you is a loving acknowledgment that you are personally guiding and inspiring both the growth of this movement and our own individual progress on the path of devotional service. Our feelings of separation from you are somewhat eased by knowing that, while we may no longer physically see you, we can witness your presence as Kṛṣṇa consciousness is being spread. When we see new people taking up this path of *bhakti-yoga,* we see that the processes of devotional service that you gave us continue to change the hearts of the conditioned souls, just as they did during your physical presence. These eternal processes of devotional service were your great gifts to us and remain available for all conditioned souls.

You gave us the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* which continues to purify the heart and enliven all who can hear or chant the holy name.

You gave us the process of hearing your brilliant translations and purports of the Vedic literature, which still transform the consciousness of those fortunate persons who contact them.

You gave us the beautiful deities of Śrī Śrī Kishora-Kishori, and so many other wonderful forms of the Lord, who always attract and inspire those who come into Their presence.

You gave us practical devotional service, which still changes the heart from enjoyer to servant and engages the mind and body of all who take it up.

Our own power to present Kṛṣṇa consciousness is certainly very limited, so we know that whatever success we achieve is directly due to your mercy and your continuing spiritual presence.

As long as we remain faithful to your message, that message has the power to change the unfortunate and misguided lives of this Kali-yuga civilization. We pray that we will always feel your eternal presence in our devotional lives, remember your words and instructions, and glorify you through our devotional service.

Begging to remain your insignificant instruments,  
Your servants in Chicago, Illinois.  
(written by Tamohara dāsa)

## Columbus

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow me to offer my fallen obeisances again and again at your lotus feet.

This is your offensive daughter and re-aspiring disciple Mālatī approaching you with prostrated obeisances, my face to the ground in the dust of your lotus feet. I have no right to be here, yet somehow you are allowing me to remain. Once, a long time ago, you very kindly and lovingly pulled me from the quagmire of material existence, but I was immature, careless, and ungrateful. My shallow affection was uprooted by a clever invitation from Māyā, which I most unfortunately accepted. Immediately I became bereft of all good sense and any intelligence. Disassociated from you and your glories, away from the devotees, and devoid of any service opportunities, I simply deteriorated. At a very desultory moment in my life, actually almost at my death, you knew the utter despair in my grieving heart. You petitioned Kṛṣṇa on my behalf to help rectify my fallen condition, and then you most graciously sent your devotees to deliver the holy name back into my life. Through them you extended your love and returned me to the shelter of your lotus feet. Although I had been an offender to you, to the holy name, and to the Vaiṣṇavas in general, all was forgiven in an instant of conversion when my heart was returned to your care and my life was truly saved through your causeless mercy and the miracle of devotional service.

You have continued to give your personal encouragement and guidance to this disobedient dog, who can only roughly bark of your glories. In India, dogs are not allowed to enter houses or temples, but Americans have no such discrimination. Therefore, I am most fortunate to be an American dog and be given entry and shelter in your many homes and temples. I earnestly pray to remain forever situated as such—to sit down, to lie down, and to roll over at your command; to be allowed to serve you through your devotees, to please them, and thereby to gain your favor and pleasure. I have no other request.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me the association of your aspiring young Vaiṣṇavas. I feel keenly privileged to have a part in this unique opportunity, and I pray not to fail you in this regard. You are their only shelter, and they have become mine.

May I always garland you again and again with loving gratitude.

Your servants in Columbus, Ohio.  
(written by Mālatī dāsī)

## Coventry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. It is always with great joy that we compose the Vyāsa-pūjā offering to you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you communicate with us through your books, but Vyāsa-pūjā is the time when we can in turn communicate with you via the Vyāsa-pūjā book. We in the Coventry *yātrā* cannot glorify you enough because its progress can only be attributed to you and your representatives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, everyone speaks of Deity worship as something very demanding and almost out of reach for many small centers. This is true, but the joy which results is all-inspiring. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you will be pleased to know that the Coventry *yātrā* now has Gaura-Nitāi Deities installed. Their names are Śrī Śacīnandana and Śrī Nitāichand. To serve Their Lordships with love and devotion requires special dedicated people who are willing to dedicate their time and effort to this service. In Coventry, by your mercy we do have such devotees. The above-mentioned greater commitment has beautified our lives, and we are all eternally grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for introducing Deity worship to the West.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, slowly but surely, more and more people are regularly attending the temple programs, thus giving us old devotees pleasure in realizing that your process keeps working day after day and forever!

Another highlight of this year was our involvement in the protest against calves exported from Coventry airport for slaughter in Belgium and France. Our Bhakta Bipin, with support of all the congregation, spearheaded our involvement in this protest, which was started by a handful of *karmī* housewives. The mood of the devotees involved became purposeful and full of compassion for the protection of the calves and the cows. The other protesters thought that this was the very first time that we got involved in such a campaign, but when we told them that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had started the cow protection scheme a long time ago in the Western countries, they were all impressed. Later, some of them turned up at the temple to take your *darśana*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your invaluable gift to the whole world has not been realized by us dumb humans, as you and only you deserve the title “Savior of the whole world.”

We apologize for using meager words to praise your exalted personality. We cannot comprehend your fathomless qualities, so we use simple words to call you “The greatest representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa!” and thus succeed in defining you perfectly.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue giving us your mercy so that one day Śrī Śacīnandana and Nitāichand will bestow upon us, Their unqualified *sevakas,* the love of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. We promise that if we receive this, we will ask no more.

Your fallen servants in the Coventry yātrā, U.K.

## Czech Republic

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the name of all the Vaiṣṇavas engaged in your service at your ISKCON farm project (Kṛṣṇuv Dvur) and your Govinda restaurants, as well as in the name of hundreds of friends and well-wishers of your movement in the Czech Republic (over four hundred people come daily to the Prague Govinda’s and over two hundred come to each major festival at the farm), this humble offering is submitted for your pleasure:

I bow down to Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
though I cannot fully appreciate the extent of his mission,  
though I know only little of this great personality.  
I can only say, “Saintly, Saintly, Saintly!”  
He is worthy of worship and veneration,  
for his lifelong dedication to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mission,  
for his exemplary renunciation,  
for his unadulterated relationships  
which he maintains with all Lord Kṛṣṇa’s devotees  
who, despite his physical absence,  
remain faithful to the Lord in all circumstances.  
Hare Kṛṣṇa!

We beg for only one blessing: that we can always remain servants of the servants of the servants of your lotus feet. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at Kṛṣṇuv Dvur Farm and Restaurants, Czech Republic.  
(written by Roman Petrov, 91 years old, former orthodox priest)

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. Ninety-nine years ago, you blessed this Earth with the touch of your lotus feet. Mother Bhūmi is so fortunate in that way. Anyone following in your lotus footsteps will certainly achieve Goloka Vṛndāvana. Your most precious gift is the practical means that you have left us to follow in your footsteps. You have established this International Society for Krishna Consciousness for the benefit of all living entities for hundreds of centuries to come. As your fame has spread all over the universe, demigods are already waiting to be born into this movement. We must carefully protect, maintain, and expand the movement that you have worked so hard to establish. In your books you have clearly laid out all the instructions we need. All we need is to read them and work cooperatively, always remembering Kṛṣṇa to overcome any obstacles in our path. There is no better way of glorifying you than to cooperate with your ISKCON. All our energies must go toward making ISKCON a success. ISKCON is your mission, ISKCON is your self, your spiritual master’s blessings, and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu's mercy.

Having been in Dallas for only eight months, I cannot take any credit for the wonderful contributions these devotees have made to your mission. But I can express my deep, heartfelt appreciation as an inside observer. This community is composed of so many great devotees—a nice mixture of your disciples and granddisciples working cooperatively. This makes a very sweet combination. Who are the fortunate leaders that have organized things so nicely? Why not take advantage of this opportunity to glorify His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami and all his people for pushing on strongly with your book distribution and all the other programs you started and inspired? It is such an enlivening atmosphere.
