So, please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us never forget the instructions you have given us. Please don’t let time cover our memories or distort the meanings of your words. The true function of the affectionate father is to give practical knowledge to the foolish sons so they can leave this world of the threefold miseries and go back home to Godhead. In this material world, the combinations of choices are innumerable and Māyā is very strong and cunning, but we know that a foolproof method for pleasing you is to distribute your books and thus participate in the greatest welfare activity. “Distribution of my books is the highest kind of preaching and thus the best engagement.” (Letter, June 9, 1974) May Kṛṣṇa therefore give us the strength to make a special effort in the field of book distribution as a Centennial offering. You said that you almost blindly followed the order of your Guru Mahārāja, “If you ever get money, print books.” So let us similarly “almost blindly” follow your order to distribute your books and thus become successful in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Perth, Western Australia.  
(written by Yādavendra dāsa)

## Philadelphia

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the sweet dust of your lotus feet, which wholly transforms the hopeless into the hopeful.

Thinking about your appearance, we are appreciating some benedictions you have bestowed upon us.

**Humility**

The boundless glories of the spiritual quality of humility are endlessly praised and exhibited by the previous *ācāryas*. It is the first item of knowledge listed by Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. Indeed, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself has deemed humility essential for cultivating the holy name.

This quality of meekness and submission is the foundation of spiritual **purity,** which you have shown us **is the force**. And this aim (humility) of all aspiring devotees has been summed up for all of us in one simple question: *What would we be if you had never come?*

**Unity**

The valor of any Vaiṣṇava can be plainly seen by the number of devotees he has created, the otherwise ruined souls he has brought to the feet of the Lord. In this, you cannot be defeated. You have gathered us far and wide. Yet, this age is expert in the tactics of divide and conquer, and we are wounded, Śrīla Prabhupāda. So it seems, and you have surely stressed that unity is our best **utility**.

Please show each of us how to be a true friend (as you are), for it is the positive association of your offspring that creates strength and intensity in your sacred mission. We are just now beginning to understand that cooperation despite differences **is the** underlying **principle** of a harmonious society of devotees. May we expand unchecked, safe from the summer heat of Kali, under the cooling shade of your umbrellalike feet.

**Inspiration**

The infusion of hope, impelling vigor and animation, life-giving breath, and the impervious fortitude of your devotees, is your **books**. Though mysteriously unintelligible and foreign to most of us at one time, they have since smeared our eyes with the salve of affection. They **are the basis** of our aspiration. We now pray to them to make us dependent, to make us into virtual addicts, in constant anxious anticipation of the next replenishing *darśana*. With every revisit we feel the sending away of *anarthas*. We see the dismantling of material identity and the genesis of an elemental reformation of the inherent self. Where else in this benumbed world can there be found such increasing freshness and vitality? Let us go on sharing with suffering souls your precious association as dispersed freely in your blessed books.

**Revolution**

The writer confesses that he sat down to write this homage and sort of *conjured* up a mood of offering, of devotion, and of glorification. It is only by your association that one is able to do such a thing, but it is indicative of a vast difference between you and us. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not something you were endeavoring to do or even to be, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but rather it is what you *are*. Your **preaching** is not something which you conscientiously engage in; it **is the essence** of your very self.

By this essence we have become stirred, enwrapped in a sense of positive frustration and unease, scorning complacency and casualness by your grace. You have invested in your movement this fighting spirit of revolution. You have embedded in us the most radical and overthrowing force: the desire to become the servant of you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at New Nīlācala Dhāma, Philadelphia, Pennsylvania.  
(written by Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura dāsa)

## Port Moresby

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance in this world, we would like to offer our most respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who have saved us from the ocean of birth and death. Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we never had your personal association, we are not discouraged, since you are always present in your many books, your lectures, your sweet *bhajanas,* and your instructions.

By your preaching you have given us a wonderful opportunity in this lifetime to engage in hearing and chanting the holy name of the Lord. It is very rare in this part of the world to have devotees’ association, but by your causeless mercy you have arranged for this, and now we have a small temple and spiritual programs.

Please continue engaging us in serving your ISKCON movement so that it grows more and more here in Papua New Guinea. We can actually see that by the potency of your transcendental books the desire of Lord Caitanya—*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma*—is being fulfilled everywhere, and Papua New Guinea is no exception. \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\]

We feel greatly indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we will continue to serve you faithfully by fulfilling the various commitments we have each undertaken for honoring Your Divine Grace during the year of your Centennial. Although our hearts are contaminated, we still are trying to chant offenselessly.

Please bless and continue to guide your devotees here in Papua New Guinea, and use us as your instruments in spreading the holy name. We all feel very fortunate to be a part of this movement.

Your mercy is all that we are made of.

Your unworthy servants in Port Moresby, Papua New Guinea.  
(written by Rādhikātmā dāsa, Bhakta Pious, and Bhakta Mathew)

## Portugal

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances prostrated at your divine lotus feet. All glories unto Your Divine Grace.

With a straw between our teeth we come before you to thank you for all the wonderful gifts you left for us and for the world. They are unlimited, so our gratitude should also be unlimited.

Here in Portugal, a small country forgotten somewhere at the end of Europe and a place your lotus feet didn’t touch, we are constantly experiencing the greatness of your gifts and of your mercy. It’s amazing to see how you’re present here and how you’re constantly acting to deliver the fallen souls forgotten in this tract of land.

So we find ourselves bereft of words, and we should just constantly weep for not being able to serve you properly and for not being able to deal with each other properly so as to expand the gifts you left for us and take them to as many souls as possible. The progress of your Movement is greatly hampered here by your children’s tendency to quarrel and their inability simply to focus completely on serving your divine lotus feet.

But, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we feel repentant for this attitude of ours. Weeping and lamenting for the strained relationships among ourselves, we beg for a drop of your mercy so that we may improve ourselves and sincerely serve you for our benefit and that of all the souls here, and also for your proper glorification, especially now with the upcoming first Centennial of your most auspicious appearance in this world.

Thank you so much a million times over.  
Your fallen children in Portugal.

## Prague

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. We devotees, your disciples and granddisciples of the Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Navadvīpacandra Mandira in Prague, are your servants.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, although most of us have never met you personally, still we want to glorify you despite our limited experience and realizations. Due to the effort of your sincere disciples, you came to the Czech Republic through your transcendental literatures. In this way you came into our hands, onto our bookshelves; you entered our hearts and thus changed our lives.

That is how we came to know Your Divine Grace, through your books. We remain enlivened by reading your books: Through your books you give us a clear and straight path to follow. Through your books you are making us pure and strong, correcting everything we do wrong. Through your books you remind us again and again that this world is not the place where we belong. By reading your books we come to understand your mission, and by your mercy that mission becomes our life and soul. In your books you have revealed your heart’s desires, and we are convinced of your statement that “If you really want to please me, then distribute my books.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your book distribution mission is the best medicine for us, because out on book distribution we have to learn how to be disciplined. By distributing your books we become properly trained, and we learn that we cannot go on with our material desires, *anarthas*. But when we are not on *saṅkīrtana,* our minds play tricks on us, and we may become pretenders. And when we are out there on *saṅkīrtana,* the only thing that actually works is genuine surrender. This taste of surrender we want to experience again and again; it is the best way to mitigate all disturbances and pains. Please do not let us miss your transcendental *saṅkīrtana* express train.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for taking us out of the world of *vikarmīs* and engaging us in the transcendental battle against *māyā* in your *saṅkīrtana* army.

Your book distribution is the highest form of meditation. Please relieve us of unnecessary hesitation and give us the strength to achieve unalloyed dedication. Let that become our only ambition: to fulfill your transcendental mission and attain, by your mercy, pure Kṛṣṇa-conscious vision.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental book distribution mission*-kī jaya!*

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Prague, the Czech Republic.

## Rāḍhadeśa

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*he guro jñāna-da dīna-bandho*  
*svānanda-dātaḥ karuṇaika-sindho*  
*vṛndāvanāsīna-hitāvatāra*  
*prasīda rādhā-praṇaya-pracāra*

**“O spiritual master, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of the fallen souls like ourselves, and you are preaching the divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon us” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.8, purport).**

The secret of success in spiritual life is satisfying the spiritual master. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has sung in his famous eight verses on the spiritual master that only by his satisfaction can one please the Personality of Godhead, and when he is dissatisfied there is only havoc on the path of spiritual realization. It is essential therefore that a disciple be very much obedient and submissive to his bona fide spiritual master.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we want to offer you the history of our preaching efforts in this remote place called Belgium, where people are very conservative and do not like things they have never seen before. Somehow or other we have ended up in the least populated area of this country, high up in the mountains in a deserted castle which was once used for a Dracula movie.

So we all very enthusiastically moved from busy Amsterdam, where our building was falling apart from ecstatic *kīrtanas* that made all the chandeliers seem like swings in a carousel. We definitely needed a better place, more in the mode of goodness, away from passionate Amsterdam. When we moved we felt as if a heavy burden had fallen off our shoulders; we could breathe again, we could chant and dance and throw off our hippie shackles, become ladies and gentlemen. First we had to burn all the old mattresses and other materials, which made the neighbors scared, but quickly we received a positive reaction from the neighborhood. People were happy to see light again behind the windows of the castle, which had been spooky and empty for the previous five years, overgrown with bushes and vandalized by neighborhood youngsters.

Soon visitors came knocking on the door. They were bold Dutch tourists visiting the area; they wanted to have a look around the old castle. Of course they did not leave without receiving an explanation of our Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy, some *prasādam,* and a book or two. Very satisfied, they often left a note for the next occupiers of their holiday house.

So more and more visitors came; as soon as we finished with one group of visitors and let them out the door, another group would come in. The *pūjārīs* started to escape out the back door to chant their rounds. We realized that we had to schedule the visits, regulate the hours for visitors, and organize the preachers. The guide tours were born at that time. We made a cassette explaining the process of Deity worship in two languages, and we arranged visitors’ hours so one devotee could take care of several people at a time. We decorated the rooms, and attractive features were developed for the guests. *Prasādam* distribution was organized.

Then Bhakti-cāru Swami suggested we contact bus companies and ask some fees for our guided tours. This more than doubled the stream of visitors. Up to nine buses a day would come during the busy season. Many old people who would have never thought of entering a Hare Kṛṣṇa temple left very satisfied and full of appreciation. Schools started coming. But then some parents complained. They sent inspectors from the Ministry of Education in disguise; these inspectors happened to be guided on a tour by Hṛdaya-caitanya Prabhu and later gave a very favorable report. They suggested that all schools who want to teach Eastern religion should have their pupils visit our castle. Thus school visits doubled. The priest in the little church across from the castle said in his Easter sermon that if people wanted to know about real religion they should go to the castle.

We arranged an open house for the neighbors, who gradually started appreciating us, especially when we cleared the area, mowed our lawns, worked the fields, and said *bonjour* just like them. *Prasādam* had a positive effect on them. Parents’ weekends were organized; they loved the surroundings and the hard work we did, renovating the buildings and increasing proper living facilities. Then we opened a cafeteria, bakery, boutique, and restaurant.

The Deities were moved to the best room in the castle, which we fixed up with a heated marble floor and gold plating on the walls and ceiling. Their beautiful marble *mahā-siṁhāsanas* made Their Lordship look like diamonds in a golden setting.

Now we are receiving 40,000 visitors a year. Many people come regularly and don’t want to skip one year without visiting the Hare Kṛṣṇa castle. People visit daily to see the color of the Deities clothes and buy Hare Kṛṣṇa bread. We hardly experience any variation in tourist seasons anymore.

Guided tours go on daily throughout the year. We lead all the visitors in Hare Kṛṣṇa *kīrtana,* and in various ways they respond enthusiastically. Our book distributors who travel throughout Belgium and Holland daily meet people who have visited the castle at least once or who come regularly, love our cookies, or read our books. Groups from monasteries and other religious organizations come and marvel at the dedication, devotion, and cleanliness, leaving inspired to increase their devotion to God.

Now we are developing a nice art museum in one of our side buildings. *Gurukula* is going on, and we are adding decorations in the main rooms of the castle, as well as living facilities for our *gṛhastha* devotees. And to show everyone that you are the inspiration behind all this, we are also opening a memorial room for Your Divine Grace with beautiful stained glass windows depicting *kṛṣṇa-līlā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, all this could have developed only by your grace. We simply came here. You picked us out of the dirt and engaged us usefully in this master plan of yours to engage all classes of people in these countries in the best ways of devotional service—*śravaṇam, kīrtanam, viṣṇoḥ smaraṇam,* and so on. By taking our tour, all our visitors engage in these processes by your mercy. Thus, by helping these people to become eligible to come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we are also becoming eligible candidates for becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg you to allow us to be always absorbed in preaching your Kṛṣṇa conscious mission in this way. On our own we cannot accomplish anything, for we are insignificant. But by working together with your blessings, we feel we can perform miracles. Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha are most merciful to us. Along with Their *sakhīs* and assistants, They bestow all benedictions on those who come to have *darśana,* daily or during holidays. Kṛṣṇa is the Lord and master of the Benelux, and you are the captain who can guide us to Him. Please allow us to serve eternally under the shelter of your lotus feet.

Your dedicated and grateful servants at Rāḍhadeśa, Belgium.

## St. Louis

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are the only shelter for the suffering conditioned souls of Kali-yuga.

Each year at this time, devotees all over the world begin to search their hearts and minds in order to write their offerings of devotion to you, in appreciation for the causeless mercy you have given them. It is a time of great joy, a time of reflection on the past year’s progress and the coming year’s commitment to your service. Many wonderfully joyous words come to mind, but usually absent among them is the word “struggle.” Yet this is the prominent thought that we are feeling here in the St. Louis *yātrā*. Struggle to become worthy tools in your effort to fulfill the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and make the whole world Kṛṣṇa conscious. Struggle to bring our *yātrā* up to your standard. Struggle to maintain our Deity worship at the proper level. Struggle to continue distributing your books in the face of obstacles. Struggle to keep up the high quality of *prasādam* and its mass distribution. And struggle to maintain our own *sādhana,* which is the foundation on which these are all built.

Kṛṣṇa says in the Fifteenth Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā* (15.17), *manaḥ-ṣaṣṭhānīndriyāṇi prakṛti-sthāni karṣati: “*Due to conditioned life, the living entities struggle very hard with the six senses, which include the mind.”

So we should not be surprised by the fact that we are struggling. Our taking to the process of devotional service does not mean that we will not experience the trials and tribulations of the material nature we are working in. Rather, taking to devotional service brings us face to face with the onslaught of the material nature. But we should not think ourselves unfortunate because we have to struggle. Throughout history all the dear devotees of the Lord have been beset by so many difficulties. If we were to cite all the examples that are relevant, this offering would challenge the length and breadth of the universe. Nonetheless, we should mention some of these.

In the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.8.25), Śrīmatī Kuntī-devī actually requests that her tribulations should continue:

*vipadaḥ santu tāḥ śaśvat tatra tatra jagad-guro*  
*bhavato darśanaṁ yat syād apunar-bhava-darśanam*

**“I wish that all those calamities would happen again and again so that we could see You again and again, for seeing You means that we will no longer see repeated birth and death.”**

As you state in the purport of this verse, “Therefore the pious person, if there is some calamity, has no other alternative than to take shelter of the Lord.”

Similarly, in the Seventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the story of Prahlāda Mahārāja describes many difficulties he underwent, yet he always stayed steady at the lotus feet of his Lord. The five Pāṇḍavas, Grandfather Bhīṣma, Dhruva Mahārāja—the list goes on and on. Each sought the shelter of the Lord in all difficulties, for they each considered devotional service essential to success. In each of these persons we see one common principle for all spiritual progress—taking shelter of a bona fide *guru*.

In the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad* we are told,

*tad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet*  
*samit-pāṇiḥ śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham*

**“To learn the transcendental science, one must approach a bona fide spiritual master who is fixed in the Absolute Truth.”**

So if we examine our circumstances with some clear vision, we will find the struggles we face not drawbacks but opportunities to take more and more shelter of your guidance. Indeed, the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* states (*Ādi-līlā* 1.45),

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers his devotees.”**

Further, in verse 58 of this chapter,

*jīve sākṣāt nāhi tāte guru caittya-rūpe*  
*śikṣā-guru haya kṛṣṇa-mahānta-svarūpe*

**“Since one cannot visually experience the presence of the Supersoul, he appears before us as a liberated devotee. Such a spiritual master is none other than Kṛṣṇa Himself.”**

Therefore, if our struggle is one that connects us closer with your mission, if our struggle is one that gives us more reason to think of how to better be of some assistance to you, than why should we desire to free ourselves from such struggle? That struggle will actually bring us closer to Kṛṣṇa by bringing us closer to you. It is said that the *ācārya* is always in the heart of Śrī Govinda. Thus to be close to one is to be close to the other. All living entities are struggling with material nature, but our great boon is that we have been given your shelter to guide us through our struggle. Therefore on this occasion of your most holy and auspicious appearance, let us reaffirm ourselves to your service. The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* says (*Ādi* 12.10), “The order of the spiritual is the active principle in spiritual life.”

In this state of mind, lacking in all qualifications and begging your mercy, we pray,

*kabe hena kṛpā labhiyā e jana*  
*kṛtārtha haibe nātha*  
*śakti-buddhi-hīna āmi ati-dīna*  
*kara more ātma-sātha*

**“When we examine ourselves we find nothing of value. Your mercy is all we are made of. If you are not merciful to us, we can only weep and will not be able to maintain our life anymore.”**

And in this mood we pray to always be of some use to your service. Bowing humbly, we remain  
Your eternal servants in the Nitāi-Gaura-naṭarāja Mandira, ISKCON St. Louis, Missouri.

## St. Petersburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow us, your unworthy servants, to bow down respectfully to your lotus feet.

We have never seen you, because you left this mundane world long before we started our spiritual lives. We were not present where you started the ISKCON movement. We could not witness or take part in your unrepeatable pastimes. We are only your spiritual grandsons, children of your children. But although it is unfortunate that we could not be with you at that time, none of us feels devoid of your mercy, which is immense like an ocean. On the contrary, sometimes it seems that you are even more favorable to us than to your own disciples, because so much mercy is flowing on us. This mercy is there in the recordings of your transcendental voice, in the video records of your divine form and pastimes, in your incomparable books, and in the instructions of your disciples.

How wonderful are the opportunities for practical devotional service, especially in our grand country, where millions of people suffering from the results of endless political and economic experiments are being tossed around, searching for the exit from the stuffy dead end in which Kali-yuga has put them. It may seem to them that they may soon find their desired shelter here in the material world, but by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy all of their material efforts are in vain. There is only one shelter, obtaining which one can actually find safety and peace. It is the house that you built for all humanity—ISKCON. This house will stay for at least the next ten thousand years, withstanding all the storms of the progressing Kali-yuga. Here you have provided a place for everyone. Wherever one lives, whatever are his nationality, race, religion, and convictions, in your books and instructions there is a key to each heart, a word for everyone.

Certainly we cannot comprehend even a drop of the ocean of your greatness and the greatness of what you have done. But still we believe that our duty is to bring everyone to this wonderful house, or at least give everyone a chance to come here, where they can find shelter in the shade of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this remarkable day, the day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, and in expectation of the event which the whole world is getting ready for—the Centennial of the day of your divine descent to our mundane world—unworthy as we are, we pray that you please allow us to continue helping you fulfill your mission despite all obstacles and troubles, overcoming illusion, fear, and idleness. Let us not have any other desires or goals in life. Please help us become just as you are—so humble, pure, and merciful. Please strengthen our intelligence and let us remain the servants of the servants of your servants.

Your injudicious grandchildren from New Navadvīpa Mandira, St. Petersburg, Russia.  
(written by Sutapā dāsa)

## Samara

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this especially auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Today, with economic, social, moral, and other problems all around the world, your books are the beacon that shows the way for all human society. The ship of devotional service you built so expertly is able to take the whole world on board, and we happily note that more and more people are taking shelter on this ship. As this ship moves toward the beacon of love and miracles, only foolish people desire to remain in the ocean of illusion and suffering.

By your causeless mercy and compassion you have given us devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You have given us the holy name, which is the method and the goal simultaneously. Actually it is impossible for us to understand the real value of these things. All we can do is sincerely try to follow your orders and instructions.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

The devotees of the Samara temple, Volga Region, Russia.

## San Diego

*śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala-bhakati-sadma,*  
*bando mui sāvadhāna mate*  
*yāhāra prasāde bhāi, e bhava tariyā yāi,*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti hoy yāhā hoite*

**“The lotus feet of the spiritual master are the only way by which we can attain pure devotional service. I bow down to his lotus feet with great awe and reverence. By his grace one can cross the ocean of material suffering and obtain the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet, to which we owe everything!

Giving up your well-deserved retirement in the holy *dhāma* while advanced in age, you sacrificed your comfort, convenience, and the spiritual sanctity of Kṛṣṇa’s birthplace to come to America. The sole purpose of this formidable journey was to pry away the hopelessly addicted materialists from the tacky mire of worldly existence. You then miraculously sowed a seemingly barren field with an ever-expanding supply of the seeds of devotion. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, orchestrated an unprecedented spiritual rescue and mercifully afforded us the rare opportunity of ongoing service to your liberating lotus feet.

Our very presence in the association of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī is proof of the great blessings of your heroic lotus feet. The California sun, beach, and ideal weather of San Diego daily whisper into our ears, beckoning us to come and be kicked by the material energy. And as you know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have no intelligence. It is only by your mercy and intelligence that we are allowed to remain in New Govardhana, serving the most beautiful Deities in the three worlds, their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī. This is the astounding miracle of your lotus feet!

Those magnanimous lotus feet, the envy of the demigods, are our only means of rescue. We, your aspiring followers, who haven’t the least iota of qualification, can only beg for this mercy to incessantly pour down upon us, lest we be swallowed again by the undiscriminating fanged mouth of *māyā* and quickly forget the power and sanctity of those beautiful feet. Śrīla Prabhupāda, this begging for your mercy is all we wayward denizens of the material world have! It is our only qualification.

Please allow those of us who are holding tightly to your feet to remain fixed and in tow. For those of us who are grappling for a hold, please never withdraw your assistance or kick us away, no matter how deserving of rejection we become. For those of us who are only lazily trailing, or who have fallen behind, we beg you to grant us an undeserved renewal of vigor in the fight for eternal association with you.

Association within your world-wide preaching movement is the tangible manifestation of the protecting nature of your lotus feet. Through your great devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa and your spiritual master, you have ignited the purging fire of this priceless Vaiṣṇava association throughout the entire world. That is surely the only hope for all mankind in this dark Age of Kali.

Though we no longer have the benefit of your personal presence, the cleansing force of your lotus feet continues stomping the material mind-set. Each day, throughout the world, the dust from your feet is cast upon the unsuspecting materialists in the form of transcendental book and *prasādam* distribution. Please force us, at all costs, to completely revitalize the execution of this satisfying task throughout the parched desert of North America.

In spite of many obstacles, including changes in management, the New Govardhana community is experiencing the dynamic potency of your lotus feet. For your pleasure, our book distribution party is slowly coming of age. Śrīmān Vaikuṇṭha dāsa has graciously agreed to serve as our new temple president. Śrīmān Sadānanda dāsa, Upananda dāsa, and Dharmasetu dāsa have expanded their thriving Govinda’s Bliss Bar business. Padayātrā America, under the auspices of Śrīmān Śaunaka dāsa, is regrouping its forces in San Diego. Bhakta Nick is repainting the temple room. Bhakta Kevin, Mahābhārata dāsa, and Śrīmān Mānasa Candra dāsa tirelessly cook wonderful feasts for our Sunday program and festivals around Southern California. The preaching center in Encinitas continues to flourish. The newly formed Prabhupāda Centennial Committee, under Mother Lādalī-devī dāsī, is utilizing the love that our devotees feel for you to manifest many new preaching projects, and Śrīmān Viśvamitra dāsa is taxing his mind to expand our Food for Life program. All of this—the potency of your fatherly, loving lotus feet!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are praying for your protection and guidance as we struggle against the difficulties of the material world. You have instructed that the devotees can best please you by cooperating. Please reveal to us the knowledge of tolerance and the gift of spiritual appreciation that you so perfectly exemplified when dealing with the frustrated youth of a godless civilization. Let us understand the depths of how special each and every Vaiṣṇava is by analyzing your unlimited compassion while transforming the crazed hippies of New York into devotees. Protect us from the deteriorating qualities of offenses and false ego by allowing us to take advantage of your most glorious gift, Śrī Hari-nāma! Help us to help each other as we try to maintain and increase your mission in San Diego by remembering your exemplary determination amid two heart attacks during your voyage to America aboard the S.S. Jaladuta. Only by remembering and pleasing you can we be at all successful in any of these endeavors. And only by surrendering to those fearless and benevolent lotus feet can this be accomplished.

As the monumental year of your illustrious Centennial boldly rises above all else on the horizon, your servants of New Govardhana Dhāma humbly beg again and again that the sweet taste of your glorification will delight our tongues and reveal itself through our actions. We pray to capitalize upon the nectar of your lotus feet as our foremost meditation, always!

Sincerely, your insignificant servants in New Govardhana, San Diego, California.

## Śāntipura

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

Now the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is developing continuously here. As your books are being distributed, more and more people are accepting your mercy and beginning to know themselves and Kṛṣṇa. There are many pious people wanting to approach Kṛṣṇa in Śāntipura. By reading your books, they have come to know the goal of human life. All glories to you! You are more merciful than the Lord. Without your mercy, we would be in the darkness of ignorance continuously, struggling in the distress of material existence. All glories to you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! Now please give us your mercy. As our grandfather, you can be more merciful than the father. We are so fallen, so sick and ignorant. If you don't give us your mercy, who will save us?

We wait for your mercy!

The servants of your servant in Śāntipura, Khasadeśa.

## Śaraṇāgati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you, thank you, thank you a thousand times, thank you, and thank you again.

Since you enjoy hearing good news about your movement, I am happy to inform you about another successful project. Your influence is so widespread that even in the mountain forests of Canada, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is being practiced. The devotees here call their community Śaraṇāgati.

It is a wilderness long abused by the exploitive interests of logging companies and cattle ranchers, but now it is in the hands of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees, who, by following your good instructions, are transforming a wasteland into Vaikuṇṭha.

They work hard. During the week the men wear boots, jeans, and work shirts. They drive muddy old trucks, they are unshaven, and their hands are imbedded with the grit of workers. At first glance one would not know that these are the Hare Kṛṣṇa people. But despite appearances, they truly have the hearts of Vaiṣṇavas. They are always serving one another and are forever talking about Kṛṣṇa. And on Sundays, when the temple room echoes with loud *hari-nāma,* there is no doubt that this is the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Here at the far end of the earth, where bears and deer still roam free, grizzled workers are transformed, as if by magic, into dancing angels. In that far-off valley a brightly painted temple room fills up with glowing devotees wearing *dhotīs,* saris, neck beads, *tilaka,* and shaved heads. Radiant children, adorned like gods and goddesses, also join in the *kīrtana,* and the farm dogs linger outside the door, eagerly waiting for remnants from the feast.

They are free and happy people, Śrīla Prabhupāda. They work cooperatively, and they share a hope of going back to Godhead. Most importantly, they are all your deeply indebted spiritual offspring.

Along with all of those gentle Śaraṇāgati devotees, I want to say that Kṛṣṇa consciousness, as given by you, is a wonderful, wonderful, wonderful thing. Thank you again and again and again.

Your servants at the Śaraṇāgati Farm in British Columbia, Canada.

## Siberia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we all became devotees only by your mercy. It is difficult to imagine what we would have done if you had not gone to America from Vṛndāvana and had not begun this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement in the West. You did not only save millions of people; you alone did the most fantastic thing: you turned around the whole movement of human civilization, changing the path of the whole misdirected society. All your life, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were devoted to one goal, to save humanity. And you have asked us so sincerely and with such humility, “Please, save the world,” that we can’t refuse, and so we have devoted our lives to your mission. There is no better occupation in this world than to take part in this mission. Thus we are humbly prostrating ourselves at your lotus feet, asking for your blessings and help.

By the Lord’s arrangement we are all preaching in Siberia, one of the most degraded places in the world. But in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (Second Canto) it is said that even the people of Siberia can be purified. We can see that this is actually true: many people in Siberia are sincerely attracted to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They are receiving your wonderful books and eagerly hearing the preaching of your sincere followers. We hope that in the future thousands and even millions of Siberians will become devotees.

So, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our repeated obeisances at your lotus feet, and please give us your mercy so we may make progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please give us the strength and power to preach and thus satisfy you by helping to fulfill your transcendental mission. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, be merciful to us and forgive all our imperfections. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servants in Siberia, Russia.

## Sierra Leone

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance anniversary, please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

The political leaders of the world today are scheming for a new world order. Due to their illusion, they think the creation of a world order depends on them. Little do they know that the new world order has already been created with the inception of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Nor do the so-called leaders of today know that Your Divine Grace, ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya,* A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, is the creator of the new order. The evidence of this new world order can be seen in the way your mission is spreading throughout the world like wildfire, and also in the way the bewildered and disillusioned people of the world are gradually turning to the literatures Your Divine Grace left behind for solace and guidance.

All over the world, from the so-called first world to the third world, people are daily accepting the superiority of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy. Without force or coercion, which were used to instill the ideas of democracy and communism, men and women, young and old, people from all races and from all different walks of life, are choosing Kṛṣṇa consciousness principles as their life-standard. It is obvious that in a not-too-distant future the success of our communities, the peace found in the lives of those who have taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness practices, and the purity of your disciples will convince the world of the “new” world order which has existed since ancient times yet which is suitable for chaotic, directionless modern man.

Devotees all over the world are caught up with the ecstasy of preparing for your Centennial celebration next year. Every *yātrā* is either planning to do something or trying to be part of the grand global Centennial in Māyāpur, India. Unfortunately, in this part of the world, sub-Sahara Africa, war and political instability are tearing the nations apart. About five years ago, in 1990, a similar war in a neighboring country, Liberia, claimed the lives of seven devotees. Nearly 90% of Sierra Leone has been ravaged by a war of rebellion, which is being waged in the name of establishing democracy. However, although as yet we are not physically arranging anything for your Centennial—since we do not know what condition the capital city of Freetown, which is now under siege, will be in by 1996—the situation has led to the opening of a preaching center in the republic of Guinea. We hope this expansion will serve as an alternative preaching field should Sierra Leone go the way of Rwanda, Somalia, and Liberia, where genocide has taken place as a result of civil war.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our hope rests in the fact that you live in your instructions. Our good fortune is having one of your genuine representatives as our spiritual master. His Holiness Bhakti Tīrtha Swami planted the seed of devotion in our hearts and has been the most caring and careful tender of the garden of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We pray that we can follow his footsteps in the same way that he follows those of Your Divine Grace. The one who walks with Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva at his right hand, melting fear in people’s hearts with his charming smile, has knitted our attachment for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and for the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Our only wish is to remain your devotees life after life.

The servants of your servants in Sierra Leone, West Africa.

## Singapore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Our realization is that only by taking shelter of your lotus feet can we make any spiritual progress. Although Śrī Kṛṣṇa is there, a disciple’s prime consideration is his spiritual master’s mercy .

It is only your *pāda* that can really help us. If, however, we try to deviate from your lotus feet even slightly, we can really see how useless we become. Unless we are really praying for your transcendental mercy, we see that we are not going anywhere at all.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, whereas others may aspire to be with Śrī Kṛṣṇa, etc., our only wish is to be able to serve you with our lives and souls eternally. The more we see your qualities of supreme humility and complete dependence on the mercy of your *guru* and Kṛṣṇa, the more we realize your greatness. Never at any time did you deviate from this principle. You always honored your spiritual master, and we want to have similar faith in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You gave all credit to your spiritual master without expecting any yourself, although single-handedly you changed and are still changing the whole world’s direction and the lives of its conditioned souls. If we can have for you but a small portion of the transcendental love you had for your spiritual master, we know for certain that our lives will be completely successful.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very thankful that we have a dear spiritual master like you, but it is most unfortunate that you have got such useless souls as us as your servants.

We simply pray on this most auspicious day that you kindly excuse the countless offenses we have committed at your divine lotus feet.

Always praying to remain your servants,  
Your servants in Singapore.

## Switzerland

The conchshell blows three times. It is 7:20 a.m. While the *Govindam* song fades out, some sip *caraṇāmṛta,* but most devotees now gather around your *vyāsāsana*. The all-auspicious time has come for your *guru-pūjā*. A devotee starts singing, “Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda! Jaya Jaya Prabhupāda!”

*śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala-bhakati-sadma,*  
*bando mui sāvadhāna mate*  
*yāhāra prasāde bhāi, e bhava tariyā yāi,*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti hoy yāhā hoite*

Every morning, in every ISKCON temple around the world, after greeting Their Lordships on the altar, your disciples, granddisciples, and whoever else is at the temple offer you *guru-pūjā*. Although this is institutionalized and is happening every day, it is not mechanical, nor does it ever lose its dynamic spiritual potency. Your daily *guru-pūjā* is as fresh and new as the morning sun.

“The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way by which we can attain pure devotional service. I bow down to those lotus feet with great awe and reverence. By his grace one can cross the ocean of material suffering and obtain the mercy of Kṛṣṇa.”

While the *kīrtana* leader sings Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s *Śrī Guru-vandanā,* the devotees come forward one after another and offer you flower petals and *praṇāmas*. Most devotees fall flat on the floor like a stick, offering *daṇḍavats,* thus expressing their full surrender at your lotus feet. Having received your causeless mercy through the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, they are happy to surrender their lives to Your Divine Grace, to become your eternal servants and assist you in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the universe.

*guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya,*  
*ār na koriho mane āśa*  
*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei sei uttama-gati,*  
*ye prasāde pūre sarva āśa*

As the pure representative of Kṛṣṇa, you receive the same worship as the Lord. The *pūjārī* offers you incense, a ghee lamp, water, flowers, etc. Nondevotees may wonder how a human being can sit on a gorgeous seat and receive this worship. But little do they know that you accept this worship not for yourself but on behalf of Kṛṣṇa. Little do they know that you are not a conditioned soul like them but fully self-realized, beyond mundane desires for fame and distinction. Little do they know about the ancient Vedic process of surrendering one’s life to the spiritual master, as you yourself so perfectly surrendered to your *guru,* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Little do they know of the ecstasy the devotees feel when they worship Your Divine Grace. And certainly these people do not know that they can become happy only when they worship a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa.

“My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from his lotus mouth. Attachment to his lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires.”

As the *guru-pūjā* goes on, your wonderful personality remains the center of all attention. The Lord on the altar enjoys and encourages your worship. Your presence is felt very strongly, and devotees experience great bliss as they chant and dance together. Congregational chanting is the *yajña* for this age, and those who take part in it have no doubts in their mind: chanting together, serving your lotus feet together, and feasting together is the blissful and safe way back to Godhead.

*cakhu-dāna dilo yei, janme janme prabhu sei,*  
*dibyā jñān hṛde prokāśito*  
*prema-bhakti yāhā hoite, avidyā-vināśa yāte*  
*vede gāy yāhāra carito*

The movement you started is not based on some vague ideas or sentimental thoughts. It is strongly rooted in *sādhu-śāstra-guru*. What you have given us is fully authorized by the *Vedas* and has all the potency of the Lord Himself. Your soldiers who go out as book distributors and your preachers feel the power of the pure and perfect knowledge you have given us. Materialists, scientists, pseudo spiritualists, and Māyāvādīs have no hope of upholding their imperfect philosophies when confronted with the Kṛṣṇa conscious science, as long as it is presented the way you taught us.

“The spiritual master opens my darkened eyes and fills my heart with transcendental knowledge. He is my lord birth after birth. From him ecstatic *prema* emanates; by him ignorance is destroyed. The Vedic scriptures sing of his character.”

Seeing Your Divine Grace sitting graciously on the *vyāsāsana—*dressed in a silk saffron *dhotī* and *kurtā,* garlanded with beautiful flowers—is indeed pleasing to the eye. The expression on your lotus face is grave, yet warm and kind. Your mind is fixed on Kṛṣṇa, and your intelligence is always active in finding ways to bring the conditioned souls back to Kṛṣṇa. By your mercy and inspiration alone the devotees are able to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

*śrī-guru karuṇā-sindhu, adhama-janāra bandhu,*  
*lokanātha lokera jīvana*  
*hā hā prabhu koro dayā, deho more pada-chāyā*  
*ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tri-bhuvana*

The *kīrtana* started peacefully. The sounds of the voices, the *karatālas,* and the *mṛdaṅgas* were soft, and the devotees all looked calm and grave, their bodies moving only slightly. But now the voices become much louder, the faces of the devotees glow, and their bodies turn in circles and jump in the air. Some who before stood to the side with serious expressions on their faces are now swept away by the power and bliss of the congregational chanting. No doubt the devotees are tasting the nectar for which they are always hankering. By your mercy alone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, they find it.

“Our spiritual master is the ocean of mercy, the friend of the poor, and the lord and master of the devotees. O master! Be merciful unto us. Give us the shade of your lotus feet. May your fame be spread all over the three worlds. We take shelter of your lotus feet.”

As the conchshell blows again at the end of the *ārati,* the *kīrtana* reaches a crescendo as the devotees chant “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Jaya Prabhupāda!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your *guru-pūjā* will go on forever.

Your eternal servants in Switzerland.  
(written by Gaura-līlā dāsa)

## Śyāma-kuṇḍa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories unto you, who are the most illustrious transcendental savior of this Kali-yuga. As a father carefully instructs his children so they may properly mature, you are providing the immature, fallen, childlike people of this age with the divine instruction by which they may see the Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and regain their natural, sheltered position at His lotus feet.

We would really like to know how to appropriately address such a great soul as yourself in order to glorify you. Without you, we would never have known who Kṛṣṇa is, we would never have known whether we were the body or not. To not know about this is terrible! The mercy comes from you, and now we know a little about this. How happy we are! Unlike many Vaiṣṇavas, or great souls, we are stupid materialists here, yet still we are trying to worship you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day.

No doubt, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a great soul, a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* empowered by Kṛṣṇa to preach the glories of His holy name. You are the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. We are very pleased and happy, because you saved us from the dark well of material existence. Your spiritual master told you to preach the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English language. And we promise to go on helping your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in each town and village of the world, as Lord Caitanya predicted five hundred years ago. Because of your mercy, we are full of hope.

We promise you that we will always chant our sixteen rounds every day, trying to avoid the offenses, and that we will follow the four regulative principles and attend the spiritual programs with full enthusiasm. We promise to somehow or other stay in the association of devotees, living peacefully and without false prestige. And we also promise to try to please you by distributing your books, managing your temple, preaching to guests, worshiping the Deities, cooking, etc.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow us to become your book distributors. Please send us more and more books, and send us more and more nice people who like to hear the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, free us from envy, laziness, and false ego; allow us to always chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* at every second of our lives, which are so short and miserable. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow us to be spiritually strong so that we do not fall victim to *māyā*. We will remember your teachings forever.

With high esteem, we glorify you on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your attentive servants in Śyāma-kuṇḍa, Khasadeśa.

## Taiwan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

ISKCON centers, as embassies of the spiritual world, are a shelter for conditioned souls. It is here that they can be relieved of the suffering of material existence and revive their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Desiring to spread the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, we have established a small center on the island of Taiwan. Twenty-five years ago this island resembled a tropical paradise, with thick forests and lush vegetation. Now, with the influence of Kali-yuga and a surge in economic development, it has be come a nightmare of highways and factories. The progress made in most other countries over a period of one hundred years took place here in twenty years. While such progress may be due to the pious deeds of its citizens, it does not facilitate their deliverance from the clutches of material existence.

People living in such places can often be easily influenced to new ways, and we hope to bring them to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. However, we must be mindful of the great responsibility such a mission entails. If they are taught wrongly in the beginning, confusion and distrust will take root in the minds of the people. The seed of *bhakti* must be properly planted and protected from all unfavorable elements. The present civilization is in chaos, but it is still possible to be Kṛṣṇa conscious and see the spiritual world.

On this auspicious day we pray for the blessings of Śrīla Prabhupāda and all the Vaiṣṇavas so that we may be able to effectively execute the mission of Lord Caitanya and help Taiwan’s twenty million people make some tangible progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s most unworthy servants in Taiwan.

## Tampa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to you!

Celebrate the day  
When you appeared from far away.

Like a mystery in the night,  
You suddenly appeared … then took flight.

Your powerful force  
Changed the world’s course

And now we must finish your work.

Traveling *saṅkīrtana* in ’65,  
Arduous journey, barely alive.

Arriving in Boston, what a sad situation;  
Is it possible to create a devotee congregation?

With Kṛṣṇa’s desire  
You helped us higher.

And now we must finish your work.

Prabhupāda, you worked incredibly fast;  
We competed to keep up, not to be last.

With such dedication unknown,  
ISKCON was aggressively grown.

Solid in truth,  
You gave all proof.

And now we must finish your work.

The demon’s challenge was quickly defeated,  
Then with love you spiritually treated.

Such compassion and love you gave,  
Forcing us from our ego grave,

Fixing the wrong  
with a beautiful song (Hare Kṛṣṇa).

Now we must finish your work.

Turn on the holy name, tune in the spiritual master,  
Tell everyone, you said, and progress will be faster.

Drop out of sense gratification, present my books.  
This will create devotees from conditioned crooks.

Kṛṣṇa’s desire  
is your fire.

Now we must finish your work.

Prabhupāda, your life’s work is to give  
Hare Kṛṣṇa, so everyone will eternally live.

Let us give you the gift you desire:  
to remain life-long in the preaching fire.

Devotees, centers, and books  
Attract your looks.

Now we must finish your work.

Please bless us.

The devotees at the ISKCON center in Tampa, Florida.

## Tauranga

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your merciful lotus feet, and accept our heartfelt gratitude for allowing us to approach them.

Since your last Vyāsa-pūjā, we have seen historic advances in your ISKCON movement. You wanted your followers to be exemplary in their character and to cooperate, and now because of your love for us and our paltry efforts to reciprocate that real love, your desire is becoming reality. Otherwise, how would it be possible for us to go through such major disagreements and be able to, on the one hand, subordinate our own egos or beliefs or determination for the common goal (your service), and on the other stay together as a stronger, more united organization. This is truly another proof of your perfect potency to manage and lead a spiritual movement, even in your physical absence, through your surrendered, empowered followers!

No one else could do this. In Kali-yuga people are fighting at the smallest provocation, or even without any. Even husband and wife cannot stay together. As soon as there is any disagreement, people are ready to fight, separate, or even kill each other. When businesses become successful, so-called partners (or friends) are at each other’s throats. Who can inspire the world to develop spiritual unity and cooperation? Who will attract the populace by their pure friendship and mood of caring urgency? Who will impress upon us the necessity of keeping Kṛṣṇa and *guru* in the center? The politicians? The philosophers? The scientists? The priests or evangelists? The communists? The new agers? No! No one stands a chance except you! Unless one is empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one can only be an agent of misfortune (Kali).

You alone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are creating individuals of such character that they can stay together; and more than that, work together; and ultimately show the world how to do it! You have created a Society—the International Society for Krishna Consciousness—that can and is uniting all pious people the world over! This is a true miracle! All glories to Your Divine Grace; all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please allow us to continue to have your mercy through your instructions and serious followers; please allow us to manifest your miracles and glories more and more, to all people, everywhere.

This is a fitting way to prepare for the most important celebration of your 1996 Centennial. If we, the members of your ISKCON, can manifest your will, then that will be the greatest achievement, the greatest gift to offer to you, today, next year, and every year, nay, every day. That is the greatest cause for celebration; and without it, how can we celebrate? Therefore, we think that your ISKCON has made an historic step forward, by your grace.

Sometimes in the past when devotees have differed, some have left, but this time it seems different: no one is leaving. Rather, the determination to reconcile differences, to really accept the humble position, and to get on with the work of establishing and spreading Lord Caitanya’s mission is manifesting, without the weakness of splitting or loss of valuable members. This is a sign of the unity you talked about, the strength of character, the spiritual strength which we are imbibing from you. Together we stand, divided we fall.

Here in Tauranga, we’re feeling your presence influencing our hearts, inspiring our minds and intellects as we struggle to face the challenges and overcome the setbacks that make us more sober and stronger in your mission. Please continue to tolerate our deviations from your association, and continue your loving pushing and pulling us back to our real consciousness, Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

His Holiness Prabhaviṣṇu Mahārāja has suggested we call our group Śrī Varāha-Gaurāṅga-kṣetra. We would like to live up to that name, and as His Holiness Mukunda Mahārāja said when he visited, we should become “the Tauranga Gaurāṅgīs, lifting the burden of the earth.” We have also been very much encouraged and inspired by His Holiness Jagadīśa Mahārāja, another of your devoted and visionary sons, to push ahead in our endeavor to build a small, unified family of families. We aspire to become an example of a simple Kṛṣṇa conscious community dedicated to cooperation, love, and trust for the benefit of ourselves, our children, and our children’s children, etc., and of those whom we may contact or attract. We pray to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa that Jagadīśa Mahārāja may quickly regain full health and strength and again be able to give us his valuable and much-needed advice and support in the great challenges we face.

We are weak and proud, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and slow in taking your instructions fully to heart. Still, our faith is strong in Your Divine Grace and in the potency of your instructions, and also in your causeless love for Kṛṣṇa and all of us.

Begging to remain hopeful and determined to become the servants of your servants, by your grace,  
The aspiring devotees in Tauranga, New Zealand.

## Tokyo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. May we always take the shelter of your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day it is very hard to put down on paper the proper words necessary to express praise and show our gratitude for the mercy you have so kindly given us. Therefore with folded hands and tears in our eyes we ask you to please accept our heart-felt thanks.

Each morning we sing the words *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādād na gatiḥ kuto ’pi / dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*. The key words in this verse we always remember, even though the influences of this age make this hard to do. Just knowing you are here to guide us through the difficult times gives us the strength to combat our individual vices and the inspiration to go on. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taken us into your heart and have shared with us the most treasured knowledge you keep there. You have told us to stop all our nonsense and to take to this process of hearing, chanting, and remembering the wonderful name, qualities, and pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa. If we do this we will finish up the cycle of birth and death and go back to our real home. Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this material world all relationships—including those we have with our parents, friends, husbands, wives, and children—are centered not on love but on lust, causing everyone misery in the end. But you have taught us how to dovetail our loving propensity and enjoy true love.

If we truly want to show you how grateful we are for everything you have given us, then our actions must speak louder than our words. Just as at our initiation we promised before the Lord, you, the sacred fire, and the assembled devotees to always follow your instructions, so again on this day we re-pledge ourselves to do whatever you ask of us. We are your most undeserving servants. We pray that in some way we can do something that makes you happy.

Your devotees in Tokyo, Japan.

## Tucson

Bewildered minds of Vaiṣṇavas look for something new;  
Searching outside your shelter, no one’s got a clue.  
Yet true liberation is not found in the groves;  
But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the one who truly knows.

Without you, Gurudev, no one has a prayer;  
Lives frought with hopelessness and dispair.  
You’ve given your formula on the *Bhāgavatam* pages,  
Absolute truth spoken by saints and great sages.

Rescuing us from such a sorry state,  
Instill Godly principles before it’s too late.  
Chanting the holy name to purify our heart,  
We progress slowly, but finally it’s a start.

You said to “be good at many, but expert in one,”  
So through *prasād* distribution many hearts we’ve won.  
Your Govinda’s restaurant is the talk of the town,  
Quality presentation, for which we are renowned.

Hundreds coming daily, we do not find this rare;  
Temple ambiance and beauty are far beyond compare.  
Elsewhere they’ve looked for bliss—time only wasted;  
Spiritual food so good, never before have they tasted.

No one’s lasted in the desert for any length of time;  
The threefold miseries all seem to combine.  
But any austerity that’s done for your pleasure,  
The reciprocation felt is far beyond measure.

By pursuing our selfish desires, we are through.  
So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we make this vow to you:  
We’ll preach and distribute your mercy until the very end,  
And turn our rascal mind into our best friend.

To fulfill our obligation, you’ve made a simple request.  
All our time, strength, and knowledge we must now invest  
To broadcast Kṛṣṇa’s glories, giving everyone a chance;  
We’ll wait till we’re back home to witness the *rāsa* dance.

Your unworthy servants in Tucson, Arizona.  
(written by Sandāmini-devī dāsī)

## Turkey

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who are the perfect representative of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the most perfect servitor of the Lord. Your lotus feet are a source of all benedictions and complete shelter within this world. Personally, I have no qualification for offering devotional writings in praise of Your Divine Grace. Please bestow your causeless mercy upon this fallen soul so that this body may become an instrument in the service of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I always lament that I could never meet you personally, but I am having your association through your books. When I read your books for the first time, I felt a strong desire to meet you, but then the devotees told me that you had already left this planet and I was very disappointed. Still, I chose to become your follower.

About two years back, the GBC asked me to go to Turkey to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness there. I went to Turkey, and with the help of Vrajeśvarī-devī dāsī and Bhakta Paramjit, we started our Turkey preaching programs. In Istanbul we distributed your books and *prasādam* and were trying to open a Hare Kṛṣṇa restaurant. But because Istanbul happened to be a very fanatical place, we were forced to leave. We went to Antalya, which is a tourists’ place and where the people are more open-minded. Now we have some land, and temple construction is going on. This year, before the Gaura-pūrṇimā Festival, I met His Holiness Vipramukhya Swami, and he told me that you had personally instructed him to open up a temple in Istanbul. Finally, because it was the desire of a pure devotee and a pure devotee’s desire is nondifferent from Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s desire, the temple manifested. It was your causeless mercy to use us as the instruments.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please purify our hearts of all selfish motives and give us the intelligence to fulfill your desires. We depend upon your blessings and mercy to make all the impossible things possible. This year we completed the Turkish translation of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* and it is being printed for the first time by the Italian BBT. With Harivilāsa Prabhu, the GBC representative for Turkey, we developed a special plan to import your books and distribute them in Turkey. It is our humble prayer to you, a pure devotee of the Lord, to please give us the blessings so that we can fulfill these plans for your and Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure.

One hundred and eight glories to the topmost swanlike devotee of the Lord, His Divine Grace Śrī Śrīmad Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Goswami Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants who are trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Turkey.  
(written by Airāvata dāsa, Regional Secretary)

## Turku

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

As Founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON and a *mahā-bhāgavata* devotee, you are the *śikṣā-guru* of our entire Society. Your importance for us reflects the importance of the book *Bhāgavata:* “The messages of the book *Bhāgavata* . . . have to be received from the devotee *Bhāgavata*” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.18, purport) “The number-one *Bhāgavata* is the established personality of devotee, and the other is the message of the Godhead” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.12, purport).

Real knowledge is that which comes from the *Vedas,* and real intelligence is to assimilate that knowledge: “The ability not only to read many books on different subject matters but to understand them and apply them when necessary is intelligence” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 10.34, purport). The shocking scenes of degraded hippie life that greeted you upon your arrival in America did not arouse repulsion but compassion. As an expression of this compassion, you gave both knowledge of the ultimate goal of life and the intelligence to understand and apply that knowledge—and you are still giving these.

Real knowledge is there in the *Vedas,* but without your blessings, your purports, we cannot understand the meaning of *śāstra: “*By studying the *Vedas,* the *Vedānta-sūtra,* and the *Upaniṣads,* one may find processes for purifying the consciousness and elevating oneself to the transcendental platform. But for the conditioned souls of Kali-yuga, such means are beyond reach” (*Renunciation Through Wisdom,* p. 237). Your purports have brought the perfect Vedic knowledge within our reach. They are our *śruti*. According to Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, *śruti* includes all glorifications of Kṛṣṇa; Śrīla Viśvanātha “approved the songs of Ṭhākura Narottama dāsa to be as good as Vedic *mantras*. And this is so because of the subject matter. The language is immaterial”(*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.10.20, purport).

The transcendental *smṛti-śāstras* are literatures indicating the ultimate goal of life and written by liberated souls. Your books are our s*mṛti* as well as our *śruti*.

“Anyone who is trying to be aloof from this Kṛṣṇa consciousness society and yet engage in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is living in a great hallucination” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.9.11, purport). Those who think that they can become Kṛṣṇa conscious without knowing your books are living in a similar hallucination. The *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* confirms that so-called devotional service which ignores *śruti* and *smṛti* is simply a disturbance in society. We may study many books—Vedic, Upavedic, esoteric, apasampradāyic, or whatever—but if in our eagerness to become “learned” we neglect to regularly study your unadulterated teachings, our spiritual progress will be obstructed.

Please free us from the tendency to chase after material erudition, because the ensuing false pride robs us of our ability to accept your mercy. Please allow us to understand our lowly position: “You are a fool. You are not qualified to study Vedānta philosophy, and therefore You must always chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.” Lord Caitanya was thus instructed by His *guru* as He played the role of a devotee in Kali-yuga. Unfortunately, we are not playing a role: we are genuine Kali-yuga devotees, foolish and unqualified but trying to develop unflinching devotion to you, our *ācārya* and *śikṣā-guru*. Through our faith and devotion to you, your writings, and the present ISKCON *gurus,* who are the embodiment of your instructions, all the imports of the Vedic knowledge will be revealed to us.

We, the devotees of Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra, pledge to make 1996 the year that we worship Your Divine Grace with our intelligence by studying your teachings and trying to apply them in our daily lives.

Your servants at Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra, Turku, Finland.

## Udhampur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most fallen obeisances at the dust of your lotuslike feet. You are the savior of the whole world.

This year again, on the anniversary of your divine appearance, we are getting an opportunity to glorify your divine personality. It seems to us the most difficult task, because your glories are infinite. So we beg for your mercy and blessings so that we can express our feelings in this humble offering.

According to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, in this age of quarrel (Kali) there are no *varṇas* and *āśramas,* so everyone is a *śūdra*. You appeared on this day to train *brāhmaṇas* to lead society. You built a home in which the whole world can live. So if you desire, please give us a minute fraction of your intelligence so that the job you have entrusted to us can be carried out successfully to benefit the entire world.

We, your granddisciples at the Śrīla Prabhupāda Ashram, are trying to follow your divine instructions under the guidance of your dear disciple His Holiness Navayogendra Swami Mahārāja, whose only concern is to satisfy you. We feel that you must be pleased with us to give us such an able spiritual guide. We feel indebted to you for this gift.

Devotees all over the world are busy preparing for your Centennial celebrations next year. So, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us be instrumental in glorifying your divine personality by accomplishing the Śrīla Prabhupāda Ashram project. We have full faith that you will empower us to successfully complete this service.

In the end, we all beg at the dust of your lotuslike feet to give us love for your lotus feet (*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati*). We are unable to attain this love without your causeless mercy. But we know that if you are merciful upon us and grant us this love, then the ultimate goal of our lives will already be reached (*ei sei uttama-gati*) and all our desires to serve you will be fulfilled *(je prasāde pūre sarva āśā*).

Your eternal servants at the Śrīla Prabhupāda Ashram in Udhampur, India.

## Umea

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, the ISKCON-BBT Founder-*ācārya*! On this occasion of unlimited auspiciousness, let me thank you from my heart, which before was enclosed with a barbed-wire fence (although it was only a junk yard). By your mercy only, here in the north of Sweden there is a tiny but lively Govinda’s restaurant, with up to forty persons a day taking *prasādam*. We also have a Sunday feast, with youngsters especially chanting nicely, in the way that they have learned from the Kṛṣṇa conscious rock bands Shelter and 108. Sometimes teachers bring school groups, and the local Parents and Teachers Association, in conjunction with a night school, has asked us to give a course in cooking. We fervently pray that our small efforts will contribute to your pleasure, despite our many defects. Please be merciful and allow us to go on with our service, although we are not actually fit for such an exalted blessing. Falling in the dust of your lotus feet, we beg to remain your servants.

Your devotees in Umea, Sweden.

## Uppsala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**The Gift**

(1) Yesterday  
knowledge meant  
a university degree

Today  
knowledge means  
*ahaṁ brahmāsmi*

(2) Yesterday  
service meant  
to slave for money

Today  
service means  
*vaiṣṇava-dāsānudāsa*

(3) Yesterday  
peace meant  
the end of war

Today  
Peace means  
*nitya kṛṣṇa-dāsa*

(4) Yesterday  
love meant  
to satisfy one’s senses

Today  
love means  
*rādhā-kṛṣṇa-prema*

(5) Yesterday  
life meant  
to eat and sleep and mate

Today  
life means  
*vṛndāvana nitya-līlā*

(6) Yesterday  
happiness meant  
to intoxicate yourself

Today  
happiness means  
*kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*

(7) Yesterday  
God meant  
invention of the weak

Today  
God means  
*govindam ādi-puruṣam*

(8) Yesterday  
death meant  
the end of all

Today  
death means  
*nitya-līlā-praviṣṭa*

(9) Yesterday  
full of  
misery and pain,  
has gone forever

Todayhas come  
with bliss and joy,  
a gift from Prabhupāda.

Your servants in Uppsala, Sweden  
(written by Prapūjaka dāsa)

## Vancouver

**Loyalty and Love:**  
**The Golden Threads that Hold ISKCON Together**

No matter how much they criticized,  
you never let us go;  
you loved us and saved us  
and allowed us to grow.

You built us a house  
and placed us within;  
you taught us good housekeeping  
for Kṛṣṇa, our best friend.

You gave us spiritual truth  
in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*  
to build our understanding  
as to whom we truly belong.

You made a worldwide family  
based on love and trust,  
and our loyalty is to you,  
who loved us so much.

You sacrificed everything  
at your divine master’s feet;  
we bow down, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
at your divine lotus feet.

You woke us up and washed our brains  
by teaching us to chant;  
we did not really understand  
the extent of your helping hand.

We were children of the sixties—  
hippies, beatniks, the bizarre—  
but your eyes saw only our spiritual spark;  
your love for us has kept you deep within our hearts.

You trusted us by giving us  
everything we need  
to go back to Godhead—  
your teachings we must heed.

But it’s your love and loyalty  
we can never forget;  
you are the only pure devotee  
who came from the Indian subcontinent.

To love and be loved  
is real spiritual life;  
you taught us this example  
with your very own life.

Every purport centers  
upon your Lover’s feet;  
as you glorify His pastimes,  
we see two Lovers meet.

Oh, blessed pure devotee,  
you taught us to be true;  
pure love is the gift  
that we received from you.

Loyalty to that love  
is the woven cloth of faith,  
the very fabric of our ISKCON  
on which everything is based.

The devotees at ISKCON Vancouver, Canada.

## Vienna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Of course, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am not at all qualified to write anything in praise of such an exalted person as you. Just as the earth began to tremble under the weight of Dhruva Mahārāja’s lotus feet, the whole world now is changing rapidly under your powerful influence. All my acceptable qualities originate in you, because they are the effects of your mighty preaching. I have nothing to offer except my gratitude and my surrender. My writing can therefore be compared to the offering of a ghee lamp to the sun, because it is simply a manifestation of your mercy towards me. Just as a ghee lamp is nothing but an expansion of the sun, my whole existence in this glorious movement is nothing but an extension of your mercy. But whereas the flame of the ghee lamp is pure, being nothing but an emanation of the sun, my words are full of impurity and ignorance because out of unreasonable envy I am still hankering after the wretched idea of controlling and enjoying the material nature. Therefore I am missing the splendid opportunity to surrender unto your divine lotus feet, and so I can only pray to you to look kindly at my honest endeavor and relieve me of these burning desires.
