You are offering me this opportunity to praise and glorify you as a means of my purification, and this makes me eternally indebted to you.

By your divine teachings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have saved the world, for this demoniac civilization was declining rapidly, becoming more and more abominable in the name of scientific, social, political, economic, and technical progress.

Despite your advanced age and so many inconveniences, you came to the filthy place called New York City and from there traveled to so many other filthy places all over the world, whether hot or cold, rainy or sunny, pleasant or unpleasant. You had in mind only one consideration—the mission entrusted to you by your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. On February 25, 1935, on the occasion of your Guru Mahārāja’s sixty-second birthday, you recited a Vyāsa-pūjā poem during the evening ceremony in the small Bombay center.

Since then these four lines have become especially famous:

Absolute is sentient  
Thou hast proved,  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast removed.

When this poem reached Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, this particular stanza made him so happy that he made a point of showing it to all of his guests. These few lines carry the essence of personal, transcendental reality in them, and they appear like shining jewels emanating a divine light of spiritual splendor and personal concern; it is this light which will soothe all the sufferings and all the troubles of all people in this world, because its rays have a gentle and cooling effect surpassing even the sublime qualities of moonlight. They are like soft and cool breezes and like showers of pleasing rain on the bodies and minds of people captured in the blazing fire of impersonal calamity.

Because the inhabitants of this planet have forgotten Kṛṣṇa, their Supreme Lord and Master, they have plunged deeply into the venomous ocean of materialism and impersonalism, the source of all the problems in this world. “Eat, drink, and be merry, for tomorrow we may die” and “You are God. I am God. Everyone is God. We are all one. It’s all one. It’s all nothing”—such philosophies are dominating the world, depriving people of their natural heritage of *sac-cid-ānanda*. These philosophies are being spread widely by demoniac persons to capture people tightly in their hopelessness and control them by unscrupulously exploiting their misled desires. These demons are always ready to keep people in the darkest ignorance, for in this position they can all be easily bled to death. And there seems to be no way out. People are forced to drink their own blood in the name of humanism, pluralism, and democracy, absurd concepts put on their bent shoulders by bribed politicians, and the foolish people are gratefully accepting being beaten on their faces again and again, their minds totally charmed by the poisonous snakes of total cheating: “You don’t need a *guru*. Be your own *guru.*” After elections, the prostitutelike politicians can be seen on the TV screen, patting the mob on the back: “The citizens of Austria have proved their democratic maturity. They have made a wise decision.” Such an unbearable bluff—and no way out.

But you came, our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda. You brought the supreme message from Goloka Vṛndāvana. You brought the supreme personal message from the supreme personal place, and you are flooding this lost desert with your teachings, with your books. Simply by your book distribution and your preaching, all impersonality will be removed. And all phenomena connected with this basic impersonalism found in people who are forgetful of our supreme well-wisher and friend will also be removed. All these frauds in the name of science, progress, politics, economic development, etc., will come to an end. People will cry and laugh in joy, being relieved of this cruel weight, which has smashed their very lives. Therefore devotees go out to distribute your books, and although they may not be scientists, politicians, economic experts, or big businessmen, by the supreme power of your books they are breaking through the deceitful demoniac arrangements, penetrating people’s illusory desires, and bringing people back to their senses. Simply by distributing one *Kṛṣṇa* book—which the demons may call “mythological stories for children”—the book distributors surpass all the learned and sophisticated endeavors of all the so-called great men of this world. This is your ingenuity, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by which you are the foremost genius in the world. People will realize their foolishness more and more, and instead of continuing to accept false and heartless masters, they will bow down before you and Lord Kṛṣṇa, and all their problems will fly far away with the divine sound of Kṛṣṇa’s names, which will be heard in every town and village.

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have fully taken shelter at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa and your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. By strictly serving their mission, you have saved the whole world. We throw ourselves down in the dust of your divine lotus feet, and we pray again and again with careful attention, “Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us accept more and more the *cintāmaṇi*-like substance of your mission. We are fully dependent on you. Please fan the spark of our devotion unto you so that we may be able to fully catch the spirit of your preaching mission and serve you in it through our spiritual masters, your dedicated servants and representatives. All glories to you, the most exalted personality, the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, Your Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!”

Your insignificant servants in Vienna, Austria.

## Villaggio Hare Krishna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. On this grand day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, a year before your glorious Centennial, devotees all over the world are looking toward you as a wonderful example due to your transcendental activities.

Even though you are not physically present, nevertheless the preaching of the holy name, as you have taught, continues spreading, as it was foretold by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

When you were here with us, with great vigor you taught everyone to chant the holy name and follow the regulative principles. You tirelessly encouraged the devotees in this way.

Your personal projects, such as the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir in Vṛndāvana and the Chaitanya Chandrodaya Mandir in Māyāpur, are developing more and more. At Śrīdhām Māyāpur there is a plan to build a wonderful city. It will start to be revealed in 1997, with world-famous groups of architects participating in the construction, directed by members of ISKCON. This was all predicted by Lord Nityānanda: “A wonderful temple will be revealed, and from there the divine devotional service to Lord Gaurāṅga will spread everywhere.” With your merciful appearance in this world, the planet is turning into Vaikuṇṭha.

Wherever your ISKCON centers are flourishing, the influence of Kali-yuga is surely becoming minimized. Especially powerful weapons in this fight against the evil influence of Kali-yuga are your miraculous books, veritable Deities that enter people’s homes every day and purify the atmosphere. Your blessed descent is evidence that Kṛṣṇa is showering His blessings on the inhabitants of this planet by spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Your love for humanity is greatly evident, because with your powerful Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching you have transformed the hearts of millions of people. If I could help distribute your books, that would be your merciful blessing on an ignorant conditioned soul.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow me to express my gratitude for the gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you have kindly presented to me. May your glories and books spread more and more.

All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All the devotees in Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa are thankful to you.

Your insignificant servants at Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Recently we celebrated the 20thanniversary of the installation of Śrī-Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, and devotees who witnessed you install Their Lordships narrated your glories. They described how anxious you were to establish this temple—how you practically didn’t eat or sleep during the five days leading up to the installation. We heard how the temple room was packed with many hundreds of people and how you beamed with full satisfaction as you performed the first *ārati* to Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma. You invited several guests to speak, and when some of them indirectly stated that not all of your disciples were actually Vaiṣṇavas, you defended us. Then you turned to your followers and gravely said, “Now, don’t disappoint me.” You took many such risks for us.

The year before the temple opened, you gave many instructions regarding how you wanted the affairs of your Vṛndāvana home conducted, and you issued this formula for success: “*Vande rūpa-sanātanau raghu-yugau śrī-jīva-gopālakau*. In Vṛndāvana, they lived, and they set the examples. And if you follow their footsteps, then your life will be successful. … You just try to follow the real *gosvāmīs* and make your life as *gosvāmī*. Then people will adore you” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, March 17, 1974, Vṛndāvana).

You demanded a uniquely high standard of behavior for those who serve here, and we must accept this responsibility gravely. You said, “So Vṛndāvana . . . those who will remain here … especially to guide this temple, they must be followers of the six Gosvāmīs. … We are not interested with this bodily concept of life and … although we have got this body, we do not think that body is all in all. … No. The Gosvāmīs, they are described … the first qualification is sense control … *vāco-vegam, krodha-vegam, udara-vegam, upastha-vegam, mānasa vegam* … A *gosvāmī* means one who has control over these six urges of the senses. As soon as one is practiced to control the urges of the senses, then he becomes a *gosvāmī*. That is the first definition of *gosvāmī*. *Gosvāmī* should be very ideal. … Those who will remain in Vṛndāvana … where Kṛṣṇa actually appeared … Here, those who will manage this institution, they must be first-class *gosvāmīs*. This is my proposition. Not *gṛhamedhi*. Not *gṛhamedhi*. *Gosvāmī*” (same lecture).

You further described a *gosvāmī* as one who serves Kṛṣṇa twenty-four hours a day. “The first symptom is, *kṛṣṇotkīrtana-gāna-nartana-parau.* They were always busy—*kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. *Kṛṣṇa-kīrtana* means … Just like we perform *kīrtana* with *khol*, *karatāla*, this is also *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. And to write books, that is also *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. And to read books, that is also *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. Not that simply this *kīrtana* is *kīrtana*. If you write books about Kṛṣṇa, if you read books about Kṛṣṇa, if you talk about Kṛṣṇa, you think of Kṛṣṇa, you worship Kṛṣṇa, you cook for Kṛṣṇa, you eat for Kṛṣṇa—so that is *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*. Therefore, *gosvāmī* means twenty-four hours engaged in *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*, in this way or that way” (same lecture) .

To actually do that means we have to reduce eating, sleeping, defending, and mating practically to nil. “The mind is dictating, ‘Please eat more, please sleep more, please have sexual more, please have defense fund more. …’ Defense fund means to keep money. That is defense fund. So this is materialism. The spiritualism means, ‘No, that is no.’ *Nidrāhāra*. The senses dictating, ‘Do this, do that, do that,’ and you have to become so strong that you’ll rightly reply, ‘No, this is not.’ Then *gosvāmī*. This is *gosvāmī*. … The period we sleep, that is wasted. So we shall try to save time. They \[the six Gosvāmīs\] are our examples. They were sleeping not more than two hours, or utmost, three hours. So *nidrāhāra-vihārakādi-vijitau*. This is *gosvāmī*. They conquered over these things. What is that? *Nidrāhāra*, *nidra*, *āhāra*, *vihāra*. *Vihāra* means sense enjoyment, and *āhāra* means eating or collecting. Generally, eating. And *nidra—nidrāhāra-vihārakādi-vijitau*. Conquered. That is Vaiṣṇava. Not that out of twenty-four hours, thirty-six hours sleeping” (same lecture) .

You also warned, “We be very, very careful. *Dhāma*, *dhāma-aparādha*. … In other places if you chant Hare Kṛṣṇa … the result of such chanting will be increased thousand times if you chant in Vṛndāvana. Similarly in other place, the sinful activities … if you commit that sinful activity in Vṛndāvana, then it will be thousand times increased. So we have to be very careful.” (same lecture)

You told us to do as you are doing, and we see that you also are a perfect *gosvāmī*. And though we get inspiration from your example, we wonder if we can ever reach that level. How to break the shackles of desire for sense gratification and be satisfied only by serving Kṛṣṇa? You answer, “How? *Premāmṛtāmbho-nidhi*. Because they were merged into the ocean of *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Unless we have got *kṛṣṇa-prema,* love for Kṛṣṇa, how we can remain satisfied simply in the business of Kṛṣṇa? That is not possible. Those who have not developed love for Kṛṣṇa, they cannot be engaged twenty-four hours in the business of Kṛṣṇa. We should consider that. … We should save time always to be absorbed in Kṛṣṇa business” (same lecture)

You said we must try to develop love for Kṛṣṇa—that is the actual goal. You even defined Vṛndāvana in these terms, “So we have placed our loving propensity in so many things, even to cats and dogs, but that will not satisfy us. … Real love is to love Kṛṣṇa, love of Kṛṣṇa. So this is the highest philosophy of life, highest perfection of life, how to learn to love Kṛṣṇa. The Vṛndāvana means simply loving Kṛṣṇa. The cowherds boy, the *gopīs,* the Nanda Mahārāja, Yaśodāmayī, Rādhārāṇī. The only focus is to love Kṛṣṇa. That’s all. This is Vṛndāvana. Vṛndāvana does not mean a city or town or this or that. Vṛndāvana means where everyone is in love, in intense love, with Kṛṣṇa. So you have come to Vṛndāvana. Try to learn how to love, intensified love, Kṛṣṇa. That is perfection of life. And that is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s contribution—*kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*. … You may execute your duties very nicely, but if you do not learn how to love Kṛṣṇa, this all discharging of your duties is simply working or laboring for nothing—*śrama eva hi kevalam*. … So why this love is not invoked or awakened in us? Because we are covered by this material energy, and we have become conditioned by the material energy” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, March 14, 1974).

These are but a few of your tall orders. Though we feel incapable of accomplishing them, we have confidence that the power to do so lies within striving to fulfill them. Please let us daily meditate on these orders, for they are the rope that will pull us out of the blind well of material life. We need your blessings, as well as those of your faithful followers. We don’t want to disappoint you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. On this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, please accept this attempt to re-dedicate ourselves. We beg for another chance to fulfill your wishes. Let us always carry your closing statement in our hearts: “These were the symptoms of *gosvāmī*. You study this. I want at least twenty-five men to live here, to become ideal follower of these *gosvāmīs*. They’ll see how you’ll be adored, how you’ll be respected, how you’ll be ideal. That I want. Thank you very much” (same lecture).

Your menial servants at Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir, Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Kūrma-rūpa dāsa)

## Washington, D.C.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the bona fide representative of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, therefore, you are disclosing the conclusion of the revealed scriptures—devotional service to the Supreme Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are *patita-pāvana,* the friend and benefactor of the most fallen. You are the manifest representative of Lord Nityānanda Prabhu, and, as such, you are empowered and empower others to deliver unlimited numbers of conditioned souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most beloved disciple of your Guru Mahārāja. You selflessly endured all types of obstacles for many years to establish this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement on this planet. By your personal example, you showed how a devotee should always be involved in reducing the suffering of the living entities. Although we are most insignificant, we feel very proud of the exalted grandfather we have. You have given humanity the greatest gift, Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one of your beloved sons, His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, is patiently and selflessly training us to understand the inner spirit of your mission by teaching us how to apply whatever skills and propensities we have in furthering projects that are pleasing to you. Under his guidance we are constantly implementing different strategies for preaching. The most recent expansion is our school for Martial Arts and Sciences. This school serves as a training center for men and women who are serious about developing their *kṣatriya* propensities to serve the Vaiṣṇavas. We are also preparing for some of us to move to Gītā-Nāgarī to try to fulfill your desire to develop a self sufficient *varṇāśrama* community.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, we would like to thank you for this great opportunity to serve in such a pioneering endeavor, and we beg for a drop of your unlimited mercy so that we can become more sincere in our attempts to assist our *gurudeva* in serving the Vaiṣṇava community by fulfilling your desire to establish exemplary self-sufficient ISKCON communities.

Your most fallen grandchildren at the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology (IFAST), Washington, D.C.

## Wellington

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious of days. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

The significance of this day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that today is the day many years ago that you so mercifully came to this miserable material world, which your beloved Guru Mahārāja described as a place not fit for a gentleman.

Why then did you come to this undesirable place? You came because you are truly a gentle man, a pure representative of Lord Kṛṣṇa, an ocean of mercy. You came as the friend of all. You came to help so many lost souls. And what a success your mission has been and is continuing to be! Your beloved spiritual master said that if his movement made one pure devotee he would consider it a success—you emerged from that Gauḍīya Maṭha mission, and, as only a pure Vaiṣṇava could, you acted as a touchstone, transforming matter into spirit, changing people’s hearts, and giving everyone what they were looking for. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Mahārāja said that everything is here in this world; nothing is lacking except Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And that is what you so magnanimously gave. You have shown us the reason for our existence. You have put the one in front of all the zeros. You have made our lives worthwhile; you have saved us, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We marvel at your vision: wherever you focused your consciousness you saw the Lord’s energy in various forms, and you felt an intense desire to engage that energy, whether it be matter or spirit, in the loving service of the Lord.

There is a story about how you even utilized bogus Māyāvādī pamphlets as makeshift plates for the devotees to take *prasādam* from!

In your vast plans, you were even conscious of the little ant. Once in 1968 you were sitting with a disciple and, pointing to a nonexistent ant, commented that our goal should be how to make this ant Kṛṣṇa conscious. You then bent down and spoke to the invisible ant, telling him to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and then you told the devotee that we should give him *prasādam* and then our whole movement would be a success.

What gentleness! What personalism! You saw with pure love; you saw things clearly, as they are in their eternal situation, with no concept of being master or enjoyer. Once you were asked whether you were Allen Ginsberg’s spiritual master, and you promptly answered that you were nobody’s master, but that you were everybody’s servant.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’re simply wonderful! We pray to be able to serve your desire from here on in, eternally, and to be of some assistance in the spreading of the unrivaled gift of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*.

Thank you.

Your servants at ISKCON Wellington, New Zealand.

## Zagreb

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you and your bona fide followers.

As 1996 comes close, we see that more and more people, including us, are increasingly meditating on you—hearing from you and about you, watching you on many videos, and serving your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

One journalist here from a very provocative newspaper wrote very nicely about you and put a photograph of your *mūrti* in his article, although while interviewing us he didn’t speak with us about you, nor did we tell him to use your picture. Obviously Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa inspired him to glorify His pure devotee. And on the most prominent intellectual evening radio channel in Zagreb, one lady journalist spoke about you with considerable respect and played almost the entire *Kṛṣṇa Meditation* album. It was wonderful to hear.

Our long-term plan here is to establish you among learned circles and people in general as *the* authority on Vedic knowledge, culture, and civilization, as the spiritual leader who offers the practical solutions for today’s material and spiritual problems.

Here we are very fortunate to have the expert, strict, and loving guidance of your beloved disciple His Holiness Harikeśa Swami.

On your Vyāsa-pūjā day we offer at your feet all the services we are doing here, headed by doubly-increased book distribution. Our congregation is becoming more and more enthusiastic to sponsor your books, allowing us to distribute them to many libraries, most of them poor. These libraries gladly accept them as gifts, since the demand for your books is greater than the supply. People even steal your books from libraries. We also distribute your books to VIP’s.

We are trying to develop a strong congregation, helping them do wonderful things for your pleasure, like book sponsorship and Food for Life. As His Holiness Harikeśa Swami has said, a grandfather is more merciful even than a father, since a father must be strict so that his children grow up properly, while a grandfather simply gives them love and mercy. We try to increase Prabhupāda consciousness by holding Prabhupāda Nights every Wednesday in the apartment of one of our *gṛhastha* couples; we listen to your tapes, watch videos about you, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa to melodies you chanted, read your biography (each of us reading one page), and take *prasādam*. At the end of these Prabhupāda Nights, everybody goes home with renewed enthusiasm for advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and doing something nice for the Centennial.

Your preaching *śakti* is amazing. The young generation is attracted by your books and movement. Previously they were hippies, and now they are punks. But you always reach the soul, no matter what is the outer psychological or physical covering.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so great and merciful. You alone have saved so many spirit souls. We are very thankful to you for this. Without you we would have never met our spiritual master, who is our life and soul. Actually, you have never left. You are still here with us. We are associating with you through your books, lectures, *mūrtis,* and pictures, your highly elevated disciples—our spiritual masters—and through your mission. Whoever follows your instructions can feel your presence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to us all; please give us shelter at your divine lotus feet.

Your servants at ISKCON Zagreb, Croatia.  
(written by Akrūra dāsa and Titikṣā-devī dāsī)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, Bombay

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to America with only your trust in Kṛṣṇa and faith in the words of your spiritual master. America, Māyā’s stronghold, could not shake your faith in Kṛṣṇa. Rather, you changed the faith of so many. You went to the Lower East Side of New York and distributed the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa and His *prasādam.* You had the vision and saw what these persons needed, and you never compromised. Someone told you that you would have to use a fork to eat. but you taught people to live simply and use their hands. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you didn’t hire a company to do a survey and tell you what the people thought of the *Vedas.* You understood what the suffering people required, and you never became tired of trying to make them happy. Māyā’s influence was everywhere, but you were never affected. You worked tirelessly to bring us away from Māyā’s influence. You wanted to take us with you to the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. This surrender you have for the mission of Lord Caitanya and the words of Your Guru Mahārāja is unparalleled in the history of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas. Prahlāda was thrown off a cliff by his demoniac father. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, jumped off the safe cliff of Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* into the dark abyss of Kali-yuga’s bastion. Just as Prahlāda was saved by the Supreme Lord, you were also saved, and not only were you saved but wherever you went that place became transformed into Vṛndāvana.

We pray that on this 99th anniversary of your appearance we may become *gurudevatātmā,* or one who sees his own spiritual master as his Lord and very soul. Your are so kind to have patiently tried to show us how to enjoy happiness from within. The other day I was in an office waiting for some work to get done. I sat in the waiting room, and because I foolishly hadn’t brought my *japa* beads with me, I began to look at some books lying on the table. I went through all the books on the table, and my mind was getting more and more agitated about my work not getting done. Then I thought of your instructions to chant and be happy. I began to chant and count on my fingers, and within moments I was feeling completely at home there, as if perfectly situated. Then it dawned on me that your work of starting this movement was done while you thought of Kṛṣṇa. So you were never feeling alone or out of place, because you were always in your place, that is, with Kṛṣṇa. Your efforts to preach were not coming from the mental platform, since you were acting on the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. You had no desire for followers, wealth, or prestige. However, all these things came running to be engaged by you in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. We pray that one day we can surrender to your lotus feet and thus attain pure, transcendental consciousness.

It’s not that in your preaching there were no difficulties. You faced the threefold miseries every day. Still, because you acted for Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure these miseries could not affect you. You perfectly engaged every devotee in the service of Kṛṣṇa, helping each of them to dovetail his or her desires in the service of the Lord. Your desire and the Lord’s are one and the same, so you are perfectly qualified to engage all the conditioned souls in His service. I am only one small devotee who is trying to understand your glories. Many devotees understand you better, and many devotees, now and in the future, will glorify you much better than 1, but since it is my duty, I have tried. Please forgive me for my lack of realization of your glories.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your devotees at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Bombay, India.  
(written by Bhima dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, Khasadeśa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Let us offer our respectful obeisances to you.

Your Divine Grace spread devotional service to the Western world, which is merged in impersonalism and voidism, and enlightened the people who were groping about in material darkness. You are the incarnation of the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is most compassionate to the fallen souls in this Iron Age.

Considering the miserable and helpless condition of the people of Khasadeśa, who struggle hard in the clutches of Māyā, you sent your senior disciple His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami to this desperate land to spread the rays of the benediction moon. By the mercy of Your Divine Grace and your servant, more and more people are engaging in the transcendental loving service of the Lord and are dedicating their lives to the mission of Lord Caitanya: “Let the chanting of the holy names be spread all over the world, to every town and village.”

Books are the basis. Your magnanimous transcendental books are the most powerful weapon against the illusory energy. By reading them, innumerable people can finally understand their constitutional positions as eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa. You have built a house using your transcendental books as the stones and bricks. All the conditioned souls can find shelter in this house. May every family have a set of your books, which will take them back home, back to Godhead.

Your insignificant servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Khasadeśa.  
(written by Bhakta Wang)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, North America

I first met your devotees preaching on the street, performing *kīrtana*,  
giving your books to everyone they’d meet.  
One boy said, “Come join us, and give us a hand.  
You’ll be blissful if you chant and become a Prabhupāda man.”

I heard you speak in the park at the house of flowers,  
Condemning gross materialists, impersonalists. and those allured by mystic powers.  
I was absorbed by your style, the spark was being fanned.  
It seemed like a very attractive proposition to be a Prabhupāda man.

We all went out on *saṅkīrtana*; only one man stayed at the temple.  
Your books went out profusely and gave the masses the spiritual example.  
The funds built international projects and purchased preaching vans  
To expand the numbers of fallen souls transformed into Prabhupāda men.

It was obvious the world began feeling the weight of this movement.  
They cried, “Brainwash!” You said, “Yes, the cleansing of their brain needs improvement.”  
Farms, restaurants, and other cultural projects were added to your camp.  
The temples were overflowing—everyone wanted to be a Prabhupāda man.

We thought you’d personally be here forever; your association was so sweet.  
Every year in Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana we’d all walk with you on the street.  
But Kṛṣṇa needed you elsewhere; it was obviously His plan  
To give another planet of people the chance to become a Prabhupāda man.

Before you left, you instructed your disciples to carry on the legacy.  
For ten thousand years to come, your books the law books will be.  
You said, “Just cooperate together to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in your land.  
All programs will increase if you remain a Prabhupāda man.”

Now many years later the preaching is still strong throughout most of this planet  
Especially in the countries where devotees are still book distribution fanatics.  
Of course. there is a difference of opinion; that’s how the material world stands.  
But nothing will be successful unless one’s empowered to be a Prabhupāda man.

They say we can judge a tree by how its fruit tastes sweet.  
And all the devotees that I see actually attached to your lotus feet  
Are carrying on the *sampradāya* like a mango given hand to hand.  
’Cause we all have the fortune of some day becoming a genuine Prabhupāda man!

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in North America.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, Northern Europe

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

This year, on February 26, your Puṣpa-samādhi officially opened at Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur. Besides so many individual offerings which devotees prepare for you every year, the beautiful and impressive structure in Māyāpur is the result of the collective effort of hundreds of devotees from all parts of the world. Unlike our small personal offerings, it is public, open to everyone, and indeed thousands of visitors are coming to hear about you, speak about you, and pay their respects to you.

We are impatiently waiting for that time when your appearance day will be declared a national holiday, nay, a global holiday. This is what we want. It feels so strange when, after having taken part in the celebrations on your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we come out on the streets in so many cities of the world and we realize—it is a regular day for the rest of the people. They don’t know about you; they don’t know about Kṛṣṇa. At such times we desire to tell everyone about you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and about your books.

On the second floor of your Puṣpa-samādhi in Māyāpur, right underneath the periphery of the large dome, there is a gallery of fourteen chambers with life-size dioramas depicting in sequence various stages of your life, a life filled with preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, with Deities, devotees, and books. More than half of the scenes are directly related to your books—a Delhi tea stall, where you are distributing *Back to Godhead* magazine; a printer’s shop, where you are negotiating prices with the printer; your last meeting with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, at Rādhā-kuṇḍa, where he tells you to print books whenever you have money; your arrival at New York harbor, where you hand the captain of the *Jaladuta* a copy of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* the storefront at 26 Second Avenue in New York, where you sit on a humble straw mat, speaking from the *Bhagavad-gītā* to a group of wide-eyed hippies; a display of all of your books printed in so many languages; Tompkins Square Park, where you stand under a large tree, preaching the message of the *Bhagavad-gītā;* your rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana, where you sit at a low table translating *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The last diorama shows you lying on your bed in your rooms at the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir in Vṛndāvana. It is right before your departure from this mortal world, and you are still translating *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*

Now everybody who comes to Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur can see you in this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda. But even though we very much desire that the whole world should know you, we also know that your actual desire is that the whole world should come to know Kṛṣṇa. And it is for this reason that you want us to produce all your books in all the languages of the world. It is certainly by your mercy and by Kṛṣṇa’s grace that in our endeavor to please you by spreading your books profusely, we create the basis for transcendental projects such as your Puṣpa-samādhi and a spiritual city in Māyāpur, by which you will be glorified eternally as the greatest devotee of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Northern Europe.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, Spain

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble *daṇḍavats* in the sacred dust of your lotus feet.

It is Gaura-pūrṇimā in Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur. The last rays of the sun are bathing the sparkling waters of Mother Ganges in a reddish-golden hue. The air is filled with the voices of hundreds and thousands of pilgrims who have come to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s birthplace to celebrate His appearance. It seems that every Gaura-pūrṇimā the flood of visitors to ISKCON’s World Headquarters in Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur is increasing, and this year your recently-inaugurated Puṣpa-samādhi Mandira is attracting an unprecedented number of people.

Standing on the lotus tower of the *samādhi,* I am gazing over the sprawling fields and buildings; it is amazing how Māyāpur-dhāma, your place of worship, has developed. In 1975 the only existing facility, besides the kitchen and the *prasādam* pavilion, was the Lotus Building. We all stayed in the rooms at the boundary wall, with open-air pumps as showers and big holes in the ground, covered with bamboo, as toilets. Who could have imagined how the same place would look twenty years later?

Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur is unfolding before the world like a multipetaled lotus of good fortune that is attracting beelike conditioned souls from all over the planet. Many petals have spread over the last decades, but this year an especially beautiful and fragrant petal has grown to its full size: your Puṣpa-samādhi Mandira. No doubt Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is very pleased with your followers who have endeavored so hard to manifest this architectural wonder for the glorification of Your Divine Grace, His most dear servitor.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a humble Vaiṣṇava you probably feel unworthy to have been offered such a monumental building, but we are sure that you have accepted it in the mood of a tireless preacher. Your *samādhi* has become a major attraction for people not only from Calcutta and Bengal but from all over India, and as time goes by visitors will come here from all over the world. They will want to find out

about the empowered Vaiṣṇava who inspired and is still inspiring thousands of persons to become devotees of Kṛṣṇa. And after having visited your *samādhi* and paid homage to the spiritual master of the world, they will be invited to proceed to have *darśana* of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, accept *prasādam,* receive a piece of transcendental literature, and hear the chanting of the holy name.

Just as I was heading toward the entrance to the *samādhi,* an Indian family was walking in front of me. When the mother saw the panels decorated with the *saṅkīrtana* party, she turned to her children, raised her arms, and mimicked the devotees by swaying back and forth, chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, trying to induce her children to also chant. I was reminded of the verse in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (6.2.14) stating that the holy name, even if chanted in jest, will purify. Thus, although your *samādhi* is meant for your glorification, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is simultaneously a powerful means for spreading the glories of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Over the years, the lotus of Śrī Dhāma Māyāpur will unfold more and more, until one day in the not-too-distant future its shining golden whorl will manifest in the form of the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium.

Once the spire of the *adbhuta-mandira* rises above the plains of Māyāpur, the world will not be the same anymore. This temple will be the pivot of the *yuga-dharma,* which will spread from here all over the universe. On our planet earth, the tidal wave of the *saṅkīrtana* movement will reach unexpected heights. Book distribution will multiply beyond our wildest dreams. In Māyāpur alone millions of pieces of literature will be distributed to the countless visitors who will come here every year.

And those who have received books in the past, wherever they may be in the world, will have their curiosity rekindled by the manifestation of the magnificent Temple of the Vedic Planetarium. Many will reach to their book shelves and will want to find out about the philosophy behind the spiritual movement that is fulfilling the prediction of Nityānanda Prabhu and the previous *ācāryas* in such a wonderful way.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, as workers of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, we feel especially blessed to be able to assist you in two ways: producing transcendental literature and thereby contributing to the construction of the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium. Our only desire is to receive a drop of your mercy and be allowed to continuously beat the *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga.*

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Spain.  
(written by Vedavyāsa dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Out of your unlimited compassion for the fallen souls of this age, you found numerous ways to deliver the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. One of the most important parts of your plan to bring the whole world back to Kṛṣṇa is *Back to Godhead* magazine.

You started *Back to Godhead* fifty-one years ago, on the appearance day of your Guru Mahārāja, who had asked you to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the English-speaking world. You once said that *Back to Godhead* was the beginning of your spiritual life. We know, of course, that you were sent from Kṛṣṇa and that your life was spiritual from the beginning. But by saying that the launch of *Back to Godhead* was the beginning of your spiritual life, you taught us that spiritual life really begins when the disciple seriously tries to fulfill the mission given to him by his spiritual master.

Twenty-two years after you started *Back to Godhead,* you turned it over to your disciples, the first members of your one-temple International Society for Krishna Consciousness. As you entrusted to them what you held so dear—the beginning of your spiritual life—there was no fanfare; there were no ceremonies to mark the momentous occasion. And your first disciples knew nothing of your years of struggle to print and distribute your *Back to Godhead* in India.

There was no fanfare then either. But there were surely celebrations in higher realms, where advanced and liberated souls watched as you set out to deliver the world.

Now we know of the years you worked alone, even going without eating to print your *Back to Godhead*. We know how important *Back to Godhead* is to your mission.

For the first *Back to Godhead* workers at 26 Second Avenue, putting out the magazine was practical service to help you fulfill your mission. It was the beginning of their spiritual lives.

Who can count how many spiritual lives *Back to Godhead* has launched? Through *Back to Godhead* millions of people have been introduced to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa. They have become convinced of the existence of the personality of Godhead—Kṛṣṇa—and of the place of Godhead, which they foolishly left and were still trying to ignore. Your glorious *Back to Godhead* has done what your brilliant spiritual intelligence knew it would—it has brought souls back to Godhead.

Now many of your followers who began their spiritual lives with *Back to Godhead* are serving your mission in numerous ways. A few of us have been blessed with the service of producing *Back to Godhead*. We feel unqualified to have been handed such a grave responsibility. *Back to Godhead* is a spiritual entity—who can really understand it? It is more than a magazine. Because it carries the teachings and pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, it is Kṛṣṇa Himself.

We always wonder how you would evaluate our performance in caring for your *Back to Godhead*. Is it the way you wanted it? Are we at least heading in the right direction? You wanted *Back to Godhead* to be as popular as *Time* and *Life*. Can that happen now? Can the transcendental topics of *Back to Godhead* become as popular as the sense gratification glorified in worldly magazines?

We know that anything is possible by your grace. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is more attractive than anything of this world, so why shouldn’t millions of people want to hear about it? Please strengthen our desire to improve *Back to Godhead*. We pray that with your blessings and the help of your sincere followers the pure, irresistible attraction of Kṛṣṇa will shine from the pages of *Back to Godhead* for the benefit of all.

Your servants at Back to Godhead magazine.

## Bala Books

We offer our respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of our spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Of what benefit are these eyes, if not to look upon the most merciful form of Your Divine Grace?

Of what benefit are these ears, if not to hear the nectarean instructions of Your Divine Grace?

Of what benefit is this tongue, if not to speak of the vast, inconceivable glories of Your Divine Grace?

Of what benefit is this mind, if not to concentrate on working cooperatively to serve Your Divine Grace?

Of what benefit is intelligence, if not applied to understanding the teachings of Your Divine Grace?

Of what benefit are these eyes, if not to look upon the most merciful form of Your Divine Grace?

There was a time when the word “benefit” meant an act of kindness. Your life and teachings have revealed that original meaning to us by Your Divine Grace.

Your humble servants at Bala Books.

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Throughout your writings and lectures you have always emphasized the instruction of Lord Caitanya: *yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*. “Just try to tell everyone about Kṛṣṇa.” By following this program, your ISKCON Society, with its preaching activities like Ratha-yātrā and other festivals, temple programs, restaurants, farm communities, and, most important of all, distribution of your books is the only hope for true spiritual advancement for the people of this planet. We pray that your mercy will continue so that we can all cooperate and make the ISKCON Society the vehicle for *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* that you envisioned. Any success that we are having in this endeavor we can only attribute to your potency and your desire to please Kṛṣṇa.

As the years go by, it is with increasing wonder that we meditate on the good fortune we have had to have been able to associate with you and become the recipients of your mercy. We are most unqualified in so many ways, and we are eternally indebted to you. The key to our advancement will always be sincerity and determination to carry out your orders. You have left us a wonderful opportunity, and now it is our duty to try our best to come up to your standard. This standard is possible by carrying out the simple orders of daily *sādhana* and trying in whatever way we can to preach. On many occasions you quoted Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s statement that one who preaches, he has life. Otherwise this life is simply the flapping of a dead body.

In your *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi-līlā* 7.72, purport), you write, “Every disciple must consider himself completely unaware of the science of Kṛṣṇa and must always be ready to carry out the orders of the spiritual master to become competent in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. A disciple should always remain a fool before his spiritual master.” We are so much ignoring this simple truth of spiritual life and considering ourselves to be advanced or knowledgeable. Please help us in the struggle to overcome this false egotistical quest for profit, adoration, and distinction. Please help us to take to the chanting of the holy name so that we can become purified of these *anarthas*.

Your humble servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta Center

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Simply put, we owe you everything. Selflessly you came from the most exalted place, Vṛndāvana, to a spiritually cultureless society and gave perfect instructions on every aspect of existence. First, you taught us the unheard-of notion that we’re not this body, and second that we’re actually suffering a hellish existence—not enjoying as we thought. You also said that God isn’t dead—He’s the Supreme Person, and all of us are part and parcel of Him. Crushing our false sense of independence, you explained that Kṛṣṇa’s not just our bread supplier. He’s the controller of everything.

Simply by reestablishing our awareness of our relationship with Him—by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa—we can purify our existence and at the end of this life go back to home, back to Godhead. You said that we must be hypnotized by Kṛṣṇa, that insanity for seeking Kṛṣṇa is required, and that a Kṛṣṇa-lover is another kind of lunatic. We were lunatics anyway, and it sounded wonderful.

We thought that liberation meant that we could be free from restrictions and be able to do anything we wanted, or nothing at all. But you sagely pointed out that real liberation is never inaction but instead service without human mistakes.

Because we didn’t know anything, you explained that those who are directly addicted to Kṛṣṇa (of course that’s you, Śrīla Prabhupāda) are authorities. Preaching is everything to you, and you wanted us to follow your example. To ease our trepidation, you explained that our process is *anuśṛṇuyāt,* that we hear from the superior and repeat it. You impressed on us the urgency of preaching, since the rate of recidivism in the material world is practically cent percent.

Your boldness was evident when, even as a child, you determinedly climbed to the top of the Victoria Memorial in Calcutta via the scaffolding.

Your standard of humbleness is exemplary, as when on a morning walk you were discussing scientists and you commented that when a man barks like a dog that is science, and that their aim is how to imitate dog-barking. A few moments later your disciple stated that you, Prabhupāda, should be given the Nobel Prize, and you replied, “Oh, … I am natural dog, and they’ll not give me prize. They’ll give prize to the artificial dog.” And what a glorious warrior you are, following in the footprints of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, typified by this statement of yours: “Why should we go to research? . . . Our position is very safe. We get the perfect knowledge without wasting our time. We take from Kṛṣṇa. That’s all. Finished. We know everything. Just like so many scientists, so many psychology, psychiatrist, and religionist, they come to me, but I am neither of them. I never was a scientist nor I am a psychologist nor a psychiatrist nor this or that. But I speak on the strength of *Bhagavad-gītā;* I defeat them. It is practical. So many people came to me but nobody went unharmed. Yes.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know nothing, but we are confident you know everything. Therefore our best qualification will be simply to repeat your words and instructions without deviation.

Everyone is aware that the debt to you is insurmountable. Failure to pay you back in full is guaranteed. Still, we wish to be on the installment plan, with the installments being our attempts at devotional service, and with the interest, ours, ever rising.

Asking only to remain in your lotus shadow eternally, we remain  
Your unqualified servants at the Bhaktivedanta Center, Charlottesville, Virginia.

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Bombay and San Francisco)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Just as the dew about to be dispelled by the rising sun hangs over the world unsuspectingly, so the mist of ignorance perpetuated by scientists is hanging over this planet, about to be dispelled by the rising sun of the Bhāgavata science you have so kindly presented. In modern history, no other preacher can compare to Your Divine Grace in convincingly exposing the complete intellectual bankruptcy of scientific thought. “Cheaters, not teachers,” you thundered, for they mistook the apartment for the owner.

“Prove the necessity of God to science,” you said to us. A master ordering the servant to bring some material for him also provides the servant with the money to procure it. Similarly, along with your instructions to defeat the scientists Your Divine Grace alone is providing us with the ability to carry out the task. The task is certainly subtle and the effort required Himalayan, but victory is certain if we simply persevere, knowing full well that your will is being done.

You alone guide our research and clearly reveal your plan to us—establishing the subtle science of the *Bhāgavata* as the logical extension of modern science that will replace it. You are revealing to us how the problem of quantum theory has been especially created by Lord Caitanya to facilitate the re-introduction of Vedic *mantra* technology as a positive alternative to modern Western science. “People will lose faith in science by the year 2025,” you are reported to have said. When the project underway at the BI to show that the subatomic realm is a subtle level of material reality different from gross matter reaches its successful completion by your mercy, we will see the atheistic ideas of the origin of life and the universe simply collapse like a pack of cards. Yet the scientists will hardly suspect this as they accept the initial solution your scientists at the Bhaktivedanta Institute will offer in academic journals in the coming months.

“Don’t just say ‘The *Purāṇas* contain scientific information,’” you told Dr. Sharma. “Prove it their way, according to their science.” We feel you are personally supervising the unfolding of this master plan of yours in our research. We take it as your hint of impending success that no less than six scholars from around the world have worked at the Institute in just the last eighteen months, not only studying the *Bhāgavatam* but acknowledging in writing its subtlety and superior physical concepts. Prof. Rosinger, an eminent mathematician from South Africa, wrote as follows a recent report of his:

“A remarkable and promising vision of the research on quantum theory being undertaken at the Bhaktivedanta Institute is the underlying subtle concept that the realm of subatomic matter itself is objective information. This view has the best chance to explain how the very processes of scientific thought, as well as their products—that is, the scientific theories—come to model matter at all. Such a vision is certainly the first in the last three centuries of Western scientific thinking, which, ever since Descartes, has been so much dominated by the rather sterile dualism of mind versus body. . . . I assess the value of this research direction in the fact that, for the first time, the most deep and subtle philosophical insights of Vedic Science—in particular, of Gaudiya Vaishnava Vedanta Dualism—are brought to bear upon a scientific theory, let alone one of such topmost importance as the foundations of quantum theory.”

Just the other day, Prof. Sinari from IIT-Bombay, after being given a preliminary presentation of our work for just two hours, declared, “This work is most revolutionary. It has every chance of starting a totally new trend in science.”

Until Your Divine Grace successfully showed to the world, by your dynamic preaching, how Lord Caitanya’s prediction that His name will be chanted in every town and village is a reality, many were wondering when and how the Lord’s prediction would ever come to pass. Similarly, although you have written in the Third Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that the understanding of the mind as a level of subtle objects contains “the potential basis for great scientific research,” one may wonder whether Western scientists will ever accept the knowledge of *Bhāgavatam* as superior science in the modern sense of the word. The reactions of these visiting scholars reveal to us that, once again, you alone will show this prediction to be a reality too. On this auspicious day of your appearance, we only pray that, unqualified as we are, you will by your own causeless mercy make us your instruments in this “most important preaching mission of ISKCON.”

At MIT in the USA, you asked, “Where is the department for studying the science of the soul.” By the purity of that desire only, we have been able to set up the world’s first M.S. and Ph.D. programs in Consciousness Studies in collaboration with the Birla Institute of Technology and Science, Pilani, Rajasthan, one of India’s top technical schools, originally started in collaboration with MIT! This program is ideally placed to influence research directions in the all-important and emerging field of Consciousness Studies. Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly bless us with the proper energy and intelligence to design this course properly according to your desires.

The successful beginning of our multimedia productions is also an indication that the day when this world will be deluged by the scientifically accepted Bhāgavata version of physical knowledge is not too far off. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, the task of manifesting the reality of Vedic science and a society based on this technology (*varṇāśrama-dharma*) is certainly a most daunting one. We realize practically that you are unraveling this most important project in your own time scale, according to your own larger preaching plans. Like an expert author who is unraveling a mystery plot in the most wonderful manner, you yourself are conducting this scientific preaching. We are merely following you with ever-increasing excitement. O eternal father and spiritual leader, kindly lead us on.

Your followers at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Bombay, India, and San Francisco, California.

## Bhaktivedanta Swami International Gurukula

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Lord Caitanya enacted His pastimes as a devotee and delivered the three worlds. He tasted the essence of the mellows of love of God and propagated devotional service in the world. Thus He fulfilled the purport of His designations as the relisher of transcendental mellows and as the most merciful incarnation of all. But Kali-yuga advanced quickly. His teachings had become hidden to all except a few rare souls who kept themselves aloof from the foolish masses. Lord Caitanya desired that the *saṅkīrtana* movement be spread to all corners of the earth. Thus He sent His personal associates into this world to prepare the way for this confidential work. They appeared as strict followers in the line of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, and they are recognized in this way. They first re-established the strength of Lord Caitanya’s teachings in Bhārata-varṣa, the place of His manifest *līlā*. Then, four hundred and ten years after His appearance, a special devotee appeared in this world to spread Lord Caitanya’s teachings to the whole world.

His identity was concealed for the greater part of his life, lest anyone try to interfere with the mission Lord Caitanya entrusted to him. Yet we are assured of his identity by his phenomenal achievement, which was predicted by the eternal associate of Lord Caitanya, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura: “A personality will soon appear to preach the teachings of Lord Caitanya and move unrestrictedly over the whole world with His message.”

Lord Caitanya prepared Śrīla Prabhupāda by equipping him with the skills he would need to carry out this work. When the time was right, the Lord took everything away from him just to make him cent percent dependent on His mercy and to increase his glory thereafter. Lord Caitanya invested His power in him to spread the chanting of the holy name widely. He was to enter an environment so sinful and degraded that it could not be imagined by any previous devotee. He would have to be fearless to do this work, and therefore he appropriately received the name Abhaya. Thus when the whole world was burning in the forest fire of the darkest age, a great commander of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army, empowered with all the ammunition to fight the demons of Kali-yuga, came to the world’s rescue and landed in Calcutta, the heart of the British empire.

O beloved master, Śrīla Prabhupāda! The British sought to reform India, but it was you who would reform the British and the entire world. You appeared on Nandotsava, the festival celebrating the birth of the son of Mahārāja Nanda. Therefore, your appearance in this world inaugurated a festival of worshiping Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your father was a pure devotee who prayed to all the *sādhus* that you would become a pure devotee of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. We know you to be a pure devotee of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, for you have taught us that all devotees in Vṛndāvana put themselves under the care of Kṛṣṇa’s compassionate nature, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, in order to achieve perfection in their devotional service.

You performed the Ratha-yātrā festival at only six years of age and engaged all your neighbors in the worship of Lord Jagannātha. Later you would perform Ratha-yātrā on a grand scale in major cities all over the world and engage thousands of conditioned souls in the worship of Lord Jagannātha.

By the Lord’s transcendental arrangement, you met His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura and took *dīkṣā* from him. He marked you that you “heard very well”; you “didn’t go away.” He liked your writing and said, “Whatever he writes, print it.” He ordered you to preach in the Western countries through the medium of book distribution. When he deemed the time was right, he appeared to you three times in dreams and said, “Now it is time. Take *sannyāsa*; go and preach.” You accepted *sannyāsa* and took shelter of Śrī Vṛndāvana and the Rādhā-Dāmodara Mandira. There you received the mercy of Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī and Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī and began your translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

When the stage was set, your passage to the West was granted. You were carried on a freighter across the Atlantic Ocean. You suffered two heart attacks at sea, and it seemed doubtful that you would even survive the voyage. To external appearances you were a small and elderly Indian *sādhu* dressed in traditional Indian robes, an insignificant person on a tiny boat amidst the vast ocean. Yet you were the most important person. The redemption of the world was waiting upon you. In your heart you carried *bhakti* and Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. When we think of this now, a verse comes to our minds from *Śrīmad-bhāgavata-māhātmya: idaṁ sthānaṁ parityajya videśaṁ gamyate mayā.* “Bhakti-devī informed Nārada Muni, ‘Rogues influenced by Kali-yuga broke my limbs, and for a long time I remained in that condition; thus, along with my sons, I have become very weak and impotent. Upon coming to Vṛndāvana, I regained my youth and beauty, but my two sons (Knowledge and Detachment), who are lying here, are still old and tired. I will leave this place and be carried to foreign lands.”

O spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was you who carried *bhakti* to the West. In former times the Vedic knowledge was given only to those who were serious about knowing the Absolute Truth. *Tad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet / samit-pāṇiḥ śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham*. The bona fide disciple would approach his spiritual master carrying firewood as an offering for the performance of sacrifice. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, went to the most sinful places, where people had no interest in spiritual life. You brought the firewood in your own hands, made all the preparations, performed the *yajña,* cleaned up after it was over, and distributed the nectarean *prasādam* freely to all who, by extreme mercy or good fortune, simply happened to be standing nearby. The *yajña* you performed was no ordinary *yajña* but the *hari-nāma-yajña,* saturated with all the deep emotions of your heart.

We do not yet know who you really are or what it is you have given us. We know you as you showed yourself, as a pure representative of your Guru Mahārāja and the line of *ācāryas* coming from Lord Caitanya and the six Gosvāmīs. We know you to be a pure devotee of Lord Caitanya and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. We long for the day when you may deem us fit to know you intimately. We step forward with open and eager hands to accept your gifts, yet we find our hands still paralyzed with the disease of material attachment. Thus we are unable to grasp the sweet fruits you have kept for us. We do not feel disappointed, however, because we are confident of your mercy. You have given us the perfect process to purify ourselves. We shall always endeavor to serve your mission in the ways you have given us and exactly in accordance with the mood expressed by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāṁī in this famous verse: *anyābhilāṣitā-śūnyaṁ jñāna-karmādy-anāvṛtam / ānukulyena kṛṣṇāṇuśīlanaṁ bhaktir uttamā*. We are confident that if we proceed in this way, when you deem us fit you will infuse our hearts with genuine and deep aspirations for the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Our attempt to glorify you with these words must surely fall short because your glory is unlimited and our hearts are very tiny. We long to glorify you in a manner that is appropriate and pleasing to you. We long to glorify you not only today but every day and every moment with body, mind, and words. You have given us Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma and the Bhaktivedanta Swami Gurukula. We hope to please you by becoming good students and good devotees and serving your mission. The perfection of our writing is to glorify you, and therefore we offer these few words as a garland of flowers at your divine lotus feet.

Bhaktivedanta Swami International Gurukula, Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by the older boys’ writing class)

## Food for Life

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all your *prasādam bhaktas,* please accept our humble obeisances on this auspicious occasion.

With your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would like to offer the following realization about your personality and your *prasādam* distribution mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda: The perfect host

According to the Oxford Dictionary, hospitality is “a friendly and generous reception of guests or strangers.” To be hospitable, therefore, means to care and show respect for another being. It is a sincere expression of appreciation, love, and humility. The natural symptom of a person whose heart is filled with gratitude, magnanimity, and religiosity will be his or her eagerness to be hospitable.

There is no doubt that you were the perfect host, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a graciously hospitable Indian *sādhu,* gentleman, and loving spiritual father. Your kindness, love, and concern for others will always be remembered. Indeed, it appears that through your example of hospitality and love you came to teach the world more than just *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*. Your mission was a practical one and therefore twofold: In your hands you carried the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* encapsulating the essence of all transcendental knowledge, and in your person you embodied the very meaning of spiritual culture.

Rūpa Gosvāmī, in the fourth verse of his scientific treatise *Upadeśāmṛta* (*The Nectar of Instruction*), gives six essential activities for the development of loving relationships. One of those activities, *bhojayate,* is to distribute *prasādam:*

*dadāti pratigṛhnāti*  
*guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati*  
*bhuṅkte bhojayate caiva*  
*ṣaḍ-vidhaṁ prīti-lakṣaṇam*

**“Offering gifts in charity, accepting charitable gifts, revealing one’s mind in confidence, inquiring confidentially, accepting** ***prasādam,*** **and offering** ***prasādam*** **are the six symptoms of love shared by one devotee and another.”**

To distribute *prasādam* to another conditioned soul is to communicate: “I love you. I care about you. I want to see you happy because the Lord is our father.” Indeed, it is for this reason that *prasādam* distribution was always an essential feature of India’s ancient Vedic culture of hospitality.

The householders of Vedic times were extremely selfless and made great efforts to see that no one within their village or community went without sufficient food. The *gṛhasthas* should be “great ‘communists,’” you said, Śrīla Prabhupāda, providing the basic necessities for all living beings: “According to the instructions of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* every *gṛhastha* is a great ‘communist’ who provides the means of living for everyone. Whatever a *gṛhastha* may possess he should equally distribute to all living entities, without discrimination. The best process is to distribute *prasādam*” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Canto Seven, Chapter Fourteen) .

One of the essential principles of Vaiṣṇava etiquette and the Vedic culture of hospitality is the principle of *atithi-nārāyaṇa,* respecting the uninvited or unscheduled guest as if he were God. This principle evolved from the concept of the extended family concept and a deep spiritual vision, wherein every man, women, and child, regardless of caste, color, or creed, as well as every species of life was seen as an important member of the universal family of Kṛṣṇa.

We may then rightly ask, What has happened to such ideal hospitality and the spiritual understanding that precedes it? Why do nations fight over soil and rock that they can never permanently possess? Why do some people hate each other so thoroughly that they commit such stomach-wrenching atrocities as those we have heard have taken place in Sarajevo? Is there no end to the depths of cruelty and passion infecting a Serbian man’s heart as he happily butchers the limbs of a wounded but still living soldier because he is born in Bosnia? What drives a Bosnian sniper to nonchalantly pop a bullet through a seven-year-old Serbian schoolboy’s head just for practice?

You summed it up during an interview at London’s Heathrow Airport: “We have come to teach people what they have forgotten—God.”

For most people, however, the very concept of God is abstract—that is, if they have any concept at all. To talk of God as the Supreme Person, what to speak of Kṛṣṇa, a transcendental cowherd boy from Goloka, is absurd for many. Most people exist on the *anna-maya* platform. Hence we have the popular saying “The way to a man’s heart is through his stomach.” It is interesting and appropriate, therefore, that the scriptures state, “Spiritual life begins with the tongue,” and that you predicted, “ISKCON would be known as ‘the kitchen religion.’”

Just as the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is based on two principles—to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa as much as possible, both inside and outside the temples, and as far as possible to distribute *prasādam* (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.12.10 purport)—similarly we feel that Hare Krishna Food for Life, ISKCON’s kitchen department, is also based on two principles:

(1) To distribute massive amounts of *prasādam* indiscriminately, so that all beings can experience the higher taste of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam;*  
(2) To revive the Vedic culture of hospitality through our example of love and respect for all beings, regardless of caste, color, or creed.

Of course, our internal mission is to glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for it is you who embody Vedic hospitality, unconditional love, and respect for all beings. And it is you who deserve all the credit for the hundreds of millions of portions of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* given away to conditioned souls at our Sunday feasts, restaurants, festivals, and Food for Life programs. Where would we all be if not for your kindness in inviting us into Kṛṣṇa’s house? You never considered our dirty appearance, bad qualities, or vulgar habits. Rather, you saw in us a spark of Kṛṣṇa’s splendor. We were lost and frightened children, without a purpose in life. You took us by the hand, sat us down, offered us Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam* and a glass of water to quench our dry hearts. You then spoke kindly and lovingly to our souls, instilling in us a sense of purpose and value that we had never experienced before. You gave us hope and showed us a way to be happy, healthy, and successful in life. Oh, how fortunate we all were to have stumbled upon you doorstep!

We all feel greatly indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you.

Praying that we can please you by following your example,  
Your undeserving guests at Food for Life Global, Potomac, Maryland.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Lord Kṛṣṇa has provided the seeds, the rain, the nutrients in the soil, the strength of the oxen, the warmth of the sun, and the light of the moon by which the vegetables become juicy. We cannot say that we are farmers growing so many wonderful things. However, we can reflect on the fact that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the one who is growing the crops and that we are simply the servant of the servant, a hundred times the servant of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the most exalted of farmers, for you have planted within our hearts the most valuable seed, the seed of devotional service. You have given us instructions on how to cultivate this most precious of seedlings, which bears the fruit of love of God, so that whatever we are doing is filled with the most important ingredient of the offering, love and devotion.

Continuously we are reminded that we, the farmers, are not the doers. As we look about us we see that the rice plants are giving the fruits of their labor—kernels of rice—to be added to the milk from the cows, who have eaten the grasses and herbs in the field, to produce substances for sweet rice and other delectable sweets.

Even the cows’ dung can be used as fuel (dry paddies or methane gas) to cook these wonderful preparations to offer to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your dearmost friend, Lord Kṛṣṇa.

But where does the love and devotion start in an offering? In the kitchen with the cook? On the altar with the *pūjārī*? Or does it start with the preparation of the fields by the oxen and teamsters?

“The bull is the emblem of the moral principle, and the cow is the representative of the earth. When the bull and the cow are in a joyful mood, it is to be understood that the people of the world are also in a joyful mood. The reason is that the bull helps the production of grains in the agricultural field, and the cow delivers milk, the miracle of aggregate food values. The human society maintains these two important animals very carefully so that they can wander everywhere in cheerfulness.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.16.18 purport)

In developing our rural communities, it is of the greatest importance to show the usefulness of the bull. This can be done when the bull and the devotee teamster work side by side to serve and please Guru and Kṛṣṇa and the community of Vaiṣṇavas. You have told us that until we show the usefulness of the bull, society will never stop eating him.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us your blessings so that we can show your rural communities as embodying lifestyles of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* from seed to sustenance, and as evidence of your pure devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Our good fortune has been created by you; without you we are useless.

Your humble servants at the International Society for Cow Protection (ISCOWP).

## The ISKCON Foundation

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Prabhupāda’s Feet**

Prabhupāda’s feet, a shelter complete,  
Elusive and always in motion;  
Blessing the earth, destroying rebirth,  
For all who touch them in devotion.

Prabhupāda’s hands, of graceful commands,  
Dance the harmonium keys;  
The consummate tools, dismantling fools,  
And blessing devotees with ease.

Prabhupāda’s face, of Vaiṣṇava grace,  
Mirrors contentment within;  
Noble and bold with senses controlled,  
A single glimpse vanquishes sin.

Prabhupāda’s eyes, observing and wise,  
Peer through a scriptural lens;  
Seeing the good, like fire in wood,  
In the sightless and lowest of men..

Prabhupāda’s voice, the demigods’ choice,  
Resonates Vaiṣṇava prayers;  
Abducting your ear, it takes you quite clear  
Of the seven material layers.

Prabhupāda’s mind, Vṛndāvana inclined,  
Thinking of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa;  
Like *ācāryas* past, envisioning the past,  
Unfolding Caitanya’s mission.

Prabhupāda’s heart, conferring the art  
Of *bhakti,* so kindly, to others;  
Ferocious with liars, like a Bengali tiger’s,  
As soft as a Bengali mother’s.

Prabhupāda’s slave, an unruly knave,  
Woefully prays with regret;  
“Your mercy alone is my passport back home.  
Prabhupāda! Don’t give up on me yet!”

Your servants at the ISKCON Foundation.

## The ISKCON School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Because we work under your orders, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa has sent so many exalted Vaiṣṇavas for us to train as preachers. Without you, our children would simply be yet another source of material anxiety, frustration, and disappointment.

Because we work under your orders, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know how to love our children on the spiritual platform. Without you, our so-called love would simply be an expansion of our lust for expanded self-gratification.

Because we work under your orders, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know how to train our children so they will not only become first-class citizens, but lovers of Kṛṣṇa. Without you, our children would, at best, be absorbed in a passionate struggle for family, money, and prestige.

We pray that we have the spiritual strength to continue always under the shelter of your orders. Please do not kick us away.

Your servants at the ISKCON School in Hillsborough, North Carolina.  
(written by Urmilā-devī dāsī)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. By your mercy only have I been able to have the glorious association of devotees and thus be rescued from the loneliness of the material world. You bestowed your mercy upon me, even though I didn’t have the sense to know that I needed it.

There was once a young boy named Dave,  
Who was born in the land of the free and the brave.  
He thought he was cool,  
Though he was a proud fool,  
Who didn’t have a clue how to be saved.

Unfortunately, I haven’t changed a great deal since you found me twenty-four years ago. I still hanker for sense gratification, and my mind still drags me into nescience. I welcome old age and disease as my liberators from my material desires rather than opportunities for preaching activities. I have a long way to go toward being enthusiastic in devotional service.

Initiation changed my name to Nṛshriṁ,  
Which vibrates with a transcendental ring,  
But I have used my precious time  
To compose silly riddles and rhymes,  
Not to chant, and to dance, and to sing.

You instructed me to make films about Kṛṣṇa, yet I couldn’t even begin to follow your advice until eight years later. You shared your concern that your Godbrothers were not understanding how to preach the cult of Lord Caitanya, and you expressed your desire for your disciples to tax their brains for your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The *karmīs* complain when they are taxed 30%, and they revolt when they are taxed 50%, but you requested 100% tax!

Śrīla Prabhupāda asked his disciples to preach,  
Even though we barely know what to teach.  
I wouldn’t have stayed  
If the *prasādam* hadn’t been made,  
But I got hooked and finally realized Who I wanted to reach.
