Gradually, I saw that I resisted material activities and relished certain parts of devotional service. Much to my surprise, I found that I am very conditional about what *seva* I do for you. Only by your transcendental vision did you already know how to engage me—in films and television. You gave me the gift of ITV—ISKCON Television.

Watching devotees dancing on a screen,  
I saw what very few humans have seen.  
Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement  
Encircles the world from Māyāpur to Guadeloupe.  
Now the entire planet can see just what you mean!

Over the years of developing ITV, I also watered the roots of my material attachments. Great weeds grew up around my tiny *bhakti-latā,* so, again, you have reached into this material *tattva* from your spiritual position and offered me the chance for purification. You are no ordinary soul, Śrīla Prabhupāda; other great souls take shelter at your feet. I am most fortunate even to have heard your name, what to speak of being blessed by your *vapu* and *vāṇī*. How can I ever repay you?

There are moments of doubt in times like this,  
When I feel the long absence of spiritual bliss.  
I’m very unsure,  
Not fit to endure,  
But the advice in your books always fits.

In striving to participate in the celebration of the one hundredth year since your divine appearance on this meager earth, I have realized how difficult is the job of staying focused on serving your lotus feet. I have such big plans, but they can never be achieved without the blessings of the *paramparā*. So I pray to the entire succession, leading to Kṛṣṇa Himself, to please fulfill my desires to preserve all of your images on stable media and to allow me to somehow create a beautiful film of your miracle-making activities. Please grant me the boon of glorifying you through the only medium I know so I can share you with others who do not yet know.

I hope I can render a service  
That makes me a little bit nervous.  
The job is so big  
For a guy who acts like a pig  
That I can only dream of fulfilling your purpose.

I will do my best to make my dream come true. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are my savior. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious appearance day. Please keep me in the fire of devotional service always.

We beg to remain your fallen servants at ITV.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## KHQN

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way by which we can attain pure devotional service. Pure devotional service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa cannot be attained through mental speculation, nor by charitable works, nor penances and austerities. No amount of money can purchase pure devotional service. It comes only by the mercy of the pure devotee when he is pleased with the service of his disciple.

When one has attained the mercy of the spiritual master, everything else is attained. Money will come. Austerities and penances assume pleasing characteristics. One performs the greatest charitable work—delivering fallen souls from repeated birth and death. Mental speculation recedes before the *Bhagavad-gītā* like a firefly from the sun.

No happiness within the three worlds compares with the happiness of pleasing one’s spiritual master. Through every fiber of his being, and at every waking and sleeping moment, the disciple feels *ānanda,* bliss. Though the bliss is total, he wants more. He’s like a baby who is thrown by his father high into the air and who, when caught on the way down, delightedly yells, “More, more!”

Our regret is that we can only take one risk at a time, open, maintain, and expand new centers only one by one. Our desire is to expand our forms and go everywhere simultaneously to preach at every crossroads and in every village, town, and city. Our desire is to prepare *prasādam* in every home, institution, and restaurant, for every assembly, large or small, till Agni himself be exhausted with all the cooking. Our desire is to personally sing the *mahā-mantra* simultaneously on *pada-yātrās* on every continent, on every planet in the universe, to personally pour the nectar of the holy name from the pitchers of our mouths into the ears of each and every living entity, that they may waken from their long sleep and shout the names of Hari.

Having had a little taste of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy, we, his disciples, hanker to break the bonds of time and space. We want to do it all. One body is not enough. We want millions of mouths to preach and sing, millions of hands to serve *prasādam,* construct and clean temples, play musical instruments, and write books glorifying devotees and devotional service.

If there were millions of gold pieces lying on the ground, two hands would be inadequate to pick them all up. There are uncountable souls just hankering for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have to simply be the instruments.

Thy ransomed servant, I  
Restore to Thee Thy own,  
And from this moment live or die  
To serve my God alone.

Unconditional service to the lotus feet of the *guru* is the attachment that fulfills all desires, overcomes all limits of time and space. Śrīla Prabhupāda accepted his many disciples as having been sent directly by his *guru*, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Śrīla Prabhupāda unconditionally served his *guru,* did more than his *guru* for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He said in Australia, “By the mercy of my *guru,* I have done three times more than him. Now each one of you do three times more than me.” One can do more than his *guru* only by unconditional surrender to his *guru*. When we become perfect instruments of our *guru*, then the *guru* can expand the service of his disciple unlimitedly by sending him disciples.

Once in Māyāpur Śrīla Prabhupāda sat on the *vyāsāsana,* surveyed his hundreds of young disciples, remarked that each had fifty years of preaching ahead of him, thought it due to the remarkable mercy of his spiritual master, and openly began to cry in ecstasy.

Let us pray to follow in the footsteps of our lord, master, and friend, taking the shade of his lotus feet with the measure of devotion to him that he had for his *guru,* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

Let us pray that we will be blessed by him as he was by his *guru,* and thus neither heaven above nor hell below will be able to limit the spread of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s devotees in Utah.

## Krishnafest

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Krishnafest has a preaching center in Austin, the world headquarters of a movement started by an elderly resident of Varśana who is preaching his own brand of *rāgānuga-bhakti*. He doesn’t enforce rules and regulations and surrounds himself with a group of female disciples, all “*sannyāsinīs*.”

We often hear of this “Vaiṣṇava’s” own interpretation of *śāstra* and of his questionable activities. Yet this is simply serving to increase our appreciation of your greatness and purity, confirming that the most fortunate people in the world take shelter of you. At your feet, they will never be cheated.

This brings to mind the following letter, a letter that shows how careful you were to preserve the purity of the disciplic succession so that we could receive Kṛṣṇa consciousness as it is, fully potent and fully effective.

“Your appreciation of my spiritual master is very commendable. One who understands and appreciates the disciplic succession is certainly advanced, and we should always be very careful to give full respect to those who have so carefully handled this divine fruit of transcendental knowledge before us. Even a slight change will spoil it. That is why I have always been so careful to give you only those things which I have heard from my Guru Maharaj.”

Thank you for being so careful. This is your success formula. You said that you had no qualification but that your success was due to delivering the message of the disciplic succession without change. Externally that appears to be a simple thing, but it is not. Deviation, speculation, and concoction are enemies that continuously attempt to hinder the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The *guru’s* duty is to preserve the truth. As it is said, *rupānuga-viruddhāpasiddhānta-dhvānta-hārine:* “\[Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī\] did not tolerate any statement which was against the teachings of devotional service enunciated by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī.” In the same way, you were always exceedingly vigilant to carefully protect your disciples from philosophical deviations. And you are still protecting us today through your books and through the guidance of your surrendered servants.

You carefully preserved Kṛṣṇa consciousness and handed it over to us, asking that we “don’t spoil it.” We are not yet free from the propensity to cheat, and because we are conditioned souls we make mistakes, are illusioned, and have imperfect senses. Thus the possibility of “spoiling it” threatens all of us. But hearing of this so-called *guru’s* questionable activities and manufactured teachings has reminded us of the undesirable consequences of deviation. This is causing us to feel even more safe and protected at the shelter of your feet.

Appreciating what you have given, we pray that we preserve Kṛṣṇa consciousness exactly as you delivered it.

Your servants at Krishnafest.  
(written by Mahātmā dāsa)

## New Tālavana Community School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we are grateful that somehow or other this project has continued for another year. I remember in a lecture Your Divine Grace said, “Somehow or other, you must stay; somehow or other, you must continue to serve Kṛṣṇa.” So, somehow or other we are still here. Somehow or other this school has been going on at New Tālavana since 1978. It has had its ups and downs. At times four students, a other times forty, sometimes teacher, at other times ten. But somehow or other, by your mercy, we have managed to keep it going.

Giving children an opportunity for a Kṛṣṇa conscious education is very important. We aren’t trying to just raise kids; we want to raise Vaiṣṇavas. And somehow or other you are engaging us in the auspicious service of teaching children about Kṛṣṇa.

I know there is always room for improvement. It is easy to get caught up in just teaching the subject and forget to Kṛṣṇa-ize it. But somehow or other, by your example, we can infuse Kṛṣṇa consciousness into these normally mundane subjects.

And somehow or other, by your mercy, we managed to get a new school building this year. Now we humbly pray at your lotus feet to somehow or other keep the little Vaiṣṇavas in our new school always engaged in your service.

Your disciples, granddisciples, and aspiring granddisciples at the New Tālavana Community School, Mississippi.

## North American Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial

Helping others plan your Centennial is my main offering.  
For this glorious occasion we are anxiously preparing.

No time for relaxing and frivolous acts;  
How to include all, our brains we must tax.

Coming from a twelve-petal lotus flower,  
The Centennial celebration, your mercy will shower.

**The Petals**

*Increasing Prabhupāda Consciousness*  
No ordinary celebration could ever be sufficient;  
Engage all friends, family, congregation, and innocent.

*Distributing Books*  
Your wonderful mercy and gifts must be shared;  
Without help from your books, none Māyā will spare.

*Spiritual Standards*  
Without spiritual standards in this age of sin,  
We remain low and fallen, Vaikuṇṭha unfit to win.

*Project Unity*  
Your love for your followers still there to refresh  
The devotees who are suffering, loosely connected at best.

*Spreading the Holy Name*  
Let’s all glorify your exemplary deeds and devotion;  
Your gift of *hari-nāma* spread as the universal potion.

*Strengthening Management*  
With your ISKCON managers spiritually strong and expert,  
The world becomes Kṛṣṇa conscious in eighteen days, we’ve heard.

*Distributing Prasādam*  
*Prasādam* you gave us to capture the masses;  
The celebrations and events include it for all classes.

*Establishing Memorials*  
Wherever Memorials are made to record in granite,  
Generations will remember your stay on this planet.

*Building Congregations*  
The congregation has grown and must now be encouraged  
So they will become strong and ISKCON will flourish.

*Enhancing ISKCON’s Reputation*  
ISKCON’s your body and we’ll expand its reputation;  
Let every town and village know your glorious celebration.

*Celebrating 1996 Events*  
Feed the World day, World Holy Name day, and all global celebrations  
Awaken the whole world to your glories on these special occasions.

*Offering Special Achievements*  
If anyone is left out and has a special project,  
We encourage them to do it nicely and never object.

I have yet one simple wish to fulfill:  
At your lotus feet I may remain until  
We meet again in the spiritual world face to face,  
When I can personally thank you for saving me, Your Divine Grace.

Your eternally grateful servants at the offices of the North American Centennial.  
(written by Ramīya dāsa)

## North American Women’s Ministry

My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

I remember so vividly when you departed (physically) and returned to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Time stood still. And even though my material body continues to age and my mind continues to waver, time is still standing still in the core of my heart. The pain hasn’t gone away.

Why have you been so kind to allow me such nice service, and to allow me the association of so many wonderful devotees? I reflect on my good fortune and think that you are engaging someone like me, so incompetent and alone in this world, as a reminder to others how precious every spirit soul is. And how wonderful Lord Kṛṣṇa is to all living beings.

You have helped me give up my envy of others. I am so thankful, I just wish I could stay nestled in obeisances at your lotus feet. And I know you will guide me, and teach me to give up my fear … fear of dying.

In the mean time, there is so much to be done, and I am forever grateful for every moment of your precious devotional service. Please give me the strength to complete my tasks: to chant, read, pray, and associate with others daily, as per your wishes; to help strengthen and enliven your ISKCON temple communities through events, fund-raising, Sunday school and congregational involvement; to help your transcendental family grow and prosper through increased understanding and sharing of our spiritual insights, as well as caring for one another; and to help provide facility and encouragement for our movement’s gentler gender and congregational supporters, especially those who have felt the pangs of neglect and /or exploitation (using someone’s assets without caring for the soul within.)

My debt to you is eternal, and I may have to suffer many lives before I come to appreciate all that you offer. Do not give up on me, please. Though I am fallen, my life and being have no purpose but to please and satisfy my spiritual father.

Thank you for all you have done.

Sincerely, your devotees at the North American Women’s Ministry  
(written by Sudharmā dāsī)

## Padayātrā America

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories unto you! These glories follow you automatically. Kṛṣṇa is competing with you—insisting on arranging for your glorification despite your efforts to avoid it—and thus fame and reputation, although unsought, find you anyway. Mādhavendra Purī’s glories found him at the marketplace: despite his attempts to elude them, they continued to appear in front of him. What to do? Kṛṣṇa wants to see you get the credit for spreading His glories worldwide, and we have decided to participate fully. Since you are the representative of Madana-mohana-mohinī, you are attractive to all living entities, beginning with the Lord Himself. We will take you on *padayātrā,* accompanied by Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara. *Darśana* is incomplete without you. Rūpa Gosvāmī quotes, “The spiritual master is not only to be seen as My representative, but as My very self.” What could be sweeter? No one can argue—these are Kṛṣṇa’s words, as are the following: “Never will there be one more dear to Me than he who is spreading My glories.” You quote Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura: “The order of the spiritual master is the life and soul of the disciple,” and relate how this moved you very much to carry out the order of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura to take Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West. And, by Your Divine Grace only, this same injunction now moves us to take Kṛṣṇa consciousness far and wide via *padayātrā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is all that we are made of.  
Your servants at Padayātrā America.

## Padayātrā Europe

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

To the normal eye, you came and you left, but for us—your disciples and granddisciples—you appeared and are still with us. You changed our lives, reforming our bad habits and changing us lost souls into *bhaktas* of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You gave us meaning and a goal in life.

With your Centennial coming very soon, we are gaining an awareness of your blessings upon us. And we are trying to hand down these blessings to the rest of the world by distributing books, books, books, as you taught us repeatedly, day after day.

On Padayātrā Europe we are in a state of ecstasy, distributing books and *prasādam;* this is the only way we maintain our program on a daily basis. We depend on your mercy, and we are so proud to have you with us, sitting in the front of our chariot as we go from town to town, village to village, accompanied by Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara. We love to see people look at you, and we love to tell them about you. This year we have acquired a projector, and nightly we’ll be showing slides about your beloved ISKCON and its activities, and mostly about you, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

All along the way, people love us: their faces light up at the sight of the Padayātrā scene, and they want to know more. This is your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Only two more summers and we’ll be in Moscow. It has gone so fast on the road from Belfast to Moscow, and now we are getting closer to our goal. Last year, as we were traveling through some eastern European countries, we met lots of new devotees and saw how people are taking books, wanting them so badly. What mercy to experience that mercy. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We pray to always remember you and never deviate from your teachings.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Padayātrā!

Your servants from Padayātrā Europe.

## Padayātrā India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*maj-janmanaḥ phalam idam madhu-kaiṭabhāre*  
*mat-prārthanīya-mad-anugraha eṣa eva*  
*tvad-bhṛtya-bhṛtya-paricāraka-bhṛtya-bhṛtya-*  
*bhṛtyasya bhṛtya iti māṁ smara loka-nātha*

**“O enemy of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, O Lord of the universe, the perfection of my life and the most cherished mercy You could show me would be for You to consider me the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant of your servant” (*****Mukunda-mālā-stotra*** **25).**

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

When will be begin to become servants of the servants of the servants of yourself? Despite the long, austere, and difficult years on the road, pushing on and on and on your Saṅkīrtana Padayātrā, we still desire only to serve ourselves! Here King Kulaśekhara prays to the Lord, requesting his prayer to be granted; this would be the greatest sign of the Lord’s favor and mercy, because serving the servants of His servants many times removed pleases the Lord the most. As the Lord states in the *Ādi Purāṇa:*

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktās ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhaktatamāḥ matāḥ*

**“My dear Pārtha, those who say they are My devotees are not My devotees, but those who claim to be devotees of My devotees are actually My devotees.”**

Somehow or another, or by some transcendental trick, we have remained behind you and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara for the past eleven years, covering 45,000 kms.—walking, chanting, distributing *prasādam* and your transcendental literature, preaching your ISKCON Society’s glories, and trying to spread the holy names of the Lord, as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu desired. But it is only because of your divine presence, inspiration, and guidance—because of your being such a pure-hearted humble servant of your Guru Mahārāja—that we are still trying to maintain such a grand preaching program. Otherwise, visiting all these holy places and temples and bathing in all the holy rivers and *kuṇḍas* is simply a waste of time!

You have mentioned that originally you didn’t want to come to this material world, but you came because Lord Kṛṣṇa asked you to come and write books for the benefit of everyone. We *padayātrīs* would like to believe that you also came here to begin Padayātrā, especially since you always desired to preach in the Indian villages but we’re always too busy with your other worldwide preaching activities. Recently, when we were at your ISKCON center in Surat, one devotee commented, “How young Śrīla Prabhupāda looks on Padayātrā!” This proves that preaching on Padayātrā makes one youthful at heart!

We pray to you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you give us a simple boon, but a most important one—that we can become servants of the servants of Your Divine Grace, and continue walking with you to every town and village, just to spread your unlimited glories to everyone, not only during your Centennial, but forever. We pray for that continuous shelter under the shade of your “walking” lotus feet. Please consider our plea.

Your walking servants on Padayātrā India.

## Prabhupāda Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

It is 1975, Calcutta, and I enter your room. We are apparently alone. You are leaning back on a pillow behind your desk, your forearm resting comfortably on your upright knee, and your hand is gesturing. Your lotus face is full of expression.

You stop your current engagement (it is all so infinitely beyond me) to turn to me, and after a brief talk you mercifully tell me that I should be a spiritual physician. You pick up the *Kṛṣṇa* book and tell me that I should simply distribute your books, work with your books.

Illusion and confusion can no more exist in your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, than morning dew in the noonday sun. You are missed by all of us so very, very much. How can it possibly be expressed? We were dead and you gave us back our very lives.

Thank you for keeping me engaged in seeing to the details of the printing of your books over these years. You taught us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that all the fine points are precious: Kṛṣṇa is in the details.

You are the heaviest of all the heavy *gurus*. You are Prabhupāda, the spiritual master at whose lotus feet all others reside and from whom all received the breath of spiritual rebirth.

Please keep me engaged in the details, Śrīla Prabhupāda, life after life.

Your unworthy disciples at the Prabhupāda Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

Time and again you have proven to me your foresight and perception. Sometimes you would speak of how fallen society is, and I would offensively think, “That’s not really so bad.” Later on I would see how society is very fallen, and I would say with conviction, “Now I can see why you said that.” Sometimes you would speak of far-reaching plans for preaching and saving the world, and I would offensively think, “Does he really think these things can happen?” Many of these plans are unfolding right now, and I can say with conviction, “Now I see why you said that.” Sometimes you would speak of dangerous threats to our spiritual advancement, and I would offensively think, “Can those activities really be that bad?” Later your statements would prove to be true, and I would say with conviction, “Now can I see why you said that.” Sometimes you would speak of dangerous threats to your society, ISKCON, and I would think, “They don’t seem so dangerous to me.” Later on your statements would prove to be true, and I would say with conviction, “Now can I see why you said that.” Sometimes you would speak on the power of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, how it can completely purify one’s heart of sinful desires and enable a sincere devotee to see Kṛṣṇa face to face, and I would think, “Surely it must work. But I’m not sincere; it won’t work for me.” Now I am confident that, just as so many other doubts have been dispelled by you, my attachment to matter will gradually be replaced by love for Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is yours, you have the power to give Him to me. By your mercy, someday I will be face to face with Kṛṣṇa with a completely pure heart. Then I will say with conviction, “Now I can understand why you said that.”

Your servants at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina.  
(written by Mitrasena dāsa.)

## Siam Vedic Society

**A Poem to Śrīla Prabhupāda**

This poem is sung in *totak chandaḥ*

*(1) sva-guror-upadeśa mahārgha-maṇi śirasā vidhṛto hari-nāma-dhani*  
*(2) viharāmi janeṣv-amṛtaṁ vitaran bhava-pārakariṁ taraṇiṁ kathayan*  
*(3) vṛṣabhānu-sutānvita kṛṣṇa-manum prajapan sa-phalaṁ janayen nu januḥ*  
*(4) madhu-gopa-vane kṛpayā hi guroḥ phala-sañcaya-kṛd bhajan-ākhya taroḥ*  
*(5) gurudeva varo mama bhakti-yutaḥ nigamānn avido hi giraḥ sa sutaḥ*  
*(6) kali-dharma iyaṁ hari-nāma-ratiṁ parikīrtanataḥ saphalā bhavati*  
*(7) iti tad-bhajanād gatavān pramadaṁ praṇamāmi sadā prabhupāda-padam*

(1) *sva-guroḥ*—own *guru; upadeśa*—preaching; *mahā-argha*—most valuable; *maṇi*—jewel; *śirasā*—by head; *vidhṛtaḥ*—carried; *hari*—Lord Hari; *nāma*—divine name; *dhani*—rich with.

**I am enriched by the advice of my spiritual master, which is the holy name of Hari, a most precious jewel that I carry on my head. (1)**

(2) *viharāmi*—moving around; *janeṣu*—in people; *amṛtam*—nectar; *vitaran*—distributing; *bhava*—the world; *pārakarim*—cross over; *taraṇim*—boat; *kathayan*—teaching.

**Now I will move around and distribute to people this nectar (of** ***hari-nāma*****), which is a boat upon which one can cross over the worldly ocean of materialistic life. (2)**

(3) *vṛṣabhānu*—father of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī; *sutā*—daughter; *anvita*—joined; *kṛṣṇam*—Lord Kṛṣṇa; *anum*—after; *prajapan*—chanting; *sa-phalam*—success; *janayet*—carrying; *nu*—sure; *januḥ*—life.

**Chanting the names of Rādhā (the daughter of Vṛṣabhānu) and of Kṛṣṇa will surely carry our lives to success. (3)**

(4) *madhu*—honey; *gopa*—cowherds; *vane*—(Vṛndāvana) forest; *kṛpayā*—by grace; *hi*—sure; *guroḥ*—from the spiritual master; *phala*—fruit (result); *sañcaya-kṛd*—collector or gatherer; *bhajan*—devotional service; *ākhya*—known by; *taroḥ*—(of the) tree.

**By my** ***gurudeva’s*** **grace I can surely collect the fruits (of** ***bhakti*****) from the Vṛndāvana worship tree. (4)**

(5) *gurudeva*—the spiritual master; *varaḥ*—honorable; *mama*—my; *bhakti-yutaḥ*—full with devotional service; *nigamāt*—from the *Vedas; avidohi*—milking (like a cow); *giraḥ*—speech; *sa*—that; *sutaḥ*—Sūta Gosvāmī.

**My most honorable spiritual master (Śrīla Prabhupāda) is filled with** ***bhakti,*** **and by his speech he has milked the cowlike** ***Vedas*** **of Sūta. (5)**

(6) *kali-dharma*—the religion of the Kali Age; *iyam*—this; *hari*—Lord Hari; *nāma*—name; *rati*—affection; *parikīrtanataḥ*—congregational chanting; *sa-phala*—success; *bhavati*—to be.

**The religion of the Kali Age consists of having affection for congregational chanting of the names of Lord Hari, which will bring all success. (6)**

(7) *iti*—in this way; *tat*—by his; *bhajanāt*—devotion; *gatavān*—vanished; *pramadam*—(my) laziness (I become active); *praṇamāmi*—I bow; *sadā*—always; *prabhupāda*—His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda; *padam*—lotus feet.

**I always bow down to the lotus feet of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, due to whose devotion my laziness has dissipated. (7)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the Siam Vedic Society.  
(written by Hṛṣikeśānanda dāsa)

## Vaiṣṇava Academy for Boys

Dearmost merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

I’m feeling great ecstasy, experiencing the pleasure of glorifying you on this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day. Śrīla Prabhupāda, even if all your devotees glorified you eternally, this would not be enough to thank you for what you have done.

Kṛṣṇa says that one who offends His devotee will not be able to take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I offended you, your devotees, and Kṛṣṇa, but still you let me take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Previously, being envious by nature, I rejected even the idea of authority. I saw everything as opinion. Then one day, almost two years ago, I suddenly found myself in *gurukula,* having all my arguments taken apart one by one by the *śāstras* you gave. How is it possible to understand such mercy?

I was very fallen. You brought me to the human platform and gave me the greatest gift, the highest knowledge. What else could anyone possibly want? Yet your mercy goes way beyond even this. And I didn’t take advantage of it. In fact, I gave the devotees a bad name in more ways than one. Many people lost faith in me, but you stood by me as I slowly advanced. After millions of offenses and impious activities, by your grace I’ve started to learn sense control. Due to your mercy, very soon I will be in an environment, Vṛndāvana *dhāma*, that will make it easier to think of Kṛṣṇa. There I will be trained so that I may offer you some small service and help in the establishment of *varṇāśrama-dharma*. I hope and pray that you will accept whatever menial service I perform. Thank you for everything you’ve done for me.

Your ever-indebted servants at the Vaiṣṇava Academy for Boys, Alachua, Florida.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa dāsa, age 14)

## Vaishnava Academy for Girls

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The letter below is from one of the students at the Vaishnava Academy for Girls here in Alachua. When we read it we were very moved by the author’s simple sincerity. Each student in our class wrote an offering to you, and we will read them to you privately, but this one helped us to understand the depth of devotion and eagerness that is required for a devotee to make advancement toward Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. We wanted to share it with your other disciples so that they could see how our young devotees are also appreciating your greatness.

For a long time now we have been running a *gurukula* for teenage girls. When we read this offering it became obvious that we are all really students in your *gurukula,* and that each of us is simply sent, by your mercy, to teach something to help us to study your lessons and understand your purports. We aspire to become an instrument in your spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

\* \* \*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. Please accept this offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!! My name is Śyāma Sundarī dāsī, daughter of Buddhi-devī dāsī and Gaura Bhagavān dāsa. It is by their grace that I am able to appreciate this *saṅkīrtana* movement and be here writing this letter to you. Although raised in the company of persons who were devotees, I never really knew a whole lot about Kṛṣṇa consciousness or who you were (I still don’t). All I knew was that I was a Hare Kṛṣṇa and I wasn’t supposed to eat meat. It wasn’t until two or three years ago that I got the opportunity to find out about what I was claiming to be a part of.

I had been caught up in some childlike nonsense for awhile and was being forced to get out of it or else. As soon as I realized I was actually suffering, I ran towards Kṛṣṇa and His devotees. I don’t know how I realized that, but I did. Very soon after that, my life was turned right-side-up and I acquired, like you say, a “higher taste.” It felt so good to know I didn’t have to suffer anymore. It was like opening a door to another dimension, and it was real! When I think of how fortunate I am to have the association of your very kind sons and daughters, and of all the great Vaiṣṇavas of the past through their books and literature, I feel so privileged and happy inside. I!, Me!, Śyāma Sundarī dāsī, an eternal servant of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, has the key to the spiritual world! How can I ever repay all of the devotees and you? When will I be so advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness that I can feel enough compassion for others to want to also help them on their way back home? You are so merciful, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you so much. You are giving everyone and anyone Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy. Even me! But why me? I can only say “Thank you” millions and millions of times over again and hope that by your mercy I can repay you by doing devotional service unto you and your disciples eternally. Please accept me as your servant and never let me go. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Sincerely, your servant,

Śyāma Sundarī dāsī, age 16

\* \* \*

We Love Prabhupāda

Prabhupāda is a pure devotee. He was Kṛṣṇa conscious in his childhood and until he left the planet. When he came to America he was 71. Even though he was old, he was determined to carry out the wish of his spiritual master and preach to people all around the world. Even though he suffered 2 heart attacks, that did not stop him. He was so compassionate. When he was a boy he had a small Rathayatra and asked his parents to cook *prasādam*. We can see he was a pure devotee from the start, and when he was very sick, he was still translating *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. He was so surrendered to Kṛṣṇa. Once at the Sunday feast program, the devotees did the play of Nārada Muni’s rescuing the hunter Mṛgāri, and when the devotee playing Nārada recited verses from “The Prayers to the Spiritual Master,” everyone could notice something shining in Prabhupāda’s eye. He had a tear in his eye, but it was particularly shiny, like a diamond. A little later many devotees were commenting, “Did you see the diamond in Prabhupāda’s eye?”

Prabhupāda also loved children and said, “I want hundreds of these children, because in the future we expect to change the face of the world.” He would also tease and play with children, like Saraswati. He used to say he would mail her to school, and she would run to her mother.

Prabhupāda was so humble. Sometimes devotees would say he was God and he would say he was not God, but God’s representative. Someday, I hope I will be able to help Prabhupāda spread this Kṛṣṇa conscious movement all over the world. That is what Prabhupāda wanted.

by Nilādri dāsī, age 10

Your daughters at the Vaishnava Academy for Girls, Alachua, Florida.  
(introduction written by Lakṣmīmonī dāsī)

## The Vaiṣṇava Institute for Higher Education

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Today I come to you in a simple way. You have my heart. Placing my head at your feet, I pray to not be apart from you. Your child muses with the idea of how to be with you forever. My Prabhupāda, you are so kind to accept me, along with my tiny offerings of sentiments, food, and flowers. You allow me to wade in these undisturbed waters of devotion.

Yet this year, I perceive your transcendental presence in a different way. You are commanding a big ship’s course. The water is turbulent and deep. Strong, merciful breezes of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s purpose of pure love sail the ship. This unfathomable greatness is sometimes overwhelming. You command and assure our reaching the final destination. Simultaneously, you receive our offering of loving service, adjusting our way to Goloka.

At the VIHE we serve by your method of hearing and chanting. Devotees worldwide (your glory) come here to Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and taste the sweetness of learning and teaching in the association of Vaiṣṇavas. Aware that this offering is like that of a candle to the midday sun, we pray for your eternal guidance in transcendental loving service.

We remain at the altar of your feet and beg for mercy.

The devotees at the Vaiṣṇava Institute for Higher Education.  
(written by Svarga-devī dāsī)

## Vaishnava Scholarly Press

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are seeing how difficult it is to be your loyal and fully surrendered disciple, to be unwavering despite a myriad of enticements. You have provided us with everything, but we become distracted and careless. Like crows we are attracted by what glitters. We go to substitutes, considering them to be as good as you. We even judge you for having overlooked portions of spiritual life and for conveying what is incomplete. Now it has been proven that only the truly swanlike are fit to eternally associate with a *paramahaṁsa*, who forever abides in pristine places of pure devotion.

We must all reflect on how to come closer to you, how to go deeper, not broader, and how to be situated in perpetual association at your lotus feet. I have been reviewing explicit statements in this regard, as follows: “You can associate with me by reading my books” (8/7/75). “I have spoken in my books; now try to understand” (5/17/77). “If you read my books, your spiritual desires will be fulfilled” (3/18/69). “If you read my books, you will be benefited.” “Not my books; Kṛṣṇa’s books” (6/8/76). So, let us go on reading, discussing, producing, distributing, and studying your holy books. And, in this way, live with you.

In the past, even your Godbrothers seemed to neglect you. They never appeared to consider you to be a *sannyāsī* like them but treated you as if you were a *gṛhastha*. They would not offer you any facility or even cooperate with you, although you were endowed with literary acumen and unwavering determination to serve the mission bestowed upon you by your Guru Mahārāja. Not only they but the world failed to take note of you. You were never venerated in your own time for what you uniquely offered.

You are none other than the flawless representative of Kṛṣṇa. As much as we avoid Kṛṣṇa and our eternal relationship with the Lord, so must we overlook you. When at last we repose our love exclusively in Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, then we will cherish you. Kṛṣṇa consciousness and Prabhupāda consciousness are inseparable. You have taught me that Kṛṣṇa is couched in my innermost heart. However, you are “the external Kṛṣṇa” who beckoned me to become your disciple and surrender to “the internal Kṛṣṇa.” It was you whom I was able to serve each morning when I brought you your shoes, slipped your lotus feet into them, and handed you your cane as you proceeded on your daily walk-and-talk, while I followed at your side. May I always keep you and your divine guidance within the chambers of my heart and scrupulously follow your instructions as a trustworthy and dutiful servant, according to your satisfaction.

Your servants at the Vaishnava Scholarly Press.  
(written by Brahmānanda dāsa & Gargamuni dāsa)

## The Vedic Student Association

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. On the eve of the Centennial celebration in honor of the one hundredth anniversary of your appearance, I beg to be counted among your followers. They are the most fortunate persons in the universe.

My intention this year was to write an offering which included some of the philosophy that you taught us, with full śāstric references etc. I made notes and read in preparation.

Now for the last few days I’ve been running a fever, and my intelligence, which is usually scattered, is in complete disarray, and time is running out. I promise to complete my intended offering to you at a later date and present it to you on your appearance day.

Please keep me in the association of your followers. Slowly I’m learning what it means to be your servant, and this is my life’s goal.

Your devotees in The Vedic Student Association.  
(written by Vāṇīnātha Vasu dāsa)
